# TinapayanRMS — Project Guide & Build Plan

Product recipe management & costing app for Tinapayan Festival Bakeshoppe. Tracks ingredient expenses, recipe/product margins, and cascades ingredient and packaging price changes up through sub-assemblies and finished products automatically.

## At a glance

- **What it is**: a Windows desktop app (WPF on .NET 11, Syncfusion, ReactiveUI) over PostgreSQL 18 through EF Core, for one bakeshop.
- **Layers**: `Domain` (the entities), `ApplicationCore` (the `*Rules` classes, repository interfaces, validators), `Infrastructure` (EF Core and Dapper, the repositories, the cost propagators, `ProductCloner`, the unit of work) and `UI` (the screens and their ViewModels, printing, export, the Vim keys).
- **Costs**: a raw material's cost per base unit is stored, and the cascade keeps a made or linked one's current (Reactive Costing Cascade). A sub-assembly's batch and a product's cost and selling price are worked out on read.
- **Screens**: Raw Materials, Sub-Assemblies and Products (with its Recipe, Costing, Scaling, Clone and History tabs), then Reports, Price Simulator and Print, and the Settings dialog. The Production Plan moved to TinapayanBMS on 2026-10-31 (see "Recipe API for the shop's other apps").
- **Saves**: a screen's Save writes its whole form in one transaction, and a save that records product versions writes them in that same transaction (`VersionedSaves`, `IUnitOfWork`).

### Where the detail is

- **This file**: the tech stack, solution structure, domain model, application architecture, costing cascade, development environment and conventions.
- **[docs/plan/](docs/plan/)**, moved out of this file on 2026-10-28 and still part of it: each main screen's design notes ([Products](docs/plan/products-screen.md), [Raw Materials](docs/plan/raw-materials-screen.md), [Sub-Assemblies](docs/plan/sub-assemblies-screen.md)), the [Syncfusion notes](docs/plan/syncfusion.md), and the [build plan](docs/plan/build-plan.md) with its dated log and open items.
- **[docs/developer-manual.md](docs/developer-manual.md)** and **[docs/user-manual.md](docs/user-manual.md)**: the code as it is, and the app as users see it.

## Tech Stack

- **Language / Runtime**: C# 15, .NET 11 (`net11.0`; `net11.0-windows10.0.19041.0` for the UI and UI.Tests projects — ReactiveUI.WPF requires at least that Windows SDK version, and the bare `net11.0-windows` moniker resolves to `windows7.0`, which is too low). On the 11.0.100-rc.3.26522.148 SDK, a preview, since 2026-10-26 (RC2 from 2026-10-24, on the `dotnet11-rc2` branch, the line the app went forward on from 2026-10-30 while `main` stayed on .NET 10; `main` has been on .NET 11 since the `recipe-api` branch was merged into it on 2026-11-03); C# 14 and .NET 10 before. The one change it brought that mattered: a `double` now converts to a `decimal` at its exact binary value (Conventions, "Decimal precision"). Runtime async is on (Build settings, below)
- **UI**: WPF, styled with **Syncfusion Essential Studio for WPF 35.1.43** and Syncfusion's Windows11Light theme (the official `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` package), accent-recoloured at runtime
- **MVVM**: ReactiveUI (26.x). This version requires explicit builder-pattern initialization before any `ReactiveObject` is touched — `RxAppBuilder.CreateReactiveUIBuilder().WithCoreServices().WithWpf().BuildApp()` — done once per process by `src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ReactiveUIBootstrapper.cs`. Since 24.3, `WithWpf()` binds the main-thread scheduler to the `Application`'s dispatcher, or with no `Application` (the tests) to the calling thread's existing one, and throws if there is none; the bootstrapper therefore touches `Dispatcher.CurrentDispatcher` first when `Application.Current` is null, which is what 24.2 did implicitly. It also dropped `System.Reactive` as a dependency, so that package is referenced directly wherever Rx LINQ operators (`.Select`, `.Where`, …) are used on an `IObservable<T>`.
  - **`ReactiveCommand.Create`/`CreateFromTask`'s parameterless overloads return `ReactiveCommand<RxVoid, RxVoid>`** (`ReactiveUI.Primitives.RxVoid`), not `ReactiveCommand<Unit, Unit>` — this fork doesn't use `System.Reactive.Unit` for that. Import the type via a using-alias (`using RxVoid = ReactiveUI.Primitives.RxVoid;`), never `using ReactiveUI.Primitives;` wholesale — that namespace also carries its own C# 14 extension-member `Select`/`Where`/`Subscribe`, which collide (CS0121, ambiguous call) with `System.Reactive.Linq`'s the moment both are in scope over the same observable.
  - **25.0 moved property observation and binding into `ReactiveUI.Binding`**, a source-generated engine that replaced the reflection-based one. `WhenAnyValue`, `ToProperty`, `ObservableAsPropertyHelper<T>` and `Interaction<TInput, TOutput>` keep their names but now live there. The package adds `ReactiveUI.Binding` as a global using to every consuming project, so it can't be left out. Its `ObservedProperty.Switch` (a `WhenAnyObservable`-style switch) collides (CS0121) with Rx's `Switch` over an `IObservable<IObservable<T>>`, so the ViewModels call `Observable.Switch(source)` rather than `source.Switch()`. The upgrade from 24.3 needed no other code change.
- **Reactive collections**: DynamicData
- **Persistence**: EF Core 11.0.0-rc.3.26522.148 (10.0.13 until 2026-10-24, the RC2 preview until 2026-10-27) → PostgreSQL 18 (`localhost:5432`, role `tinapayanrms`, db name `tinapayanrms`) through Npgsql (`Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL` 11.0.0-rc.3, with `EFCore.NamingConventions` making every table and column snake_case) for writes, the schema and migrations, since 2026-10-21 (SQL Server Express before; see Development Environment); **Dapper** (2.1.x) for reads (see Conventions, "Reads through Dapper, writes through EF Core")
- **DI / Hosting**: `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting` + `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection`, wired via `HostBuilder` in `src/TinapayanRMS.UI/CompositionRoot.cs`, called from `App.xaml.cs`
- **Validation**: FluentValidation
- **Logging**: Serilog (rolling file sink)
- **Charts**: Syncfusion SfChart (`Syncfusion.SfChart.WPF`) — replaced the originally-planned LiveCharts2. **No screen draws a chart since 2026-09-27**: Reports' Price Trend and Margin Overview charts gave way to two summary grids, at the user's request ("not showing much", "remove the graph for now"). The package reference stays for when a chart comes back.
- **Excel export** (2026-10-03): Syncfusion's grid converter, `Syncfusion.DataGridExcelExport.WPF`, which brings XlsIO (and PDF) with it, at `$(SyncfusionVersion)` like every Syncfusion package. The user's key covers `Platform.Excel` as well as `Platform.WPF` (checked with `SyncfusionLicenseProvider.ValidateLicense`), so a workbook carries no trial notice.
- **Testing**: xUnit v3, AwesomeAssertions, Respawn. Integration tests run against the local PostgreSQL server, and Respawn (`DbAdapter.Postgres`) resets each test project's database between tests. `AwesomeAssertions` replaces `FluentAssertions`: FluentAssertions 8+ requires a paid commercial license for use in a for-profit product, and AwesomeAssertions is a same-API, MIT-licensed fork of the last free FluentAssertions 7 release, created for exactly this situation. `NSubstitute` was referenced but never used — the suite exercises real repositories against a real Respawn-reset database rather than mocking — and its package reference was removed during a code-quality pass.
- **MCP server for AI assistants** (2026-10-30, at the user's request, as a prototype): `src/TinapayanRMS.Mcp`, a Model Context Protocol server over stdio on the official C# SDK (`ModelContextProtocol` 2.3.1, from 2.2.0 on 2026-11-03). It gives an AI coding assistant what SQL can't, a product's cost and price, which are computed on read: `list_products`, `get_product_costing` and `simulate_price_changes` (`Tools/CostingTools`). It builds its host with `DatabaseHost` and prices with `TinapayanRMS.Pricing`, so the tools can't price differently from the screens (it referenced the UI project for that until 2026-11-04: see below). **It can't write**: `ReadOnlyConnection` sets `default_transaction_read_only` on its connection to the dev database, so PostgreSQL refuses a write whatever runs it. Claude Code starts it from `.mcp.json` through `scripts/Start-McpServer.ps1` (2026-11-04, with `dotnet run -c Release` before), which builds Release and runs a copy of the build from `bin/Release/runs/<hash>`, so a running server holds only its copy and never blocks a build: run from `bin/Release/net11.0` itself, it made another session's start that had to rebuild fail. One start builds at a time (a named mutex), a copy no server runs from goes at the next start, and the server ends with the script. It builds first, so Claude Code waits 2 minutes for it to answer (`MCP_TIMEOUT` in `.claude/settings.json`, 2026-11-04): with the default 30 seconds it missed the wait at every session start while each commit recompiled every project (Build settings, below). Its JSON rounds decimals to 6 places (`ToolJson`): full precision ran to 28 digits a figure. Tested by `TinapayanRMS.Mcp.Tests`, on its own database and on the read-only connection. See the developer manual's "Checking costs from an AI assistant".
- **Recipe API for the shop's other apps** (2026-10-31, at the user's request, for TinapayanBMS, named TinapayanPreweigh until 2026-11-01 and BatchFlow until 2026-11-03, a new WPF app in `../TinapayanBMS` that weighs ingredients out ahead of mixing and tracks the packs): `src/TinapayanRMS.RecipeApi`, a read-only HTTP API (ASP.NET Core minimal APIs on Kestrel, JSON under `/api/v1`) on port 5290 of every address, so TinapayanBMS can read the recipes on this PC or across the LAN. The user's choices, over the alternatives offered: **an API in this repo** over TinapayanBMS reading this database directly, which would tie TinapayanBMS to this schema and have it work out batches again; and TinapayanBMS in **a repo of its own**. Like the MCP server, it builds its host with `DatabaseHost` (`RecipeApiHost.AddRecipeApi` puts the web server on top), prices with `TinapayanRMS.Pricing`, and reads through `ReadOnlyConnection`. Endpoints: `status`, `ingredients` (with a made ingredient's weight loss and sub-assembly and a linked one's ingredient and share, for planning), `boms`, `categories`, `products`, `products/recipes` (each product's recipe as TinapayanBMS plans from it, with its packaging, Wastage %, price and cost), `mixes` (the sub-assemblies with lines, never a packaging set), `mixes/details` (every mix with its lines), `mixes/{id}` (its lines, grams per batch) and `materials` (every raw material with its unit, cost and purchase unit, which TinapayanBMS's purchase requisition prices). Its first `plans` (the saved Production Plans) and `POST plan` (`ProductionPlanRules.Plan` over the products as saved) went with the Production Plan the same day, when planning moved to TinapayanBMS. The records it sends are `Contracts/RecipeContracts.cs`, which the other apps copy, so a change adds rather than renames or removes. A key (`RecipeApi:Key` in `recipeapi.json`, sent as `X-Api-Key`), optional until 2026-11-03, when a code review found the API giving every cost and price to anyone who could reach the port, and the user chose to require one: with none set only this PC is answered (a 403 for any other address), and the installer makes one on a first install and keeps it after that, since publishing can put the repo's blank file over the installed one. What fails comes back as `{ error }`, a 503 in `Failure`'s fixed sentence for a database that can't be used and a 500 with a fixed sentence for anything else, and `status` reads the database, so its answer means that's there too. Installed by `scripts/Install-RecipeApi.ps1`, which starts it hidden at sign-in as the signed-in user (a Windows service couldn't read that user's `pgpass.conf`) and, run as an administrator, opens port 5290 to the local subnet on private networks. Tested by `TinapayanRMS.RecipeApi.Tests` (`tinapayanrms_test_recipeapi`), in memory through ASP.NET Core's `TestServer`. See the developer manual's "The Recipe API".
- **The pricing has a project of its own, so the servers have no UI in them** (2026-11-04, at the user's request, from a code review). The MCP server and the Recipe API referenced the UI project for `SavedProductReader`, accepted for a prototype, so the API that TinapayanBMS depends on installed at 77 MB with 20 Syncfusion files and needed the desktop runtime. `src/TinapayanRMS.Pricing` (`net11.0`, on ApplicationCore) now holds `SavedProductReader` and what it builds: `ProductUsageEdit`, `SectionLine`, `PackagingLineRow`, `ProductCostSummary`, `PackagingLinkTarget`, `PurchaseUnit`, `BaseUnitText`, `SimulatedPriceChange` and `PriceSimulatorProductRow`. **They were moved, not rewritten**, since a rewrite could change a price: the rows kept their members, and only gave up `ReactiveObject`, which they used for nothing but saying when they change, for `ObservableRow`. **The editable rows went back to the UI on 2026-11-05** (an independent code review): a saved product is read as immutable `PricedSection`s and `PricedPackagingLine`s, a row and a saved product get their figures from the same `SectionFigures.Of` and `PricedPackagingLine.Price`, the MCP server tries prices with `TriedPriceChange` rather than a Price Changes row, and `ProductUsageEdit`, `PackagingLineRow`, `SimulatedPriceChange` and `ObservableRow` are in `UI/ViewModels`. `WhenAnyValue`, `AutoRefresh` and bindings follow it the same way. Printing and comparing stayed with the UI (`ToPrintout` and `ShowsSameAs`, now extension methods), and the stage and folder titles moved to `ProductRules`. `Failure` and `ReadOnlyConnection` moved to Infrastructure, which also has `DatabaseHost`, the host the app and both servers start from. The servers are on plain `net11.0`, which can't reference a Windows project, so this can't creep back. A new project over a folder in ApplicationCore, since these rows carry display text that doesn't belong beside the rules. The API now builds at 10 MB and the MCP server at 14 MB. Checked by running the builds from before and after side by side on the dev database: all 231 MCP answers (69 products at both cost bases, 86 simulated price changes, and refusals) and all 128 Recipe API answers (every endpoint and every mix) were byte-identical. `Install-RecipeApi.ps1` now mirrors what it publishes into the install folder, since publishing into it would have left the 67 MB behind.
- **Solution format**: `.slnx`
- **Build settings** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "centralize my build settings"): `Directory.Build.props` at the repo root sets `TargetFramework` (`net11.0`), `ImplicitUsings` and `Nullable` for every project, plus a `WindowsTargetFramework` property that the UI and UI.Tests projects use as their `TargetFramework`. **Runtime async** is on for every project too (`<Features>$(Features);runtime-async=on</Features>`, 2026-10-30, the user's choice to try it): .NET 11 runs `async` methods itself, so the compiler no longer turns them into state machines (Infrastructure went from 157 such classes to the 1 its iterator needs). Still an opt-in compiler feature in RC3. With it on, all 1,975 tests passed and the app ran clean (startup, the day's automatic backup through `pg_dump`, Products, Print, History). The suite's time didn't change measurably, since it waits on PostgreSQL. To turn it off, delete that one line. **The commit stays out of everyday builds** (2026-11-04, from the code review): the SDK put each commit's hash into every assembly's version and its Source Link file, so the first build after any commit, even one that changed only docs, recompiled every project, and the MCP server, which builds as it starts, missed Claude Code's wait at every session start. `EnableSourceControlManagerQueries` and `EnableSourceLink` are off unless `_IsPublishing`, which `dotnet publish` sets, so a published copy still carries its commit (`1.0.0+<hash>`, its Product version). Nothing in the app reads it. `tests/Directory.Build.props` imports it and adds what every test project shares: `IsPackable=false`, the `Xunit` global using, and the xUnit v3, AwesomeAssertions, test SDK and coverage packages. It applies them only to projects whose name ends in `.Tests`, so `TinapayanRMS.Tests.Common` stays a plain library. Referencing `xunit.v3` would make it a test executable, so it takes `xunit.v3.extensibility.core` instead. `Directory.Packages.props` enables Central Package Management: every package version is a `PackageVersion` there (EF Core, Microsoft.Extensions and Syncfusion through the `EFCoreVersion`/`MicrosoftExtensionsVersion`/`SyncfusionVersion` properties), and a project's `PackageReference` has no `Version`. `CentralPackageTransitivePinningEnabled` is on, so a `PackageVersion` also overrides a transitive dependency: the "Transitive pins" group lifts `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Relational` to `EFCoreVersion`, because `EFCore.NamingConventions` asks for EF Core 10 and every project should run the one EF Core, the one Npgsql's provider takes. From 2026-10-15 until the move to PostgreSQL (2026-10-21) it also lifted `System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs` past the `9.0.11` that `Microsoft.Data.SqlClient` brought, which had known vulnerabilities. Drop a pin once the package that brings it no longer needs it. A `.csproj` only holds what is unique to it. **Code analysis**: `AnalysisLevel` is `latest-recommended`, the SDK's built-in .NET analyzers (no NuGet package) at their Recommended rule set; the Default set reported nothing and All reported about 1,700 warnings, mostly style (`ConfigureAwait`, argument null checks, `internal` types). The root `.editorconfig` marks `Migrations/` as generated code (they can't be hand-edited) and turns off CA1707 in `tests/`, whose method names use underscores, and CA1716 everywhere (it flags `IPriceHistoryEntry.Date` for being a Visual Basic keyword; the app is C# only and the name is a database column). Warnings are not errors, but the build had none once they were resolved (2026-09-17): name equality checks are `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, sorts for display stay `StringComparer.CurrentCultureIgnoreCase`, display dates format with `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture` and the Serilog file sink with `InvariantCulture`, and the six ViewModels that own a DynamicData `SourceCache` are sealed and `IDisposable`, so the host disposes the caches with its singletons at exit.
- **`dotnet test`**: since the .NET 10 SDK, `dotnet test` has no VSTest support; running under the new Microsoft.Testing.Platform mode needs a `test.runner` setting in `global.json` at the repo root (already added). Run tests with `dotnet test --solution TinapayanRMS.slnx`.
- **Syncfusion license key**: compiled into the app at build time from the `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` Windows user environment variable (2026-10-23, so an installed copy runs without the variable): the UI project adds it as an `AssemblyMetadata` item named `SyncfusionLicenseKey` when the variable is set, and its `RequireSyncfusionLicenseKey` target fails `dotnet publish` when it isn't. `App`'s static constructor (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/App.xaml.cs`) reads that attribute, falls back to the variable, throws with neither, and registers the key before anything else in the process touches a Syncfusion type. The key is readable from the published assembly, as it would be with Syncfusion's own advice of a string literal in code; the build-time step only keeps it out of git. Building the app, or running its licensed UI tests, on another machine still needs the variable there.

## Solution Structure

```
TinapayanRMS.slnx
├── Directory.Build.props             — shared compiler settings and target frameworks
├── Directory.Packages.props          — Central Package Management: every package version
├── .mcp.json                         — how an AI assistant starts the MCP servers (src/TinapayanRMS.Mcp, and Syncfusion's WPF docs server)
├── scripts/                          — Install-RecipeApi.ps1 and Uninstall-RecipeApi.ps1 (2026-10-31), Start-McpServer.ps1, which .mcp.json runs (2026-11-04), and Test-Changed.ps1 and Watch-Tests.ps1, for fewer tests while developing (2026-11-05)
├── README.md                         — what the app is, and where the manual and these guides are
├── docs/
│   ├── user-manual.md                 — the user manual, for the shop's staff: every screen, and how costs are worked out
│   └── developer-manual.md            — the developer manual: prerequisites, setup, architecture, patterns, testing, and steps for common changes
├── src/
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.Domain            — entities and enums (no external deps)
│   │   ├── Entities/                  — the persisted domain types
│   │   ├── Enums/
│   │   └── Interfaces/                — contracts shared by entities (none since 2026-09-18, when IPriceHistoryEntry went with PriceHistoryRules)
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.ApplicationCore   — business and costing rules, repository contracts, validation
│   │   ├── Interfaces/                — repository/service contracts that Infrastructure implements
│   │   ├── Rules/                     — static business rules and calculations (*Rules, UnitConversionRules, IngredientCostGraph)
│   │   └── Validators/                — FluentValidation validators
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure    — EF Core; TinapayanRMSDbContext and its design-time factory, DatabaseHost and ReadOnlyConnection at the root
│   │   ├── Configurations/            — one IEntityTypeConfiguration<T> per entity
│   │   ├── Migrations/                — generated by `dotnet ef migrations add` and never moved; since 2026-09-15 never hand-written or hand-edited either (the user's instruction; earlier ones carry hand-added data-conversion SQL)
│   │   ├── CompiledModels/            — the compiled EF Core model, generated by `dotnet ef dbcontext optimize` after every model change and never edited by hand (see "Startup time" under Application Architecture)
│   │   ├── Queries/                   — Dapper reads: DapperReads (connection/transaction helpers), Columns (every entity's column list), RawMaterialReads and SubAssemblyReads (shared graph loaders)
│   │   ├── Repositories/              — one implementation per ApplicationCore repository interface
│   │   └── Services/                  — cross-entity workflows (the cost propagators, ProductCloner), and JsonViewStateStore (how the screens were left, in a file per Windows user)
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.Pricing           — what a saved product costs (2026-11-04): SavedProductReader and what it builds, for the screens and both servers; no WPF, Syncfusion or ReactiveUI, and no folders
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.UI                — WPF + Syncfusion + ReactiveUI; app startup (App, CompositionRoot, …) at the root
│   │   ├── Views/                     — MainWindow, SettingsWindow and one UserControl per screen or Settings tab (.xaml + code-behind)
│   │   │   └── RawMaterials/          — Raw Materials' cards, one UserControl each, inheriting the screen's DataContext
│   │   ├── ViewModels/                — ViewModelBase and one ViewModel per View
│   │   ├── Controls/                  — reusable UserControls (ErrorToast, SuccessToast, UnitConversionOverview), grid columns (NumericColumn) and attached behaviors (GridFillerRows, GridEditCommit, GridAddNewRow, GridRowReordering, GridRowInserting, AutoCompleteDropDown, NumericEditing, MouseWheelForwarding, FocusScrolling)
│   │   ├── Converters/                — IValueConverters
│   │   ├── Assets/Icons/              — icon image files (.png, .ico) compiled in as WPF Resources by a glob in the .csproj; no code, so not a namespace. Empty (a .gitkeep) until icons are downloaded (2026-10-08)
│   │   └── Printing/                  — a product's printout, or a set's, on pages: PrintPagination (pure), ProductPrintLayout (the pages, built in code), ProductPrintManager (Syncfusion's PrintManager) and PrintPaneFit (the pane fitted to the app's text)
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.Mcp               — the MCP server for AI assistants (2026-10-30), net11.0, on Infrastructure and Pricing; Program at the root
│   │   ├── Tools/                     — CostingTools (the [McpServerTool] methods) and PriceChangeInput
│   │   ├── Results/                   — the records the tools return
│   │   └── Serialization/             — ToolJson: the SDK's JSON options, decimals rounded to 6 places
│   └── TinapayanRMS.RecipeApi         — the read-only Recipe API for the shop's other apps (2026-10-31), net11.0, on Infrastructure and Pricing; Program, RecipeApiHost and recipeapi.json at the root
│       ├── Contracts/                 — RecipeContracts: the records it sends and takes, /api/v1
│       └── Endpoints/                 — CatalogEndpoints, MixEndpoints, ProductRecipeEndpoints
└── tests/                             — one test project per src layer (split 2026-09-28, at the user's request: the single project had grown unwieldy)
    ├── TinapayanRMS.ApplicationCore.Tests — net11.0, no database
    │   ├── Rules/                     — unit tests for ApplicationCore/Rules
    │   └── Validators/                — unit tests for ApplicationCore/Validators
    ├── TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure.Tests  — net11.0; the only project Infrastructure's InternalsVisibleTo names
    │   ├── Persistence/               — integration tests against its Respawn-reset test database
    │   ├── Queries/                   — ColumnsTests: the Dapper column lists against EF's model (no database)
    │   ├── Services/                  — JsonViewStateStoreTests, FailureTests
    │   └── Fixtures/                  — DatabaseFixture (its database's name), DatabaseCollection
    ├── TinapayanRMS.Pricing.Tests     — net11.0; the pricing, some of it on its own test database
    │   └── Fixtures/                  — DatabaseFixture, DatabaseCollection
    ├── TinapayanRMS.UI.Tests          — WPF; CompositionRootTests at the root
    │   ├── ViewModels/                — ViewModels on real repositories and its own test database
    │   ├── Controls/                  — grid behaviors in a real, off-screen SfDataGrid on an STA thread
    │   ├── Printing/                  — pagination (no WPF), and the pages and Syncfusion's PrintManager on an STA thread
    │   ├── Converters/
    │   └── Fixtures/                  — DatabaseFixture, DatabaseCollection, InMemoryViewStateStore, StaThread, OffscreenGrid, OffscreenGridCollection
    ├── TinapayanRMS.Mcp.Tests         — net11.0, like the project it tests; its own test database
    │   ├── Tools/                     — CostingToolsTests, on the server's read-only connection
    │   └── Fixtures/                  — DatabaseFixture, DatabaseCollection
    ├── TinapayanRMS.RecipeApi.Tests   — net11.0, like the project it tests; its own test database
    │   ├── Endpoints/                 — RecipeApiTests, served in memory (TestServer) on the API's read-only connection
    │   └── Fixtures/                  — DatabaseFixture, DatabaseCollection
    └── TinapayanRMS.Tests.Common      — a plain library, not a test project: DatabaseFixtureBase and PersistenceTestBase, and the appsettings.json link
```

**Folder = namespace, grouped by kind.** Every folder inside a project is a sub-namespace of it (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ViewModels/` → `TinapayanRMS.UI.ViewModels`). A new file goes in the folder for its kind, never at the project root. Only app-startup plumbing (`App`, `CompositionRoot`, `ReactiveUIBootstrapper`, `ConfirmDeleteHandlerRegistration`, Infrastructure's `DatabaseHost` and `ReadOnlyConnection`, the MCP server's `Program`, and the Recipe API's `Program` and `RecipeApiHost`), the DbContext pair, and `TinapayanRMS.Pricing`, one kind of thing throughout, live at a project root. Code in a sub-namespace sees its parent namespaces without a `using`: a View calls `RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler` from `TinapayanRMS.UI`, and a repository uses `TinapayanRMSDbContext` from `TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure`. Sibling folders need a `using`. The solution was reorganized into this layout after the Phase 16 UI work: projects moved into `src/`/`tests/`, and each project's files went from flat to grouped by kind. Moving the entities to `TinapayanRMS.Domain.Entities` left the EF model unchanged (`dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` reported no changes), because table names come from the DbContext, not from entity namespaces. `Migrations/` was therefore left untouched.

The Windows11Light theme is not a project in the solution: it's Syncfusion's official `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` NuGet package (see Syncfusion UI Framework below).

## Domain Model

- **UnitOfMeasure**: `Id`, `Name`, `Abbreviation` — nothing else. **No unit is seeded or protected by a fixed id** — the table starts empty on a fresh database, and the shop owner adds every unit they need (gram, piece, mm, cm, in, m, or any custom unit like a sack or roll) in Settings → Units of Measure (see Application Architecture below), the same as any other user-managed lookup. **A unit carries no conversion factor of its own.** Conversions live on each raw material instead (see Multi-unit materials below): every material has a **base unit** its quantities and costs are tracked in (always the gram for an ingredient; chosen per packaging material), plus any number of **alternative units**, each defined as a multiple of that base unit. **Standard units have a fixed multiplier, though** (2026-09-26, at the user's request: "Set the standard unit such as kilogram to be predefined as 1000 grams"): `ApplicationCore/Rules/StandardUnitRules` recognises a unit by its name or abbreviation, whatever the case (units are the owner's own rows with no seeded ids, which the user rejected on 2026-09-12), across mass (mg, g, kg/kilo, oz, lb), length (mm, cm, m, in, ft) and volume (ml, l). When the unit and the material's base unit are the same kind — an ingredient's base unit is always the gram — the multiplier is fixed: a kilogram of an ingredient is 1,000 g, an inch of ribbon in centimetres is 2.54. Anything else is still typed: a bag, a litre of an ingredient (only its density says how many grams), and a kilogram of wrappers counted in pieces (the dev data's cello sheet wrappers are 200 and 240 pieces a kilogram). The fixed multiplier is rounded to the 4 places the column keeps (an ounce is 28.3495 g), so a saved one reads back equal, and one that rounds to 0 (a milligram of a kilogram) is left to type. `RawMaterialUnitRepository.AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync` save a standard unit at its fixed multiplier whatever was sent (`MultiplierToSaveAsync`); on the screen a row takes it as the unit is picked, renamed into one in Settings, or the base unit or type changes under it (`RawMaterialUnitRow.ApplyStandardMultiplier`), and neither the Alternative Units grid's Multiplier cell (`CanTypeMultiplier`) nor Purchase Cost Setup's Qty per Purchase Unit (`CanTypeQuantity`, via `IBrandPriceRowHost.IsMultiplierFixed`) can be typed into for one (`controls:GridCellEditing.AllowWhen`). Length units (`IsLengthUnit`/`MillimetersPerUnit`/`LengthConversion`) were removed on 2026-09-13 to make way for this.
- **RawMaterial: ingredients and packaging share one table, told apart by `Type` (`Ingredient` or `Packaging`).** Merged on 2026-09-13 at the user's request ("the difference between the two is too thin"). Until then they were two tables, `Ingredient` and `PackagingMaterial`, each with its own category, unit and price history tables, and the schema itself kept packaging out of recipes. The merge kept the three differences that are real, as rules on the type:
  - **Base unit**: an ingredient's is always the gram, so its `BaseUnitId` is null — recipes, BOMs and sub-assembly weights all add up grams. A packaging material picks its own (`BaseUnitId` required).
  - **Source**: only an ingredient can be `Made` (made from a sub-assembly, with add-ins and a weight loss; until 2026-10-27 a BOM of its own) or `Linked` (a share of another ingredient's cost); packaging is always `Purchased`.
  - **Where it can be used**: sub-assembly lines and a made ingredient's add-ins (BOM lines until 2026-10-27) take ingredients only; a product's packaging lines take packaging only.
  - `RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchType`/`EnsureFieldsMatchSource` run in `RawMaterialRepository` before every save, backed by two check constraints (`CK_RawMaterials_BaseUnitMatchesType`, `CK_RawMaterials_OnlyIngredientsAreMade`). Type and source are fixed once created (`EnsureTypeUnchanged`/`EnsureSourceUnchanged`). Each line repository checks every line's type (`EnsureUsableInRecipes`/`EnsureUsableAsPackaging`) before it replaces anything, with a message naming the material, and the pickers only offer the right type. A name is unique across both types.
  - The line entities kept their role names: `SubAssemblyLine.Ingredient`, `IngredientBomLine.Ingredient`/`ComponentIngredient` and `PackagingLine.PackagingMaterial` are all `RawMaterial`s. A packaging line's unit is `UnitId` (a `RawMaterialUnit`); recipe lines have had no unit since 2026-09-14 (they're in grams — see Ingredients). `IngredientCostPropagator` kept its name too, since it only ever deals with ingredients (as did `SubAssemblyCostPropagator`, until it went on 2026-11-05). (`IngredientQuantityConverter`, which turned recipe lines entered in a unit into grams, went with those units on 2026-09-14.)
  - **`CostPerBaseUnit`** (stored, derived): the true cost of one base unit — one gram, for an ingredient (it was `Ingredient.CostPerGram`).
  - **Buffered cost** (2026-09-14, at the user's request): a per-material **`CostBufferPercent`** (0–100; a new one starts at Settings' default, 10% since 2026-10-07, see Costing defaults; `RawMaterialRules.EnsureValidCostBuffer`, the validator, and `CK_RawMaterials_CostBufferPercent`) is a cushion for price swings, waste and yield variation. **`BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`** (stored, derived) is the cost with it on top (`RawMaterialRules.BufferedCost`, the only buffer formula). **It's the value every sub-assembly line, BOM rollup and packaging line uses**, so products are costed and priced with it; `CostPerBaseUnit` stays the true cost, shown beside it on the Raw Materials screen and in Price History.
    - Purchased: **a fixed amount** (since 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "the cost buffer is a fixed amount without relying on the maximum cost of raw materials in the list. But it can be reinitialize"). Brand prices don't move it. `ApplyBrandPrices` only works it out, as `CostPerBaseUnit × (1 + buffer / 100)`, the first time the material is priced (while it's still 0), and sets it back to 0 when the last price goes. Otherwise it changes only when the screen sends a new one: typed, or from typing the Cost Buffer % (the dearest brand as typed with the % on top; until 2026-09-21 a **Recalculate** button did this, see Raw Materials). `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync` writes the form's amount (0 sent means "work it out from the cost and the %", and `SaveAsync` does that from the new prices when it has them) and cascades an ingredient whose buffered cost changed. `EnsureValidBufferedCost` (and the validator) rejects a negative amount; one below the cost is allowed, and the screen warns about it. So since 2026-09-19 a price change alone reaches product costs only through the true cost, and the buffered cost everything is costed with waits for the screen to send a new one. `CostBufferPercent` is kept as the % that puts the buffer back on top. No migration: existing materials kept the buffered cost they had.
    - Made: the BOM rollup of the components' *buffered* costs, and nothing more. **Since 2026-09-21 a made ingredient adds no buffer of its own** (at the user's request: "there should never be a buffered cost % in the cost per gram because the ingredients use is already has a buffered cost"), so a buffer is counted once however deep the tree, replacing the stacking the bullet used to describe. `RawMaterialRules.StoredCostBufferPercent` is the one place that says so: `RawMaterialRepository`'s Add and Update store a made ingredient's `CostBufferPercent` as 0 whatever the form sends (rejecting it instead would leave the three rows that already had one unsaveable, since the screen has no buffer box for a made ingredient), and `IngredientCostPropagator` sets it to 0 on every recompute, which is what cleans a *dependent* made row the propagator reaches without a save. The dev database's stale percents (Butter Icing 84.4147%, Ube Filling 16.9973%) were cleared by re-saving each row, taking Butter Icing's buffered cost from ₱0.4374 to ₱0.2372/g. A linked part of a made ingredient now inherits 0%, which is right: it takes a share of an already-buffered cost.
    - Linked: the same share of the linked-to ingredient's buffered cost as its true cost is of the true cost (`LinkedCost(linkedTo.BufferedCostPerBaseUnit, …)`, since 2026-09-19, so a part follows the linked-to ingredient's fixed amount). Until then it was the part's true cost with the inherited percent on top.
    - It's stored, not computed on read, because a made ingredient's buffered cost isn't a multiple of its own true cost once its components have buffers.
- **Multi-unit materials** (built 2026-09-13, from the user's Ingredients mockup):
  - **`RawMaterialUnit`** (`RawMaterialId` Cascade, `UnitOfMeasureId` Restrict, `BaseUnitsPerUnit` > 0, `Description`; unique per material + unit; never a packaging material's own base unit). It merged `IngredientUnit` (`GramsPerUnit`) and `PackagingMaterialUnit`. The base unit never has a row: **an empty unit means the base unit** everywhere a unit is optional (price entries, packaging lines), so nothing depends on a "gram" row existing in Units of Measure. Recipe lines take no unit at all (2026-09-14): an ingredient's alternative units price it and work in the Usage Example, but a recipe is always written in grams.
  - `UnitConversionRules.ToBaseQuantity(quantity, multiplier?)` / `CostPerBaseUnit(price, multiplier?)` / `EnsureValidMultiplier` are the only conversion formulas.
  - **Any number of alternative units per item** (2026-10-01, at the user's request). From just after the feature shipped until then a material could have at most two (`UnitConversionRules.MaxAlternativeUnits`, checked by the repository, the screen's Save and a brand row's unit picker, with the Alternative Units grid's add row hidden at two through `CanAddUnit`); all of it went. Nothing in the schema ever limited them.
  - **Every price records the unit it was priced in** (`UnitId?`), both a brand price and a price history entry, so switching from buying bags to buying packs leaves the old per-bag prices correct. The stored cost is per base unit only: `CostPerBaseUnit` = the dearest brand price ÷ its unit's multiplier. `CurrentCost`, `PurchaseUnitId` and `PurchaseUnitQty` were dropped from both material tables before the merge: a price is meaningless without its unit, and storing the unit on the material would make a material ↔ unit FK cycle.
  - **Correcting a multiplier reprices.** `RawMaterialUnitRepository.UpdateAsync` (and the screen's Save, through the same shared write method) reprices the material from its brand prices when one is in that unit (`RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.RepriceAsync`, which also logs a new cost in price history); for an ingredient whose cost moved that way it then calls `IngredientCostPropagator.RecomputeAndPropagateAsync`, which reaches every dependent made ingredient and sub-assembly. Recipe lines are in grams, so nothing else moves (until 2026-09-14 it also rewrote the `Grams` of every recipe line entered in the unit). A packaging material needs nothing more: packaging lines are costed on read. A unit a brand price or a price history entry is in can't be deleted.
  - **A unit in use can't be deleted**: the repository counts price entries, and for a packaging material's unit its packaging lines and its packaging sets' lines, first and says which (Restrict FKs back that up). An ingredient's unit is never on a line.
  - **A packaging material's base unit is fixed once it has alternative units, prices or packaging lines** (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureBaseUnitCanChange`, checked in `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync`), since all of them are measured in it.
- **RawMaterialCategory** (Flour, Dairy, Boxes, Ribbons, …): one user-editable list, merged from `IngredientCategory` and `PackagingCategory` (a packaging category named like an ingredient one joined it), **each category typed Ingredient or Packaging** (`Type`, a `RawMaterialType` stored by name; 2026-09-18, at the user's request). A material goes only in a category of its own type (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureCategoryMatchesType`, in `RawMaterialRepository`'s add and update, after the type and source checks), and a category's type can't change while any material is in it (`EnsureCategoryTypeCanChange`, in `RawMaterialCategoryRepository.UpdateAsync`). Migration `RawMaterialCategoryType` added the column with a default of Ingredient; the dev database's packaging categories (Container, Label, Wrapper, and the empty Adhesive Tape) were set to Packaging by hand afterwards, since no category held both types. Managed in Settings → Material Categories, which has a Type column and a Type picker on its form — see Application Architecture below.
  - **A third type, Made, for made ingredients only** (2026-10-27, at the user's request: "let the category type be limited to Mixed type raw materials", the made ingredients' In-House category, before sub-assemblies can be moved into it; the user's choices, over the alternatives offered: the name **Made**, **strict both ways**, and its folders **inside Ingredients**). `Type` is a `RawMaterialCategoryType` now (`Ingredient`, `Made`, `Packaging`), still stored by name, so no migration (only the compiled model changed). `RawMaterialRules.CategoryTypeFor(type, source)` says which a material goes in (a made ingredient a Made one, any other ingredient an Ingredient one) and `MaterialTypeOf` which material type a category holds; `EnsureCategoryMatchesType` takes the source too ("Flour is an ingredient category. Put this made ingredient in a Made category."; "In-House is a Made category, for made ingredients only. Put this ingredient in a category for ingredients."), in `RawMaterialRepository`'s add, update and `MoveAsync`. **A category's type can change while everything in it fits the new one** (it was while it was empty): `EnsureCategoryTypeCanChange(name, newType, materialsThatDontFit)`, so an ingredient category of made ingredients alone becomes Made in Settings.
    - **Existing data**: no migration retypes or moves anything (a data step would mean hand-editing one). The user can retype a category of made ingredients in Settings and drag the rest into it. **Done for the dev database the same day, at the user's request** ("make those two changes"), by SQL in one transaction after a manual backup into the app's Backups folder (`tinapayanrms_20261027_073245_manual.backup`): In-House retyped Made, and Custard Filling moved into it from Filling/Topping. All seven made ingredients are in In-House now. A made ingredient left in an ingredient category still loads, but its Save is refused with the message above until it's moved; the user's In-House (six made ingredients) can be retyped Made as it is, and Custard Filling dragged into it out of Filling/Topping.
    - **Raw Materials screen**: the Category picker offers the categories of `CategoryTypeFor(EditingType, EditingSource)`, and a new material's type or source changed under one of another type empties it (only while `CanChangeTypeAndSource`: loading a saved one sets its category before its source). A Made category's tree folder (`RawMaterialFolderNode.CategoryType`; its `Type` is Ingredient, so it sits in Ingredients and its `Key` is unchanged) offers **New Made Ingredient Here**, which starts a made ingredient filed there; the Ingredients folder, the empty space and the toolbar's New Category drop-down offer **New Made Category…**. Move To, drags (`RawMaterialTree.MoveTo`/`MovesTo`) and a category typed into the picker all follow `CategoryTypeFor`, so made ingredients and the others never move together. Covered by `RawMaterialRulesTests`, `CategoryPersistenceTests`, `RawMaterialMovePersistenceTests` and `RawMaterialsViewModelTreeTests.A_Made_categorys_folder_starts_made_ingredients_and_takes_only_them`.

- **ProductCategory** (Breads, Cakes, Pastries, …; 2026-09-17, at the user's request: "Products doesn't have categories"): a user-editable list **of its own**, not shared with `RawMaterialCategory` (the user's choice, so a Category picker never mixes Flour and Boxes with Cakes). `Product.CategoryId` is **optional** (also the user's choice): existing products needed no backfill, and a product without one is listed as Uncategorized. Its FK is **Set Null**, not Restrict like a raw material's, so deleting a category just leaves its products uncategorized (the delete confirmation says so). Managed in Settings → Product Categories, or added from the Products header's Category picker. `ProductRepository` reads it with the product (`Include`) and writes it in add, update and `SaveAsync`; `ProductCloner` gives a clone its source's category.
- **Product stage** (`Product.Stage`, a `ProductStage` stored by name; 2026-09-26, at the user's request: "organize products not only by categories but whether the product is under research and development or production"): `ResearchAndDevelopment` or `Production`, the Products list's two top-level folders. A clone and a variant start in Research and Development, the CLR default (`ProductCloner`); a product started by New goes in the stage Settings gives a new product, Production unless it says otherwise (`CostingDefaults.NewProductStage`, 2026-10-07, below). The products there before stages were migrated into Production (migration `ProductStage`): the column's database default is Production, and `HasSentinel((ProductStage)(-1))` makes EF always write the product's own stage, the `QuantityRequired` precedent. Only moving a product in the list changes it (dragging it to another folder, or its right-click menu's Promote, Send Back and Move to; `IProductRepository.MoveAsync`, which sets the stage and the category together, for any number of products in one save, and stamps `UpdatedAt`); the form's Save never writes a saved product's stage (`UpdateAsync`, `SaveAsync`). **A new product's is Save's to write** (2026-09-26, the user's choice): `SaveAsync` copies `Stage` for a product it adds, so New Product Here on a Production folder files the product there, and the header says so until it's saved (see the Products screen's list); a clone and a variant still start in Research and Development.
- **Deleted products** (`Product.DeletedAt`, a nullable `datetime2`; 2026-10-06, at the user's request: "there is no way to undo or restore deleted products"). Delete used to be a hard delete, and its confirmation said "This can't be undone". It's a **soft delete** now, and a product is deleted in two steps. The user's choices, over the alternatives offered: a **Deleted folder** in the Products list, over an Undo on the toast (or both); and **a deleted product keeps holding what it uses**, over letting a sub-assembly or product it uses go and restoring it with a hole.
  - `IProductRepository.DeleteAsync` stamps `DeletedAt` and changes nothing else: stage, category, recipe, packaging, links, costing and `UpdatedAt` all stay. `RestoreAsync` clears it. A category deleted in the meantime has already left the product uncategorized (`SetNull`). `DeletePermanentlyAsync` is the old hard delete with its cascades. Both deletes are refused while another product, deleted or not, links its packaging or holds it in a composite (`EnsureNothingLeansOnAsync`), so a restore never needs a check.
  - **Reads**: `GetAllAsync` returns only the products in use, and `GetDeletedAsync` the deleted ones. `GetByIdAsync` and `GetByIdsAsync` return either. Every screen but Products reads `GetAllAsync`, so Reports, Print, the Price Simulator, the Production Plan and every picker leave deleted products out. There's no EF global query filter, because it would also hide them from the "in use" checks.
  - **Named as deleted where it's in the way**: `ProductRules.ListedName` gives "Cheese Roll (deleted)" in the Sub-Assemblies Used By and its refusals (`SubAssemblyRepository`'s `LinkRow`), Raw Materials' Used By (`GetUsedByAsync`), the delete refusals, and a composite's role refusals. A Used By tile for one opens its row in the Deleted folder.
  - **Its name stays taken** (the unique index covers it): Add, Update, Rename and a clone refuse it first with "A deleted product is named Mamon. Restore it from the Deleted folder, or delete it permanently there first." (`ProductRules.EnsureNameNotHeldByDeleted`). A deleted product can't be newly linked for packaging or put in a composite; each refuses by name.
  - Migration `ProductDeletedAt` (additive, no data step). Covered by `DeletedProductPersistenceTests`, and on the screen by `ProductTreeTests` and `ProductsViewModelTreeTests`.
- **Ingredients** (`Type = Ingredient`):
  - **The base unit for every ingredient is the gram.** Every weight total is in grams: sub-assembly lines' and BOM lines' `Grams`, sub-assembly total weights, and weight-per-piece. **Recipe lines are entered in grams only** (2026-09-14, at the user's request): sub-assembly lines and BOM lines have no unit picker. Until then a line could be entered in an ingredient's alternative unit ("3 pc of egg") and stored in both; migration `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` dropped `Quantity`/`UnitId` and kept the grams.
  - **`Source`**: `Purchased`, `Made` or `Linked`. It is set when the ingredient is created and can't change afterwards, so an ingredient's cost always comes from exactly one place.
  - **`Units`**: its alternative units (a 25 kg bag is `BaseUnitsPerUnit = 25000`, an egg piece `50`). Either source can have them; only a purchased one is priced in them.
  - **Purchased** ingredients are priced through their brand prices (`RawMaterialBrandPrice`), each in one of the ingredient's units (or per gram). `CostPerBaseUnit` = the dearest brand's price ÷ grams in its unit.
  - **Made** ingredients are produced in-house **from a sub-assembly, with add-ins of their own** (2026-10-27, at the user's request: "Sub-Assemblies would be the source of truth for the In-House ingredients … Made type ingredients only link with the sub-assemblies … the Made type ingredient shall have an Add-Ins"). Until then each had a bill of materials (BOM) of its own, edited on the Raw Materials screen, so the same recipe was often written twice (Butter Icing's BOM and a Butter Icing topping). The user's choices, over the alternatives offered: **exactly one sub-assembly** per made ingredient; **convert the existing BOMs** (below); **no new role** for them; and **rework both Move to In-House commands to link** (see Move to In-House under SubAssembly).
    - **`RawMaterial.SubAssemblyId`** (FK Restrict, indexed): the recipe it's made from. Required for a made ingredient and null for any other (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchSource`, the validator, `CK_RawMaterials_SubAssemblyMatchesSource`). Only a recipe kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen: not a packaging set, not a product's own (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureLinkableByMadeIngredient`). Several made ingredients can be made from one sub-assembly, each with its own add-ins and weight loss, and products can link the same one.
    - **Add-ins: `IngredientAddInLine`** (`IngredientId` Cascade, `AddInIngredientId` Restrict, `Grams`, `SortOrder`; unique per made ingredient and add-in), the made ingredient's own extras on top of the recipe, as a product's linked usage has `UsageAddInLine`. None is fine; each needs positive grams, comes once, and isn't already in the recipe (`IngredientBomRules.EnsureValidAddIns`, sharing `AddInRules` with `ProductRules.EnsureValidAddIns`). They belong to the made ingredient: the sub-assembly's lines and totals never include them.
    - **Its bill of materials is read, never stored**: the sub-assembly's lines, then the add-ins (`IngredientBomLine`, a read model since 2026-10-27, `IsAddIn` telling them apart; `Infrastructure/Queries/IngredientBoms` is the one definition, which the cascade, the cycle check and `IngredientBomLineRepository` all read). So everything that read a BOM (the Price Simulator, the Production Plan, the Scaling tab) works unchanged.
    - **Migrations `MadeIngredientSubAssembly` and `DropIngredientBomLines`, with a one-off SQL script between them** (a migration is never hand-edited). The first added the column and `ingredient_add_in_lines`; the script, run on the dev database in one transaction after a manual backup (`tinapayanrms_20261027_124436_manual.backup`), made each made ingredient's BOM the sub-assembly it's made from; the second dropped `ingredient_bom_lines` and added the check. The script reused a standalone sub-assembly of the same name whose one line was the made ingredient at the BOM's whole output (Cinnamon Syrup and Milk Streusel, both at 0% loss, put there by Move to In-House), putting the BOM's lines back in it; for the other eight it added one named after the ingredient in the Filling role (Butter Icing, Breads; Custard Filling; Ube Paste Mix; Pork Oil; Macapuno Filling; Monggo Paste Mix; Sugar Cinnamon; Dedication Chiffon Cake Base). Every raw material's stored cost and every existing sub-assembly's totals were the same before and after (compared row by row), and Recalculate Costs then found nothing to correct.
    - They have no brand prices or price history.
    - **`WeightLossPercent`**: weight lost while making the ingredient (e.g. water boiled off syrup). At least 0 and less than 100, enforced by `RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchSource`. **The screen defaults a new made ingredient to 3%** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: "the Weight Loss % TextBox should be preserved with a default value of 3% and should be customizable"; it was 0% before), seeded by the `EditingSource` subscription with `??=` so a saved material keeps what it was saved with, and the box caps at 0–99.99 to match the rule. A cleared box still saves 0 — a legitimate custom value.
    - Output grams = `Σ BOM line grams × (1 − WeightLossPercent / 100)`, the BOM being the sub-assembly's lines and the add-ins.
    - Cost per gram = `Σ (BOM line grams × component cost per gram) / output grams`. The buffered cost per gram is the same formula over the components' buffered costs, with nothing of its own on top (see `CostBufferPercent` above, 2026-09-21). The formula itself is `IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost`; a component's cost used here is whatever's currently stored on it — see the propagation note below for why that's always safe to read directly rather than walking the tree every time.
    - Example: simple syrup made from 1000g sugar (₱0.09/g, so ₱90) and 500g water (₱0, i.e. never priced: a literal ₱0 brand price is rejected, so "free" means unpriced, not a zero price), with 20% weight loss, gives 1500 × 0.8 = 1200g. It costs ₱90 / 1200 = ₱0.075/g.
  - **Linked** ingredients (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "Raw Material cost link to another raw material") are parts of another ingredient, costed as a share of its cost. An egg is bought by the 1,500 g tray at ₱135 (₱0.09/g); egg white is 70% of its weight and carries 30% of its cost, egg yolk 30% of the weight and 70% of the cost. A made ingredient couldn't do this: a BOM spreads cost by weight only.
    - **`LinkedToId`** (self-referencing FK, Restrict: an egg can't be deleted while a part links to it; the driver's `SqlException` shows as "in use elsewhere"), **`WeightSharePercent`** and **`CostSharePercent`** (each above 0, at most 100). Only a linked ingredient has them, and it has no price history, no BOM and no weight loss (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchSource`, the validator, `CK_RawMaterials_LinkMatchesSource`). The link and shares can be edited after creation, like a weight loss; the source can't.
    - **Cost per gram = linked-to cost per gram × cost share ÷ weight share** (`RawMaterialRules.LinkedCost`, the only place the formula is). White: 0.09 × 30/70 = ₱0.038571; yolk: 0.09 × 70/30 = ₱0.21. 1,050 g of white and 450 g of yolk cost ₱40.50 + ₱94.50 = ₱135, the whole tray. It doesn't depend on the unit the egg was priced in. Stored costs keep 4 decimals (`decimal(18,4)`), so white is stored as ₱0.0386 — the same limit every stored cost per gram has.
    - **The cost share is of the linked-to ingredient's unbuffered cost** (the user's rule, 2026-09-16: "the Cost Share must be the percentage of the unbuffered cost not the buffered one"), **and the part inherits the linked-to ingredient's `CostBufferPercent`** (2026-09-17: "i want the linked ingredient to inherit the buffer cost %"). Egg at ₱0.09/g with a 10% buffer gives yolk (30% / 70%) ₱0.21/g, buffered ₱0.231/g. **Since 2026-09-19 the buffered cost is the same share of the linked-to ingredient's buffered cost** (the egg's being a fixed amount now), so the inherited percent is only shown; the numbers match while the egg's buffered cost is its cost with its % on top. The percent, not the buffered cost, is what's inherited: the share is still taken before any buffer, so it never counts twice, and a made linked-to ingredient's stacked buffered rollup doesn't leak in.
      - **Stored on the part, kept in step.** `RawMaterialRepository` sets a linked ingredient's `CostBufferPercent` from the linked-to ingredient's on add and update, whatever the form sent (`EnsureValidLinkAsync` returns it), and `IngredientCostPropagator` copies it again every time the part is recomputed. A buffer edit on an ingredient already cascades, so it reaches every part, down a chain of links; relinking takes the new ingredient's. Storing it keeps everything that reads `CostBufferPercent` (the Usage Example, the list) right without knowing about links.
      - The Linked Cost card shows the inherited percent read-only (bound to `EditingLinkedTo.CostBufferPercent`); it's changed on the linked-to ingredient.
      - History: it first shipped stacking the buffers the way a made ingredient's did until 2026-09-21 (the share of the egg's *buffered* cost, then the part's own buffer); on 2026-09-16 the share moved to the unbuffered cost with only the part's own buffer on top; on 2026-09-17 the part's own buffer gave way to the inherited one.
    - **The parts of one ingredient take at most 100% of its weight and at most 100% of its cost** (the user's choice; `RawMaterialRules.EnsureLinkSharesFit`, checked by `RawMaterialRepository` on add and update against the *other* parts, so a part never counts against itself). Less is fine — the shell is neither white nor yolk. The message names the other parts: "Egg Yolk (30%) already takes 30% of Egg's weight, so Egg White can take at most 70%."
    - **Ingredients only** (the user's choice): the target must be another ingredient (`EnsureValidLinkTarget`, which also refuses a link to itself), purchased, made or itself linked. Packaging stays purchased-only; `CK_RawMaterials_OnlyIngredientsAreMade` already covers it.
    - **A link is one more edge in the cost graph** (see Reactive Costing Cascade), so a cycle can't close through a mix of BOMs and links: linking egg white to a meringue base made from egg white is refused, and so is a BOM that uses a part linked to the ingredient being made. `IngredientDependencies.LoadAsync` builds the combined graph for both checks (`IngredientBomRules.EnsureNoCycle`).
    - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, against a throwaway migrated database (dropped afterwards), with real clicks and keystrokes: a seeded Egg Yolk loaded into the Linked Cost card; Egg White was created as Linked to Egg, 75% / 30% was refused with "Egg Yolk (30%) already takes 30% of Egg's weight, so Egg White can take at most 70%.", and 70% / 30% saved at ₱0.0386 with the other-parts caption showing 100% of the weight and cost.
- **IngredientBomLine**: one component of a made ingredient. **A read model since 2026-10-27** (see Made ingredients above): a line of the sub-assembly it's made from, or one of its add-ins (`IsAddIn`), read through `IngredientBoms`; the table and its configuration are gone, and the bullets below describe it until then. It has `IngredientId` (the made ingredient), `ComponentIngredientId` (a raw material of the Ingredient type, purchased or made), and `Grams`, the amount. `IngredientBomRules.EnsureValidLines` rejects an empty set, non-positive grams, or the same component twice (combine it into one line instead).
  - BOMs can nest to any depth: caramel can be made from syrup, and syrup from sugar. `IngredientBomRules.EnsureNoCycle` blocks an ingredient from appearing anywhere in its own BOM tree, checked at save time by walking the *other* made ingredients' current BOM graph.
  - `IngredientId`'s FK is Cascade (deleting the made ingredient takes its own BOM lines with it); `ComponentIngredientId`'s FK is Restrict (deleting an ingredient that's in use as a component is blocked — remove it from the BOM first). Both can't be Cascade: SQL Server rejects two cascade paths from the same table to the same parent table.
  - BOM lines use ingredients only, never packaging (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureUsableInRecipes`, checked by `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`).
- **Packaging materials** (`Type = Packaging`):
  - `BaseUnitId` (FK to UnitOfMeasure; `StandardUnitId` until 2026-09-13) is its base unit, set per material: typically a count unit (piece) for boxes and wrappers, a length unit (cm) for ribbon. No rule restricts which unit that can be — the shop owner is trusted to pick a sensible one — but it's fixed once anything is measured in it (see Multi-unit materials).
  - `Units` are its alternative units (a pack of 100 boxes is `BaseUnitsPerUnit = 100`, a 1000 cm ribbon roll `1000`), and `CostPerBaseUnit` its stored cost.
  - Always purchased, and priced through brand prices like a purchased ingredient.
  - Packaging lines can be entered in the base unit or any alternative unit (see PackagingLine).
- **RawMaterialBrandPrice** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "convert the textboxes to a datagrid" with Brand, Purchase Unit, Quantity per Purchase Unit and Cost Buffer columns): what each brand of a purchased material costs now. `Brand` is optional free text (trimmed, max 100, `null` when blank); `UnitId` is one of the material's units or `null` for per base unit; `Price` is per unit and positive (`CK_RawMaterialBrandPrices_Price`). **The material is costed at the dearest brand per base unit** (`RawMaterialRules.ApplyBrandPrices`/`FindDearestBrandPrice`; the user's call: "always use the max of the purchase price of different brands"), so costing stays conservative. A brand is priced once per unit, whatever its case (`EnsureDistinctBrandPrices`); the same brand in two units is fine. There is no default-brand flag: the user dropped it once the max rule decided the cost. `RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.SetAsync` (and the screen's Save) replaces them all, reprices and cascades.
- **RawMaterialPriceHistory** (merged from `IngredientPriceHistory` and `PackagingPriceHistory`): **a log of the prices a purchased material has been costed at** since 2026-09-18, at the user's request. Before, it was the price timeline the cost came from (latest by date, then id, via `PriceHistoryRules`, now removed); entries could be added, edited and deleted, each repricing the material. Now `RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.RepriceAsync` adds an entry, dated today, with the dearest brand's price, unit and `Brand`, whenever brand prices move the cost; a Save that leaves the cost as it was logs nothing, and one that leaves no prices logs nothing either. **Deleting an entry only tidies the log** and never reprices. Made ingredients have none. The migration `RawMaterialBrandPrices` added the table and `Brand`; each purchased material's latest entry was carried over as one unnamed brand by a one-off SQL script run against the local database (a migration is never hand-edited, so the data step couldn't live in it). **A Save that changed nothing logged the same price again, until 2026-10-03** (found in a code review: Cello Sheet Wrapper 10 x 14 had three entries at 170 in the same unit, two of them on one day). A cost that doesn't come out even (170 ÷ 240 = 0.708333…) was worked out to 28 places and compared with the stored one's 20, so it always read as changed, and the cascade likewise re-dated a made ingredient's cost on a Save that changed nothing. Every stored cost per base unit is now set through `RawMaterialRules.StoredCost` (20 places, rounded half away from zero as SQL Server rounds: Egg Whole is stored as …66667), so an unchanged cost compares equal (`StoredCostPrecisionPersistenceTests`, which fail on the old code). The entries already logged twice were left as they are.
- **SubAssemblyRole**: an editable lookup table (`Id`, `Name`, `SortOrder`, `IsPackaging`), seeded with Dough, Filling, Topping, Inclusion and Finishing. More roles, such as Glaze, can be added in Settings → Roles. `SortOrder` sets the order of a recipe's sections. A role can't be deleted while any sub-assembly uses it.
  - **The Packaging role** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "Sub-Assemblies can contain a subassembly for packaging"): seeded at **Id 6** with `IsPackaging = true`, the only role with the flag. A standalone sub-assembly in it is a **packaging set** (see Packaging sets below). The code checks the flag, never the name, so the role can be renamed; `SubAssemblyRoleRepository.DeleteAsync` refuses to delete it, and nothing in Settings can clear the flag (`UpdateAsync` writes only `Name` and `SortOrder`).
    - Id 6 was freed by hand first. The dev database's roles had drifted from the seeds (Inclusion, Id 4, deleted; Finishing, Id 5, renamed Pan Greasing; Sponge added at 6; an unused "Packaging" role at 7), so a `HasData` row would have clashed on the key or the unique name. The user deleted the old Packaging row, and a one-off script moved Pan Greasing to 4 and Sponge to 5 (neither was used by a sub-assembly) and reseeded the identity to 5; the migration `PackagingSubAssemblies` then inserted Id 6. A fresh database gets 1–5 as seeded plus 6.
  - **Product-owned roles: `IsProductOwned`** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "allow products to own Filling, Pan Greasing, and Topping subassembly … add an option in the Roles setting which can set a role if it can be product owned"). A checkbox on Settings → Roles (and a read-only Product-owned column in its grid); never on the Packaging role (`SubAssemblyRoleValidator`, `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureProductOwnedAllowed`, in `AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`, and the checkbox is disabled for it). See "Product-owned sub-assemblies" under SubAssembly. Not seeded: a seeded flag would skip the conversion below, and seed Id 4 is Pan Greasing in the dev database, not Inclusion. The dev database's Filling, Topping and Pan Greasing were ticked in the app.
- **SubAssembly** + **SubAssemblyLine**: a sub-assembly is an in-house recipe that products use directly, such as a dough, a filling or a topping. Its lines are the ingredients that make it up.
  - **Every sub-assembly is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen, and every product links the ones it uses** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "after the subassembly is added to the product and save, that subassembly which is unique to the product itself is stored automatically to the Subassembly with product name as the name … it will automatically use by the product … as link subassembly"). **Nothing was owned by a product from then until later that day** (see Product-owned sub-assemblies, next): `OwnerProductId` and `OwnerProduct` are gone (migration `SubAssembliesAllStandalone`), `Name` is required, and the unique index is on `Name` alone. The ownership bullets below record how it worked from 2026-09-14 to 2026-09-23.
    - **A new section becomes a sub-assembly on Save.** A section typed in the Products screen's Recipe Structure (New, Copy an existing one, or Make a Copy of a linked one) goes to `SetRecipeAsync` as a `NewSubAssemblyDraft(RoleId, Lines, AuthoredField, AuthoredValue, SectionName)` (it replaced `OwnedSubAssemblyDraft`, which carried an id; `RecipeDraft` is `(New, Linked)`). Save inserts a sub-assembly with its lines and links it: the piece size goes on the usage, as for any link, and it has no add-ins. From then on the product shows it like any link: read-only lines, the badge "Cheese Roll Dough, from Sub-Assemblies" that opens it there, Make a Copy. **Its lines were edited only on the Sub-Assemblies screen** (the user's choice, over the product that made it still editing them), until 2026-09-27: see Edit a linked one's lines in the product, below.
    - **Its name is the product's and the role's** (`SubAssemblyRules.StandaloneNameFor`): "Cheese Roll Dough", and with the section's own name after them when it has one that isn't just the role's, "Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese" (the user's choice, over the product's name alone, which would have needed names unique per role). The product's name is read inside the save's transaction, so a new or renamed product saves under its new name. **A name already taken gets the next number**, "Cheese Roll Dough 2" (`UniqueStandaloneName`, case- and space-insensitively, against every sub-assembly and the ones the same save just made), cut short to fit 100 characters with its number. Renaming the product later leaves its sub-assemblies' names alone.
    - **Removing a section, or deleting the product, keeps the sub-assembly** (the user's choice, over deleting it when nothing else links it): `SetRecipeAsync` only replaces the usages, and a product's usages cascade while the usage's sub-assembly FK is Restrict. So nothing refuses a product's Delete or a section's removal because another product links it any more. What's left unused stays on the Sub-Assemblies screen until it's deleted there, which is still refused while a product uses it.
    - **How a linked section goes by its name in a recipe** (`SubAssemblyRules.SectionLabel(name, productName, roleName)`, the user's choice of "section name only"): less the "Cheese Roll Filling: " it was saved under from that product, so the tab reads "Filling: Cheese"; and nothing at all when it's exactly "Cheese Roll Filling", saved from there without a name, so it goes by its role alone, as it did before it was saved, and still counts as the one without a name in its role. Another product's goes by its whole name ("Filling: Ube Bun Filling"). `SetRecipeAsync`, `EnsureDistinctWhereLinkedAsync` (a rename or new role on the Sub-Assemblies screen) and the Products form all read it the same way.
    - **Existing data**: the 11 sub-assemblies products owned in the dev database were converted by a one-off SQL script before the migration (a migration is never hand-edited, so the data step couldn't live in it), after a `COPY_ONLY` backup (`C:\Users\infotech\TinapayanRMS_before_AllStandalone_20260923.bak`). Each was named as above, numbered where taken, and released (`OwnerProductId` null); its usages were left exactly as they were, so its product and any other linking it link it now ("Milky Bread x 12 Topping: Milk Streusel" is used by both Milky Breads). The script ran with `QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON`, which `sqlcmd` leaves off and the filtered index needs. Tried first on a restored copy: the usages were identical before and after, and the migration went up, down and up. `TinapayanRMS_Render` and `TinapayanRMS_Demo` predate owned sub-assemblies and were left alone.
  - **Product-owned sub-assemblies** (2026-09-23, later the same day, at the user's request). `SubAssembly.OwnerProductId` is back (nullable, Cascade from `Product`; migration `ProductOwnedRoles`, with `SubAssemblyRole.IsProductOwned`), set only in a product-owned role. The user's choices, over the alternatives offered:
    - **A section made in a product in a product-owned role is that product's own** (new, Copy an existing one, or Make a Copy): `NewSubAssemblyDraft` gained an `Id` init property (0 new, else the product's own saved one, rewritten in place; the ~50 call sites that make new ones didn't change). In any other role a new one is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen and linked, as before (unless it's unlinked: see Unlink below); one of its own moved out of a product-owned role into such a role is released there (`OwnerProductId` null) and linked. Its lines are edited in the product (the Products form's third state, `ProductUsageEdit.IsOwned`; it had a "This product's own" badge until 2026-09-24, when the user had it taken out and its Role and Name moved into the badge's place, level with Move to Sub-Assemblies), it isn't on the Sub-Assemblies screen (`GetStandaloneAsync` filters it out, and `SaveStandaloneAsync`/`DeleteStandaloneAsync` refuse it), and taking it out of the recipe or deleting the product deletes it.
    - **Copy only**: another product can copy one (the Copy picker lists it as "Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese — Cheese Roll (Filling)", `GetLinkableAsync` now bringing each one's owner) but never link it (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureLinkable` in `SetRecipeAsync`; the Link picker leaves it out). So nothing but its product ever uses it, and it can be changed or removed freely. A product-owned role can still link one from the Sub-Assemblies screen. **Since 2026-10-26 its product's variants link it** (Create Variant, below), the one exception.
    - **Named like any other** ("Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese", unique, `StandaloneNameFor`/`UniqueStandaloneName`), and kept so: renaming its product (`ProductRepository.UpdateAsync`, which `SaveAsync` runs even without a recipe) or its role (`SubAssemblyRoleRepository.UpdateAsync`) renames it (`SubAssemblyRules.IsNamedAfter`, numbered or cut to fit or not: one not named after them, like an adopted "Pan de Coco", keeps its name). **Its label is stored, never parsed back out of its name** (`SubAssembly.SectionName`, 2026-11-05, an independent code review): the label was read from the name by prefix until then, so a name numbered when the plain one was taken ("Cheese Roll Filling 2") read as a section called all of that and kept the old product's name after a rename. `SubAssemblyRules.LabelIn` reads a product's own by its section name and anything else by `SectionLabel`; Save, Clone and a role made product-owned write it, and a role made not product-owned clears it. A version snapshot keeps each section's label too (schema 2). Existing ones got theirs from their names once, by the `2026-11-05 section names` data upgrade (`Infrastructure/Upgrades/`). Renames go through `SubAssemblyRepository.RenameAsync` (a throwaway name first, then the new ones, so two can swap).
    - **Turning a role product-owned** (`UpdateAsync`, one transaction, after a confirmation saying how many move, `CountOwnershipChangesAsync`): each of its sub-assemblies on the Sub-Assemblies screen that exactly one product's recipe uses becomes that product's own, keeping its name (`AdoptSingleUseAsync`); that product's add-ins on it are merged into its lines, after its own. Used by several, or none, it stays. **Turning it off** releases every one products own in it to the Sub-Assemblies screen, still used (linked) by the same product (`ReleaseOwnedAsync`); merged add-ins stay merged, which the confirmation says. The Products screen picks either up on its next refresh: with nothing typed it reads the product again, so what Save compares against is how it's saved now.
    - **Clone and Create Variant give the new product its own copy of each owned section** (named after the new product; lines, piece size and what it went by), and link what the source links (`ProductCloner.DerivedRecipe`, which replaced `LinkedRecipe`). Each copy is drafted `SectionPlacement.ProductsOwn`, so it stays the new product's own in a role that isn't product-owned (one the source unlinked).
    - **Unlink, in any role** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "the ability for Product's subassembly to unlink and copy the link Subassembly"; the user's choice, over surfacing Make a Copy or releasing a product's own). A linked section's form has **Unlink** beside Make a Copy (`UnlinkCommand`; both build the copy in `CopyOfLinked`, the add-ins merged into its lines). Make a Copy is unchanged: its own in a product-owned role, otherwise kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen and linked. Unlink makes the copy **the product's own whatever its role**: its Role and Name show at once in place of the linked badge (`ProductUsageEdit` takes `isOwn`, and `IsOwned` is `LinkedName is null && (SubAssemblyId > 0 || isOwn)`), and Save sends it as `SectionPlacement.ProductsOwn`. The one it was linked to stays on the Sub-Assemblies screen as it was. The role flag is only a default now:
      - `SetRecipeAsync` makes a new section its own when it's placed `ProductsOwn`, or placed `ByRole` in a product-owned role. A saved one of its own is released only when it moves **out of a product-owned role** into one that isn't (the stored role's flag is read before `RoleId` changes, since the tracked `Role` isn't moved over until SaveChanges). One unlinked in a plain role was never the role's, so it stays its own wherever it moves.
      - Renaming a role renames the own sub-assemblies named after it whether or not it's product-owned (`SubAssemblyRoleRepository.UpdateAsync`), after any adopt or release.
      - Turning a role's flag off still releases every own one in it, unlinked ones included: nothing tells them apart without a column, and the confirmation gives the count.
      - **Fixed along the way (the user's request): undoing an Unlink or a Make a Copy left Save offered**, though Save then wrote nothing (`IsSameRecipe`). Opening the copy's form moved the settled snapshot's form fields onto it (`RebaseSettledForm`), and a restore deliberately doesn't rebase, since the form it puts back may hold typing not yet in the recipe; so the linked form put back was measured against the copy's. Now **`UndoHistory` keeps, beside each step, the settled snapshot as it stood when the step was taken** (at `Begin` for an undo step, at the move for the other stack), handed back by new `TryUndo`/`TryRedo(out restore, out settled)` overloads, and `ProductsViewModel.RestoreSnapshot` ends by rebasing the settled snapshot's form and tabs, and nothing else (`WithFormOf`), onto it. A form put back as it showed then is clean again, and typing put back still counts, since the settled form then was the section as it showed, not as typed. The recipe, costing and header stay as settled now, so a costing-defaults rebase in between isn't lost. Raw Materials and Sub-Assemblies don't rebase their forms and keep the old overloads. `Undoing_Make_a_Copy_stops_offering_Save` fails on the code before; `Undoing_to_a_form_with_typing_not_yet_applied_still_offers_Save` guards the other half; `UndoHistoryTests` covers the overloads.
      - Covered by three cases in `ProductOwnedSubAssemblyPersistenceTests`, `Unlink_makes_a_linked_section_the_products_own_in_a_role_that_isnt_product_owned` and `Undoing_Unlink_puts_the_link_back_and_redo_unlinks_again`. No migration.
    - **Change a link in place** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "Add an Edit icon besides the Link badge in the Products subassembly so that i can change the link without deleting the Subassembly").
