An Edit glyph beside a linked section's badge (`StartChangingLinkCommand`; a plain `Button` in the theme's `WPFGlyphButtonStyle`, as MainWindow's icon buttons) swaps the badge for a searchable picker (`ChangeLinkOptions`: the Sub-Assemblies screen's recipes the recipe doesn't use yet, any role, never packaging or a product's own) and a cancel glyph (`CancelChangingLinkCommand`). Picking one (`ChangeLinkTarget`) runs `ChangeLinkAsync`: it reads the new one's lines, then replaces the section in place (`ReplaceUsage`, one undo step), so it keeps its tab, which takes the new one's role and name. What's the product's carries over from the form as it stands: the piece size (the typed figure) and add-ins, less any add-in the new recipe already has, which a toast names. Lines edited in the old link's form but not saved are dropped with it. `EnsureSectionsDistinct` still applies. Save writes the new link; the old sub-assembly stays on the Sub-Assemblies screen. Changing `EditingUsage` ends a change in progress. Covered by `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.Changing_a_link_relinks_the_section_keeping_its_piece_size_and_the_add_ins_the_new_recipe_lacks`.
    - **Move to Sub-Assemblies, Unlink's reverse** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "the reverse of this feature is to have the subassembly owned by the product to be move to the SubAssembly and link to it"). On a section of the product's own (`IsOwned`: saved, or an unlinked copy not saved yet), in any role, the form has **Move to Sub-Assemblies** (`MoveToSubAssembliesCommand`, shown by `IsEditingOwnedUsage`) where a linked one has Unlink and Make a Copy. It applies whatever is typed into the form first (`ApplyUsageForm`; a refused one stays, with the message), so what moves is what shows, then marks the section (`ProductUsageEdit.IsReleasing`, via `Released()`), in one undo step. Until Save the section is still edited here, and a "Moves to Sub-Assemblies on Save" badge shows before its Role and Name (`IsReleasing`). Save sends it as `SectionPlacement.MoveToSubAssemblies`, which `SetRecipeAsync` puts ahead of the role: a saved one keeps its id, name ("Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese") and the lines just written, loses its owner, and the product's usage links it at the same piece size, the end state `ReleaseOwnedAsync` gives a whole role; a new one is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen, named after the product. After the reload it's a linked section like any other, and other products can link it. In a product-owned role it stays on the Sub-Assemblies screen; only turning the role's flag off and on again would adopt it back. Covered by three cases in `ProductOwnedSubAssemblyPersistenceTests`, `Move_to_Sub_Assemblies_puts_the_products_own_there_with_what_was_typed_and_links_it` and `Undoing_Move_to_Sub_Assemblies_keeps_it_the_products_own_and_stops_offering_Save`. No migration. The Packaging tab has the same since 2026-09-30, for the product's own packaging lines: see "Move to Sub-Assemblies on the Packaging tab" under Derived products.
    - **Move to In-House, reworked to link the same day** (2026-10-27, with made ingredients made from sub-assemblies, above). **On the Products screen**, Save moves the product's own section to the Sub-Assemblies screen as Move to Sub-Assemblies does (`SectionPlacement.MoveToInHouse` is a move there too: `MovesToSubAssemblies`), keeping its lines and baker's percentages, links the product to it at the same piece size, and adds the made ingredient made from it (`RawMaterialRepository.AddMadeAsync(dbContext, made, subAssemblyId)`, 0% loss, no add-ins). The section is no longer replaced by one line of the ingredient, and "can't move to Sub-Assemblies and to In-House in one save" went with `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureCanBecomeMadeIngredient`. Its badge reads "Moves to Sub-Assemblies and In-House on Save, as …". **On the Sub-Assemblies screen** the list item is **Make In-House Ingredient…** (`MakeInHouseIngredientCommand`, `ISubAssemblyRepository.AddMadeIngredientAsync`, which returns the ingredient's id): it adds the made ingredient made from the recipe and leaves the recipe, its products and their versions alone (`MovedToInHouse` and its "one no product uses goes" branch are gone). Both prompts are `MadeIngredientPrompt.RequestFor`, now given a title. The bullets below describe the first version.
    - **Move to In-House** (2026-10-27, at the user's request: "how about moving a subassembly to in-house?", then "limiting it to subassemblies owned by a product"). The question before it was whether made ingredients ("In-House", the user's category of them) were redundant beside sub-assemblies; they aren't, since a `SubAssemblyLine` only references an ingredient, so a made ingredient is the only recipe one recipe can use inside another (Butter Icing is a line in 16 toppings), and only a made ingredient has a weight loss. Nesting sub-assemblies instead was weighed and rejected (the cascade's two fixed steps would become one mixed ordering). So a product's own section can become a made ingredient instead: on its form, beside Move to Sub-Assemblies (`MoveToInHouseCommand`, shown by `CanMoveEditingUsageToInHouse`, i.e. `ProductUsageEdit.CanMoveToInHouse`: its own, not already moving either way, and not just one made ingredient already, which would only be wrapped).
      - **Limited to a product's own** (the user's choice), so only that product, and the variants linking it, use the section, and nothing needs a "this also changes …" warning; doughs and batters, on the Sub-Assemblies screen, are left out with the baker's percentages they need. In the dev database that was 62 own sections, all fillings, toppings and miscellaneous, 2 of them with baker's percentages, and 6 already one made ingredient.
      - **The prompt** (`PromptForMadeIngredient`, a `ChoicePromptRequest` shown by `TextPromptWindow.PromptWithChoice`, which added a picker row under the name box) asks for the name, starting with the section's saved name (`SubAssemblyRules.StandaloneNameFor`), and a **Made category** (the first part of this request: see RawMaterialCategory), read fresh from `IRawMaterialCategoryRepository` on each click (a new dependency of `ProductsViewModel`), starting on In-House when one has that name. With no Made category it says to add one with New Made Category; a name a loaded ingredient has, or another section here is moving under, is refused at once.
      - **Applied on Save**, as Move to Sub-Assemblies is: the form is applied first, then `ProductUsageEdit.MovedToInHouse(MadeIngredientDraft)` marks the section, in one undo step, with a "Moves to In-House on Save, as …" badge; the snapshot and `IsSameRecipe` compare `ToMadeIngredient`, so Undo stops offering Save. `NewSubAssemblyDraft.ToMadeIngredient` carries it. `SetRecipeAsync` checks, before writing anything, that the section stays the product's own and isn't also `Release`d, and that the name is free (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureCanBecomeMadeIngredient`, against every raw material and the other sections of the save) and the category exists; then, where it writes the section's lines, `RawMaterialRepository.AddMadeAsync` adds the made ingredient
 (`RawMaterialRepository.AddAsync`, which refuses a category that isn't Made), writes the lines as its BOM in order (`IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`), costs it (`IngredientCostPropagator`), and the section gets the one line `(ingredient, Σ grams)`.
      - **Weight loss 0%** (the user's choice in the proposal, over the screen's usual 3%), so the section costs what it did: the ingredient's cost per gram is the lines' cost over their weight, and the one line takes their whole weight back. The real loss is set on the Raw Materials screen afterwards and cascades as usual. A BOM has no baker's percentages, so base ticks and typed percentages go.
      - Covered by `MoveToInHousePersistenceTests` (five cases), `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.Move_to_In_House_asks_for_a_name_and_Made_category_and_Save_makes_the_section_that_made_ingredient` and `…needs_a_Made_category_and_a_name_no_ingredient_has`, and `ProductsViewModelUndoTests.Undoing_Move_to_In_House_keeps_its_lines_and_stops_offering_Save`. No migration.
      - **And from the Sub-Assemblies screen** (the same day, at the user's request: "add a feature wherein i can move a subassembly in the Sub-Assemblies view to Made type of Raw Materials", lifting the product's-own limit for that screen). **Move to In-House…** on a recipe's row in the list (`SubAssembliesViewModel.MoveToInHouseCommand`; not on a packaging set), beside Rename and Delete, and **saved at once** as the list's other items are: the prompt's OK is the confirmation, and its message says what happens to the sub-assembly. `ISubAssemblyRepository.MoveToMadeIngredientAsync` (one transaction) refuses a packaging set, a product's own ("…is Cheese Roll's own. Move it to In-House from that product's recipe instead.") and a taken name, then adds the ingredient from the lines in order. **One no product uses goes** (the default chosen without asking: a move leaves nothing behind where nothing needs it); **one a product uses stays**, its lines the one ingredient at the batch's weight (`MovedToInHouse.IsSubAssemblyKept`), so the products keep their links, piece sizes and add-ins and cost what they did. Each such product gets a version as a Save here gives one ("Ube Base moved to In-House as Ube Filling", `ProductVersionRecorder`). Unsaved changes to the one on the form are refused first ("Save Custard first, or undo its changes…"): what moves is what's saved. Not undoable from the screen.
      - Shared by both: `RawMaterialRepository.AddMadeAsync` (moved there from the usage repository) adds and costs the ingredient and returns the line that replaces the lines; `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidMadeIngredientName` checks the name (`EnsureCanBecomeMadeIngredient` calls it for a product's section); `MadeIngredientPrompt` (UI) reads the Made categories and builds the prompt, and `SubAssembliesViewModel` takes `IRawMaterialCategoryRepository` too. Covered by three more `MoveToInHousePersistenceTests` cases and three `SubAssembliesViewModelTreeTests` (`Move_to_In_House_…`). No migration.


    - **Edit a linked one's lines in the product** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "the possibility that the Linked SubAssembly can be edited within the Product"). The user's choices, over the alternatives offered: the change is **written with the product's Save** (not on Apply), so Undo and Revert cover it; **the form names the other products using it and Save asks first**; and the lines are **always editable**, not behind an Edit Lines button. Unlink and Make a Copy are unchanged, for a change meant for this product alone.
      - A linked section's lines grid is typed into like an own one's (`ProductsViewModel.IsFormLinesEditable`, `IsFormEditingLines || IsEditingLinkedUsage`; `IsFormEditingLines` still means a new or own section, whose Role and Name show). Only a link being picked still shows its lines read-only. Its role and name stay the Sub-Assemblies screen's.
      - `ProductUsageEdit` keeps a linked one's lines as last read (`SavedLines`); `AreLinkedLinesEdited` compares ingredients, grams and base ticks, not costs. Only then does `CurrentRecipe` send them, as `LinkedUsageDraft.Lines` (null otherwise), so a product's Save never puts back lines the Sub-Assemblies screen changed since. `SetRecipeAsync` checks them with `EnsureValidLines` up front, checks this product's add-ins against them rather than the stored ones, and relines the sub-assembly (`SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`, which restores its totals) before writing the add-ins, all in the product's transaction. Products are costed on read, so nothing else cascades.
      - A refresh keeps lines changed here: `WithLinked` takes the fresh lines as `savedLines` and keeps the edited ones repriced, and the open form keeps typing not yet applied (`IsFormShowing`), where until now it always showed the Sub-Assemblies screen's lines.
      - **Who else uses it**: the form shows no notice (an "Also used by …" banner under the badge was dropped as noise that took up room). Save reads `GetStandaloneLinkedByAsync` and asks through `ConfirmDelete`: "Save changes the lines of Cream Loaf Dough, also used by Cream Loaf. Their recipes and costs change with it. Save anyway?"; declined, nothing is written and the change stays in the form.
      - Not checked, as on the Sub-Assemblies screen: a new line that *another* product has as an add-in on the same sub-assembly.
      - Covered by `LinkedLinesPersistenceTests` (four cases) and `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.A_linked_sections_lines_are_edited_in_the_product_and_Save_writes_them_to_the_sub_assembly_after_asking`. No migration.
    - **Existing data**: after a `COPY_ONLY` backup (`C:\Users\infotech\TinapayanRMS_before_ProductOwnedRoles_20260923.bak`) and the migration, Filling, Topping and Pan Greasing were ticked in Settings → Roles on the running app. 4 Fillings and 3 Toppings became their products' own; "Milky Bread x 12 Topping: Milk Streusel", used by both Milky Breads, stayed on the Sub-Assemblies screen; Pan Greasing had none. Every sub-assembly's totals and every usage were identical before and after, so no cost moved.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** **Owned by the product it's made in** (`OwnerProductId`, Cascade from `Product`; 2026-09-14, at the user's request — until then sub-assemblies were a library of their own, on a Sub-Assemblies screen, and any product picking one shared it without anything saying so). A product's own sub-assembly is made and edited only inside its recipe on the Products screen; the owner always uses it. Another product can **link** it (a usage of it — live, with read-only lines and its own piece size) or **copy** it (an independent sub-assembly of its own). See Derived products below.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** every sub-assembly is standalone now. **Or standalone: `OwnerProductId` null** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: "a Subassembly not owned by any product but can use for linking or cloning"). It's made and edited on the restored **Sub-Assemblies screen** (see Application Architecture), and every product using it links it, since none owns it. Products link or copy it exactly as they do another product's. `ISubAssemblyRepository.SaveStandaloneAsync`/`DeleteStandaloneAsync` write it (one transaction: name, role, then the lines through `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`), and refuse a product's own; `SetRecipeAsync` refuses a standalone one listed as a product's own.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** a new section's optional name now only tells repeats apart and goes into the name it's saved under; `SectionLabel`, `DisplayName` and `EnsureValidOwnedNames` (now `EnsureValidSectionName`, a length check) changed or went, as above. **A product's own sub-assembly goes by its role, and by an optional name when its role repeats** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "Give repeats a name again: the tab reads 'Filling: Ube' and 'Filling: Cheese'"). From 2026-09-17 to 2026-09-18 a product's own had no name and a product had one sub-assembly in each role (`EnsureOnePerRole`, the user's choice then over numbering repeats "Filling 2"). Now **a product can have several sub-assemblies in one role, its own and linked together, as long as they can be told apart** (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureSectionsDistinct`, in `SetRecipeAsync` and on the Products form's Apply): within a role no two go by the same name (case- and space-insensitively), and at most one goes without, so an existing recipe stays valid and a second Filling only needs a name of its own. What a section goes by (`SubAssemblyRules.SectionLabel`): a product's own by its `Name`, which it needn't have; a linked one by its name, or else its owner product's ("Filling: Cream Loaf"). A product's own names are optional, trimmed (blank is null, `NormalizeName`), at most 100 characters, and unique within the product whatever their roles (`EnsureValidOwnedNames`, as the `(OwnerProductId, Name)` index filtered on `[Name] IS NOT NULL` has it; the schema already allowed this, so no migration). `SetRecipeAsync` saves the names (it used to clear them), letting renamed ones' names go first so a swap doesn't trip the index halfway. Where it isn't plain whose it is — messages, the link picker — one is called by its product and role, with its name when it has one: "Cream Loaf's Dough", "Cream Loaf's Filling: Ube" (`SubAssemblyRules.DisplayName`). A clone keeps each one's name; in a role the source has more than one in, a linked one without a name of its own takes its owner's, which is what told it apart there.
    - **A new role or name can't leave a linking product unable to tell its sections apart**: giving an owned one (in `SetRecipeAsync`) or a standalone one (`SaveStandaloneAsync`) another role or name is refused when a product linking it would then have two in a role by one name, naming that product (`SubAssemblyRepository.EnsureDistinctWhereLinkedAsync`, which replaced `EnsureRoleFreeWhereLinkedAsync`).
    - **Existing data**: migration `ProductSubAssembliesGoByRole` made `Name` nullable and filtered the unique `(OwnerProductId, Name)` index to `[Name] IS NOT NULL`; nothing was renamed or deleted (a data step would have meant hand-editing the migration). Nothing shows an old owned name, and `SetRecipeAsync` clears it the next time its product's recipe is saved. A product saved with two sub-assemblies in one role still loads (two tabs of that role, Almond Cheese Bread's two Doughs among them), and can still save its name, packaging and costing; changing its recipe, or cloning it, is refused until one of them is given another role or taken out. Its unchanged tabs can still be looked through: `ApplyUsageForm` only checks the role of a section that changed.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** every name is required and unique, on a plain unique index on `Name`. **Standalone names are unique among themselves** (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureStandaloneNameAvailable`, case- and space-insensitively). The same index enforces it: its filter is `[Name] IS NOT NULL` (`HasFilter(null)` until 2026-09-17), which keeps the ownerless rows in, where SQL Server's unique index treats their NULL owners as equal, and leaves the nameless owned ones out. EF's SQL Server convention would otherwise add `[OwnerProductId] IS NOT NULL` too, and drop the standalone rows from the index.
  - `RoleId` (FK to `SubAssemblyRole`, required): each sub-assembly has exactly one role, so custard is always a Filling. The owner can change it at any time; every recipe using the sub-assembly then shows it in the new role's place. (Until 2026-09-14 a role could only change while no product used the sub-assembly, since that broke role-tab links; those links are gone.)
  - Each line has an FK to an ingredient (a `RawMaterial` of the Ingredient type — rejected otherwise) and `Grams`. Line cost = grams × the ingredient's `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`, its buffered cost per gram. `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidLines` rejects no lines, non-positive grams or an ingredient twice, so a sub-assembly always has a batch.
  - **Baker's percentage: `SubAssemblyLine.IsFormulaBase`** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "a checkbox column to mark the ingredient as a reference, then find the total of all ingredients with check and use that as the divisor"). Each line is read as a percentage of the ticked lines' total weight — the formula's 100% base, usually the flour. `SubAssemblyRules.FormulaBaseWeight` sums the ticked lines and `CalculateBakersPercent(grams, baseWeight)` gives the figure, **null when nothing is ticked**: a glaze or a filling needn't have a base, and a blank is the honest answer, not a nought. The sum of every line's percentage — "whole formula 201.5%" in the caption under the table — is the number a batch is scaled by (`flour = target ÷ (total% ÷ 100)`).
    - **Per line, not per ingredient** (the user's design, over an `IsFlour` flag on the raw material). Sugar is the base of a syrup and an extra in a dough; two flours tick together and make 100% between them; and it never depends on the `Flour` category, which is user-editable free text and was already drifting (Cornstarch was filed under Butter). Named `IsFormulaBase` because "base" already means a *unit of measure* throughout this codebase (`BaseUnitId`, `CostPerBaseUnit`); the column header is "Base".
    - **Nothing about cost changes.** Grams stay the source of truth; the tick is a reading of the batch, never stored as a figure, and the batch's weight and cost don't move.
    - **Lines travel as `RecipeLine`** (`ApplicationCore/Interfaces/RecipeLine.cs`), a `readonly record struct (IngredientId, Grams, IsFormulaBase = false)` that replaced the `(int IngredientId, decimal Grams)` tuple in `OwnedSubAssemblyDraft.Lines`, `ISubAssemblyRepository.SaveStandaloneAsync`, `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidLines` and `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`. It has an **implicit conversion from the old tuple**, so the ~120 call sites writing `[(flourId, 1000m)]` compiled untouched (only three test helpers that declared the tuple type themselves changed). The same conversion makes a *lossy* projection compile silently — `.Select(l => (l.IngredientId, l.Grams))` drops the tick without an error — so the three places that rebuild lines from existing data name it explicitly: `ProductsViewModel.CurrentRecipe()` (miss it and ticks never save, and `IsSameRecipe` never even sees the change), `SubAssembliesViewModel`'s save, and `ProductCloner`'s own-copy branch. Anything that carries sub-assembly lines from one place to another in future has to do the same.
    - **Add-ins have no tick** and stay on the plain tuple (`LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns`, `UsageAddInLineRepository`): an add-in is never part of the base. Each still gets a percentage, **read against the linked recipe's base**, not its own weight — 100 g of buttermilk on a dough's 1,000 g of flour reads 10.0, and the whole-formula figure includes it. BOM lines are untouched: a BOM is a yield calculation with weight loss, not a baker's formula, and uses its own `(ComponentIngredientId, Grams)` tuple.
    - **Typed either way, and whichever was typed stays put** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "make typing percentages to get grams and vice versa work two ways", and their choice of what happens after). A line's grams or its baker's percentage can be typed; the other is worked out. **`SubAssemblyLine.AuthoredBakersPercent`** (nullable) records a typed percentage, null when the grams were typed — the same idea as a usage's `AuthoredField`/`AuthoredValue`, and chosen over "percentages always win" and "grams always win". When the base weight changes, a line typed as a percentage has its grams worked out again from it (`SubAssemblyRules.GramsFromBakersPercent`), and a line typed in grams keeps them while its percentage moves: on 1,000 g of flour, water typed as 65% and eggs typed as 100 g; double the flour and the water is 1,300 g, still 65%, while the eggs stay 100 g at 5%.
      - **Grams are still stored for every line** and still all that's costed. A typed percentage is worked back into grams the moment the base changes, in the form, and saved that way; the base only ever changes by editing that same formula, so the stored grams are never stale. `GramsFromBakersPercent` rounds to the 4 decimals grams are stored in, so reopening a saved line reproduces exactly what was worked out; unrounded, a line could come back a ten-thousandth off and make a recipe nobody touched look changed.
      - **Any change to the base weight counts**: a base line's grams, a tick, or a base line added or removed. A typed percentage always stays that percentage, which is the simple rule to predict. Ticking a line into the base makes it a grams line (the base is 100% between its lines by definition), and `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidLines` refuses a base line carrying a percentage. With nothing ticked, a typed percentage keeps its last grams and its percentage waits: tick the flour again and the line follows it.
      - **Where it can't be typed, it won't open**: on a base line, and while nothing is ticked (`IngredientLineRow.CanTypeBakersPercent`). SfDataGrid's `AllowEditing` is one setting per column, so `controls:GridCellEditing` (a grid-level `IsEnabled`, and `AllowWhen` naming a `bool` property of the row on the column, the way `MappingName` names the value) cancels `CurrentCellBeginEdit` on the rows that say no. The row's setter ignores such a value too, as a backstop.
      - **A typed percentage is in bold** (`controls:GridCellEmphasis`, `When="IsPercentTyped"`), the way the Products screen's typed piece-size figure once was. Only the percentage is marked: grams are the usual way in, and bolding them on nearly every row would drown out the ones that follow the flour. It's a renderer (`EmphasisNumericRenderer`, replacing the grid's `"Numeric"` one) binding the text's `FontWeight`, not a `CellStyle`: a cell style replaces the theme's own `GridCell` style, and basing one on the theme's (`SyncfusionGridCellStyle`, as Syncfusion's ConditionalFormattingDemo does) needs its whole dictionary merged in, beside the accent colours the app sets at startup. `OnUpdateDisplayBinding` calls `OnInitializeDisplayElement`, so the override also covers recycled rows.
      - **Carried everywhere lines are**: `RecipeLine.AuthoredBakersPercent`, `SectionLine`, `ProductUsageEdit.ShowsSameAs`, the three line-copying projections named above (`CurrentRecipe()`, Sub-Assemblies' save, `ProductCloner`), `Repriced`, and both add rows' item factories, which hand a new line the current base so a percentage can be typed into it straight away.
      - **Add-ins can be typed either way too, but only grams are kept.** An add-in's base is the linked recipe's, which belongs to another product and can change there without this form open; remembering a typed percentage would mean working its grams out on every read, in Products and Reports alike, from a base those screens don't load. So a percentage typed for an add-in fills in its grams and isn't kept, and isn't shown in bold.
    - **With nothing ticked, the column shows absolute percentage** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "if an assembly does not have a base that is checked, use the absolute percentage … the total grams/weight of assembly as the divisor. The header will become Absolute %"). This replaces the blank a base-less formula showed before. `SubAssemblyRules.CalculateAbsolutePercent(grams, batchWeight)` is a line's share of the whole batch, so the lines come to 100 between them. `IngredientLineRow.Percent` (renamed from `BakersPercent`, since it's now either figure) reads baker's percentage while `BaseWeight > 0` and absolute percentage against `BatchWeight` otherwise. `ApplyFormulaBase` hands every row both weights, and the batch counts the rows with an ingredient picked (`IngredientLineRow.BatchWeightOf`, the same count as the screens' batch weight).
      - **The header follows** (`SubAssembliesViewModel.PercentHeader`, `ProductsViewModel.UsageFormPercentHeader`, both from `IngredientLineRow.PercentHeader`), through a **`HeaderTemplate`** (`PercentHeaderTemplate` in each view) whose `TextBlock` binds the view's `DataContext` by `RelativeSource AncestorType=UserControl`.
        - **Not a bound `HeaderText`**, which was the first version. It looked right on the Sub-Assemblies screen, but the user found Products' Recipe Structure tabs showing the previous tab's header ("Baker's %" over Topping's absolute 100.0, which has no base). Every tab has its own `UsageFormPanel`, so its grid, and a changed `GridColumnBase.HeaderText` only reaches the header cell through the grid's row generator (`OnUpdateBindingInfo`, decompiled 34.2.7), which didn't redraw the header of the grid a newly shown tab brings in. A visual-tree binding in the header cell is always current. Verified in the app on Almond Cheese Bread by clicking Dough → Topping → Filling → Dough → Topping → Dough → Packaging → Topping (the header right each time), by ticking and unticking Topping's only line as its base (the header switched both ways), and on the Sub-Assemblies screen. The caption under the table says "Absolute %: each line's share of the batch. Tick the base — usually the flour — for baker's %."
      - **Read-only** (the user's choice, over typing one to work out the grams, or keeping a typed absolute percentage). `CanTypeBakersPercent` was already false without a base. The row's setter now checks that before a cleared value, so a line whose typed baker's percentage is waiting for a base to come back doesn't lose it, and `IsPercentTyped` (the bold) only holds while there's a base, since the absolute figure is never typed.
      - **On a linked recipe with add-ins, both tables divide by the whole batch** (the user's choice, over the recipe's weight alone): the recipe's lines and the add-ins together come to 100%. With a base, add-ins are still read against the recipe's base, as before.
    - Not modelled: a preferment (a sponge made as a made ingredient) ticked as the base counts its whole weight, not the flour inside it, so such a formula will disagree with a textbook — the user has a separate plan for preferments; and true hydration counting the water in milk and eggs.
  - A sub-assembly has a total weight in grams (the sum of its line grams) and a total ingredient cost, both stored and refreshed whenever its lines are saved or an ingredient's cost changes (the cascade).
  - **It can't be deleted from under a product that links it** (since 2026-09-23 that's only its Delete on the Sub-Assemblies screen: a section removed, or a product deleted, leaves it there). Until then, removing it from its owner's recipe, or deleting the owner, was refused while another product's usage points at it, and the message names those products (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureNotLinkedElsewhere`: "Cream Loaf Mini links Sweet Dough. Make a copy of it there, or remove it there, first."). Deleting a standalone one on the Sub-Assemblies screen is refused the same way while any product uses it.
- **Packaging sets: SubAssembly in the Packaging role + SubAssemblyPackagingLine** (2026-09-18, at the user's request; the columns "similar to the packaging in the Recipe" Packaging tab). A standalone sub-assembly whose role has `IsPackaging` holds packaging materials **per set** instead of ingredients in grams — a gift box with its ribbon and tag, or the 8 bags and the box that make up an "Ensaymada Box of 8".
  - **`SubAssemblyPackagingLine`** (`SubAssemblyId` Cascade, `PackagingMaterialId` Restrict to a `RawMaterial` of the Packaging type, `UnitId?` Restrict to a `RawMaterialUnit`, `QtyPerSet` > 0 in that unit; unique per set + material; `CK_SubAssemblyPackagingLines_QtyPerSet`). A table of its own, not `SubAssemblyLine`, which is ingredients in grams and feeds the cost cascade.
    - **`UnitId?`** (2026-09-20, at the user's request: "I have an idea to utilize the alternative units stored per packaging materials … I select that and it will be used"): the unit the quantity is entered in — one of the material's own alternative units, or its base unit when empty — exactly as `PackagingLine.UnitId` has always been. The quantity is stored as typed and converted at cost time, so 1 roll stays 1 roll rather than the 1,000 cm it comes to, and correcting the roll's multiplier reprices the line without rewriting it. `SubAssemblyRepository.SaveStandalonePackagingAsync` refuses a unit that isn't that material's own, with the same message `PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` uses, and `RawMaterialUnitRepository.DeleteAsync` counts set lines beside packaging lines before it lets a unit go. Migration `SetPackagingLineUnits` adds the column, nullable, so every existing line stays in its base unit with no data step.
  - **Costed on read**, like a product's packaging lines. A set has no ingredient lines, so the cost propagator never meets one.
  - **Standalone only.** A recipe section never takes the Packaging role (a product's own sub-assembly, until 2026-09-23) (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureRecipeRole`, in `SetRecipeAsync` for owned and linked sections alike; the Products form's role picker leaves it out), and `GetLinkableAsync` leaves sets out of the recipe link/copy picker.
  - **The kind is fixed once saved** (the user's decisions were a role for the marking; fixing it follows the raw material type rule): `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureKindUnchanged` refuses moving a saved recipe to the Packaging role or a saved set to a recipe role. `SaveStandaloneAsync` refuses the Packaging role; `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync` (one transaction: name and role through the shared `StageStandaloneAsync`, then `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidPackagingLines` — at least one line, positive quantities, no material twice — and `RawMaterialRules.EnsureUsableAsPackaging` per material, then the lines replaced) requires it.
  - **Used by a product's packaging, one whole set per sellable unit** (the user's choices: link the Packaging tab rather than add a recipe tab, and one whole set at a time; it read "per piece" until Quantity Required arrived on 2026-09-20, which is the same thing wherever a unit is one piece), linked or copied from the Products screen's Add Sub-Assembly. See "Link the packaging" under Derived products. A packaging material's base unit is fixed once a set uses it too (`RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync`'s `hasLines`).
  - **Deleting a linked set is refused**, naming the products (`DeleteStandaloneAsync`, the same wording as `EnsureNotLinkedElsewhere`), and `GetStandaloneLinkedByAsync` counts packaging links, so the screen's Used By names them.
- **Product** + **ProductSubAssemblyUsage**: a finished product is built from distinct sub-assemblies, its own and linked ones. It can use several in one role, e.g. an ube filling and a cheese filling, told apart by their names (one in a role can go without; see SubAssembly above; from 2026-09-17 to 2026-09-18 it had one in each role), and each sub-assembly only once. Since 2026-09-23 every usage is a link (until then, a usage whose sub-assembly's `OwnerProductId` wasn't the usage's `ProductId` was one).
  - **`Description`** (optional, at most 500 characters: `ProductValidator.MaxDescriptionLength`, which `ProductConfiguration`'s `HasMaxLength` also uses) and **`UpdatedAt`** (`DateTime?`, local time), both added 2026-09-14 for the Products screen's header. `UpdatedAt` is stamped by `ProductRepository`'s add and update, so by the screen's Save and by a clone when it's made. Products not saved since then have null, shown as "—".
  - **`CurrentPrice`** (`decimal?`, per sellable unit, VAT included; added 2026-09-19 at the user's request, per piece until Quantity Required on 2026-09-20): what the shop actually charges now, entered in the Products header beside the computed Selling Price and saved with the product (null until entered; `ProductValidator` refuses a negative one). A clone copies it. It prices nothing: the header shows the **Markup %** it earns, `CostingRules.CalculateMarkupPercentFromPrice` — VAT taken out first, then the markup on the base cost, `(CurrentPrice ÷ (1 + VAT%) − BaseCost) ÷ BaseCost` — the same definition as the costing's Margin, so the computed Selling Price gives back exactly the Margin % (the user's choice, over a markup on the material cost alone).
  - **Each usage has its own piece size, as total weight, yield and weight per piece** (2026-09-14, at the user's request). The user types either the **weight per piece** (grams) or the **yield** (pieces per batch), and the other is worked out from the batch's total weight. A usage stores only what was typed: **`AuthoredField`** (`UsageAuthoredField.WeightPerPiece` or `Yield`) and **`AuthoredValue`** (≥ 0, `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_AuthoredValue`). Until then it stored `WeightPerPiece` only; the migration carried every one over as a typed weight.
    - **Neither figure is required** (2026-09-20, at the user's request): a section goes into the recipe, and saves, before how much of it a piece takes is known, stored as `AuthoredValue` 0. `ProductRules.EnsureValidUsages` and the check constraint both take 0 and refuse only a negative (migration `OptionalUsagePieceSize`); `ResolvePieceSize` already had no other figure to work out from 0, so an unsized section has a batch but costs the product nothing per piece, and the form's caption says so. It was refused on Apply (`EnsurePieceSizeEntered`, now gone) and at the repository before that.
    - **The pieces share out the whole batch: no shrinkage** (2026-10-23, at the user's request: "Currently there is a shrinkage percentage for Products Subassemblies. I want to remove that and add a single wastage cost percentage to be added per piece"). What's lost making a product is now a cost on it, the shop-wide Wastage % (see Costing defaults below), not a smaller batch: `ResolvePieceSize` and `CalculateUsageCostPerPiece` divide the effective batch's total weight, `ProductRules.CalculateFinalWeight` went, and migration `ReplaceShrinkageWithWastage` dropped `shrinkage_percent` and its check constraint. A section that shrank keeps its typed figure, so the other moves by the old shrinkage: a typed 60 g piece of 1,700 g at 10% yielded 25.5 and now yields 28.33. Saved versions' sections still hold a `ShrinkagePercent` in their JSON, which `ProductVersionContent.FromJson` ignores, so their figures then are worked out without it. What it was, from 2026-09-22 (at the user's request: "Add Shrinkage % on top of the Weight per Piece (g) in the Recipe Structure. Final Weight (g) = Total Weight (g) * (1 - Shrinkage %)"): **`ShrinkagePercent`** was what the batch loses being made — a dough baked off, a filling reduced — at least 0 and under 100 (`CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_ShrinkagePercent`, migration `UsageShrinkagePercent`, which carried every existing usage over at 0). It belongs to the *usage*, not the sub-assembly: two products can bake the same dough to different weights. **`ProductRules.CalculateFinalWeight(totalWeight, shrinkagePercent)`** is the formula, the same one `RawMaterial.WeightLossPercent` uses one layer down (`IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost`). **The final weight is what the pieces are cut from** (the user's choice, over showing it and feeding nothing): the weight per piece is the *finished* piece, the yield is the final weight over it, and the batch keeps its whole cost, so shrinking a batch leaves fewer, dearer pieces. 1,700 g at 10% → 1,530 g; a typed 60 g piece yields 25.5, not 28.33.
    - **A section added in a product-owned role starts at a yield of 1** (2026-10-11, at the user's request: "For Sub-Assembly roles that can be owned by the product make the default Weight/pc equal to the total Grams weight"). Such a section is written for the one piece it goes into, so the add form opens with `AuthoredField` `Yield` and `AuthoredValue` 1 where any other role opens unsized (`ProductsViewModel.StartingPieceSize`), New and Copy alike; a Link stays unsized, its batch being the Sub-Assemblies screen's and shared. The user chose the yield over a typed weight: the weight per piece is then the total of the Grams column (the *final* weight, less the section's shrinkage, until 2026-10-23), the cost per piece is the batch cost, and since the yield is the figure stored, the weight per piece goes on following the lines after Save. It's the role's start, not an entry: while neither figure has been typed it follows the role picked (`IsPieceSizeAsStarted`, read by the `EditingUsageRole` subscription against the role before), and a form still at it counts as untouched (`IsUsageFormUntouched`); both are worked out from the form rather than remembered, so they hold for a form an undo put back. Only the add form starts this way; saved sections, unsized ones included, are left as they are. No migration.
  - **`ProductRules.ResolvePieceSize(authoredField, authoredValue, finalWeight)`** is the one formula: weight typed → `Yield = TotalWeight / WeightPerPiece`; yield typed → `WeightPerPiece = TotalWeight / Yield` (the final weight, less the shrinkage, until 2026-10-23). It's worked out on every read, never stored, which is what makes **the typed figure stay put when the batch changes**: a 60 g piece stays 60 g and the yield moves; a 24-piece yield stays 24 and each piece gets heavier or lighter. Being computed on read, an owner's line edit never has to update other products' rows. A batch that weighs nothing has no other figure (0). Every caller passes the effective batch's weight — `SectionFigures.Of` (for `ProductUsageEdit` and saved products) and `ProductUsageForm`'s `Recompute` and `FollowPieceSize` — so the rule itself stays one line.
  - Usage cost per piece = `TotalCost × WeightPerPiece / TotalWeight` (the effective batch's, worked out from its lines and add-ins), with the resolved weight per piece, which comes to the same thing as `TotalCost ÷ Yield`. For example, 100g/₱24 filling used at 10g/piece gives ₱2.40/piece, yield 10.
  - **Per-piece line breakdown** (2026-09-14): a piece takes `WeightPerPiece / TotalWeight` of the batch, so it takes that share of every line. `ProductRules.CalculateLineGramsPerPiece` scales a line's grams and `CalculateLineCostPerPiece` its cost (grams × buffered cost per gram), with the same share. A usage's lines add up to its cost per piece and its weight per piece. The Recipe Structure showed this per line until 2026-09-16, when a sub-assembly's tab became its form. **Both rules were removed on 2026-11-04**, at the user's request, from a code review: nothing had called them since then, the Costing tab's Cost Breakdown working off `ProductCostSummary`, a piece at a time, not line by line. They'd been kept, with their tests, as the costing rule for a line's share of a piece, should a per-line view be wanted back. **If one returns (from git history), it must divide by the usage's *effective* batch** (`ProductRules.CalculateEffectiveBatch`), not the sub-assembly's own, or a linked usage's add-ins would be left out of every line's share; each took that batch's weight as `totalWeight`.
  - **UsageAddInLine: a product's own extras on top of a sub-assembly it links** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "the first datagrid has headers containing the linked recipe and the bottom one contains some additional recipe like food colors, flavoring etc"). The same dough goes into several products, but one wants ube flavouring and another pandan colour; until then the only way was **Make a Copy**, which breaks the link, so the dough stopped being shared and the owner's changes stopped reaching it.
    - `UsageAddInLine` (`UsageId` Cascade, `IngredientId` Restrict, `Grams`; unique per usage + ingredient) hangs off the **usage**, not the sub-assembly: two products linking the same dough each keep their own set, and neither moves the dough's own batch, its lines, which stay the owner's. The only cascade path in is `Products → ProductSubAssemblyUsages → UsageAddInLines`, a chain rather than a second path, which SQL Server allows because a usage's own sub-assembly FK is Restrict.
    - **Add-ins go into the batch** (the user's choice, over "a fixed amount per piece"): the usage's **effective batch** is the sub-assembly's plus its add-ins (`ProductRules.CalculateEffectiveBatch`, over `SubAssemblyRules.CalculateTotals`), so 1,700 g of dough with 20 g of flavouring is a 1,720 g batch and a piece takes its weight-per-piece share of the whole thing. `ResolvePieceSize` and `CalculateUsageCostPerPiece` were **unchanged**: they just receive the effective figures. The typed figure still stays put, so adding a flavouring moves the other one.
    - **Linked usages only.** A new section (a sub-assembly of the product's own, until 2026-09-23) has no add-ins — its extras just go in its lines, and a second table there would be two ways to do one thing.
    - **An ingredient the linked recipe already has is refused** (`ProductRules.EnsureValidAddIns`, the user's choice over merging the grams): "Water is already in Variety Bread Dough. Add-ins are only for what its recipe doesn't have — change how much it uses where Variety Bread Dough is kept, or make this product a copy of it." Checked in the form the moment Apply is pressed, and again in `SetRecipeAsync`'s pre-flight and `UsageAddInLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` as it writes. An empty set is valid, unlike a sub-assembly's own lines: most linked usages take the recipe as it is.
      - That guard is load-bearing beyond validation: since an add-in can never duplicate a base ingredient, **Make a Copy (and `ProductCloner`'s owned branch, until Clone started linking on 2026-09-23) concatenate the base lines and the add-ins** into the new sub-assembly's lines without a merge step, and the result always passes `EnsureValidLines`. The batch it was showing is the batch it keeps. A clone of a *standalone* link stays linked, so its add-ins stay add-ins.
    - **Written with the rest of the recipe**: `LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns` (an `init` property, so the existing positional call sites are untouched). `SetRecipeAsync` adds the linked usages together, saves once so every one has the id its add-ins need, then writes each one's lines — the same "save for the id, then reline" shape the owned loop already uses. `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetAddInsForProductAsync` reads them back, on the interface Products and Reports already inject, so no ViewModel constructor changed.
    - **`ReportsViewModel.CalculateMarginRowsAsync` had to learn about them**: it was the only place that costed a product without going through `ProductUsageEdit`, reading a sub-assembly's stored totals as the usage's batch, so without `CalculateEffectiveBatch` the margin chart silently understated every product with add-ins. **Since 2026-09-27 it has no pricing of its own**: it prices each product through `ViewModels/SavedProductReader` (see the Products screen's Print), as the Products screen prices the product it opens, and `ReportsViewModelTests` kept their figures, which shows the two agree. (The chart became the Product Cost Summary the same day, `ReportsViewModel.ReadProductCostsAsync`.)
  - A product has **no yield of its own**. Each usage row has its own yield, and those will usually differ between a product's sub-assemblies. That's expected, not an error.
  - Product material cost per piece = the sum of all its usages' cost-per-piece, plus its packaging lines' cost-per-piece.
  - Sub-assembly and product weights are raw input weights, and baking/moisture loss is not modeled for them. Made ingredients are the only place weight loss is modeled (`WeightLossPercent`).
- **PackagingLine**: one packaging material used by one whole sellable unit of a product — its wrapper, its box, its label, its ribbon. "Unit" throughout this bullet means that sellable unit, which is one finished piece unless the product's `QuantityRequired` says otherwise.
  - FK to a `RawMaterial` of the Packaging type (`PackagingLineRepository` rejects an ingredient), so packaging price changes cascade like ingredient price changes.
  - **`UnitId?`** (`PackagingMaterialUnitId` before the raw materials merge): the unit the line's quantities are entered in — one of the material's own units, or its base unit when empty. Quantities are stored as entered, in that unit. (A length-only `EntryUnitId` existed until the length units were removed on 2026-09-13; this general unit replaced it the same day.)
  - **`PackId?` is gone** (2026-09-20, with `ProductPack`; see Quantity Required below). It pointed at the `ProductPack` a line packed, null meaning a per-piece line. Its FK had to be `Restrict` rather than `Cascade`, since `PackagingLine` already cascades from `Product` and `ProductPack` did too, and a second cascade path `Products -> PackagingLines` is something SQL Server refuses — so `ProductPackRepository.SetPacksAsync` and `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` deleted a pack's lines by hand, lines before packs. All of that went with it: `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` now just deletes the product and lets its cascades do the rest.
  - **`QtyPerUnit`** (`QtyPerPiece` until 2026-09-18): amount of the packaging material used by one sellable unit, in the line's unit (12.7 for 12.7 cm of ribbon, `0.1667` for a 6-piece box, 1 for a label). **Typed, and never rescaled**: changing a product's `QuantityRequired` from 8 to 12 doesn't touch it, so the packaging is revisited by hand. The Packaging tab's caption says which unit the figures are for (`ProductsViewModel.CostUnitCaption`), so that stays visible rather than silent.
  - **`UnitsPerPackagingUnit`** (`PiecesPerPackagingUnit` until 2026-09-18): sellable units served by one of the line's unit of the packaging material (6 for that box). **Both were renamed when `PackId` arrived** (2026-09-18), since "piece" stopped being true for a pack's line; the names outlived packs, because "unit" is now the sellable unit. The columns were renamed by a generated migration (`PackagingLinesServeUnits`) with no data loss.
  - **`AuthoredField`**: records which of the two the user typed. The other is recalculated as its reciprocal on every edit. Ribbon is naturally entered as `QtyPerUnit`, a box as `UnitsPerPackagingUnit`. Its members are stored as ints, so they may be renamed but **never reordered**.
  - The unique index is **`{ProductId, PackagingMaterialId}`**: one line per material per product, a material a unit takes twice over belonging on a single line at the combined quantity. It was `{ProductId, PackId, PackagingMaterialId}` with `HasFilter(null)` while packs existed — the filter being required, since EF's SQL Server convention would otherwise have added `[PackId] IS NOT NULL` and dropped every per-piece line out of the index (the same trap `SubAssemblyConfiguration` documents). `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks` put the two-column index back.
  - Cost per unit = `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit × QtyPerUnit` converted to base units (`ProductRules.CalculatePackagingLineCost(costPerBaseUnit, qtyPerUnit, baseUnitsPerEntryUnit?)`, passed the buffered cost), computed on read. It is a cost per *sellable unit*, so `CalculateMaterialCost` adds it once however many pieces that unit holds.
  - Example: a ribbon costs ₱45.00 per roll. Its base unit is cm and a roll holds 1000 cm, so it costs ₱0.045 per cm. One product uses 12.7 cm per piece (₱0.5715); another is entered as 0.08 m per piece (8 cm → ₱0.36).
  - Storing both directions differs from `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` on purpose. People describe packaging quantities both ways, but they describe recipe weights only one way.
- **Quantity Required** (`Product.QuantityRequired`, 2026-09-20, at the user's request: "first let's have the product a quantity required. then we can set the necessary packaging in the subassembly"): how many finished pieces make one sellable unit. 1 for a product sold by the piece, which is nearly all of them; 8 for "Ensaymada Box of 8". `int`, defaulting to 1 in the CLR and in the database, `CK_Products_QuantityRequired` keeping it positive, and `ProductValidator` refusing anything below 1 on the screen.
  - **The one rule it establishes: the recipe is authored per piece, costing and pricing are per sellable unit, and this is the bridge.** `ProductRules.CalculateMaterialCost(usageCostsPerPiece, quantityRequired, packagingCosts)` = `usageCostsPerPiece.Sum() × quantityRequired + packagingCosts.Sum()` — only the usages are multiplied, since a packaging line is already for one whole unit. It replaced `CalculateMaterialCostPerPiece` and `CalculatePackMaterialCost`, whose bodies it merges. At a quantity of 1 it is exactly the old per-piece figure, so nothing that existed changed value.
  - **`ProductConfiguration` sets `HasDefaultValue(1).HasSentinel(-1)`.** The database default is what let the migration add the column to existing rows without failing the check constraint. The sentinel is what moves "leave it to the database" off 0, which EF would otherwise take it to be: without it a product explicitly saved as 0 pieces would be quietly stored as 1 on an insert while being refused on an update.
  - **It replaced `ProductPack`** (2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20), which was one sellable pack of a product — "Pack of 8", "Pack of 12" — with its own `PiecesPerPack` and its own packaging lines, shown on a **Packs** tab. Create Variant (2026-09-19) made it redundant, and a pack had turned out to be a second-class SKU: it never reached Reports (`ReportsViewModel` costs per piece, and `PackagingLineRepository` filtered `PackId IS NULL`), it had no `CurrentPrice`, Markup %, category, description or `UpdatedAt`, it had no row in the Products list, it was never linkable so two products sharing a box format each kept their own copy, and it cost the schema the `PackId` complications above. **A pack is now a product**: a variant of the single, with a Quantity Required and a packaging set of its own.
  - **The worked example**, which `ProductQuantityRequiredPersistenceTests` pins: Ensaymada takes 10 g of a 1,000 g / ₱50 dough, so ₱0.50 of recipe a piece. "Ensaymada Box of 8" is **Create Variant** of it — the recipe linked, so still 10 g a piece — with Quantity Required 8 and its packaging linked to a standalone **packaging set** holding the 8 bags (`QtyPerSet` 8) and the one box (`QtyPerSet` 1). Its material cost is `8 × ₱0.50 + 8 × ₱0.25 + ₱12 = ₱18`.
  - **The per-piece wrapper is written down twice**, which was the user's choice (2026-09-20) over giving a product a second packaging slot. A product's packaging is its own lines **or** one `PackagingLink`, never both (`CK_PackagingLinks_OneTarget`), so a boxed variant can't read the single's wrapper *and* link a box set; the set carries both instead. Prices still cascade on their own — both point at the same `RawMaterial` — and only the composition is duplicated. The other consequence of that choice was that `SubAssemblyPackagingLine` had no unit column, so a ribbon a product entered in metres had to go into a set in cm; `UnitId` (2026-09-20) put that right, and the two now hold a quantity the same way.
  - **Pricing is linear in the sub-assembly cost** (labor/overhead/administrative are percentages of it, packaging passes straight through, margin and VAT are multiplicative), so **a unit of 8 with no packaging of its own prices at exactly 8 times the single's price** — its own packaging is the whole of the difference. That's pinned by a test in `CostingRulesTests`, as it was for packs.
  - **The Scaling tab counts sellable units too** (2026-09-30): N units scale the recipe to N × Quantity Required pieces and the packaging to N units. See the Products screen's tabs.
  - **Every figure on the Products screen is per sellable unit**: `ProductHeaderCard`'s Material Cost, Base Cost, Current Price and Markup %, and the whole of `CostingCard`'s breakdown. `ProductsViewModel.CostUnitCaption` ("Per piece", or "Per unit of 8 pieces") is bound into their tooltips, the breakdown's column header and the Packaging tab's caption, so which unit is never in doubt. The recipe tabs stay per piece: a usage's weight per piece, yield and cost per piece are untouched by this.
  - **Typed in on `ProductDetailsCard`**, beside Current Price (a Syncfusion `IntegerTextBox`, `MinValue` 1), since that card is where the product's own typed fields live and the header holds only read-only figures. Undo covers it (`ProductFormSnapshot.QuantityRequired`), a clone and a variant both copy it, and Reports' Product Cost Summary is per unit too (through `SavedProductReader`), with a Pieces column saying how many.
  - Gone with the Packs tab: `ProductPack`, `IProductPackRepository`/`ProductPackRepository`, `ProductPackValidator`, `ProductPackRow`, `PacksCard`, `PackCostSummary`, `ProductRules.EnsureValidPacks` and `CalculatePackMaterialCost`, the `packs` parameter of `IProductRepository.SaveAsync`, and `PackState` in the form snapshot. `ProductsViewModel.LinksTab` went back from 3 to 2.
  - **Migration `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks`**, which has **one hand-added statement** — the second in the project, after `DropLengthUnitsAndPackagingLineEntryUnit` — `DELETE FROM PackagingLines WHERE PackId IS NOT NULL;` before the column is dropped. Without it a pack's own lines would be left behind as ordinary packaging lines of their product (a box charged on every sellable unit), and a material that wrapped the piece and packed a pack, or two packs sharing one, would break the two-column unique index as it is created. The user's call was that the few packs that existed are re-entered as variants rather than converted.
- **Composite products** (`Product.IsComposite`, 2026-09-29, at the user's request: "A composite product is a product composed of two or more products"): a product made of other products and its own packaging, such as a Pasalubong Box of 6 Ensaymada and 4 Cheese Rolls. The user took the recommended option on each of four questions:
  - **A kind of product holding products and packaging only, fixed once saved** (over letting any product take in whole products beside its own sub-assemblies).
    - It's picked with New ▸ New composite product, or New Composite Product (Here) in the list.
    - `CompositeProductRules.EnsureKindUnchanged`, in `ProductRepository.UpdateAsync`, refuses a change, as `RawMaterialRules.EnsureTypeUnchanged` does for a raw material.
    - `ProductComponentRepository.SetComponentsAsync` refuses products for a product of sub-assemblies.
    - **Its own sections are only in product-owned roles** (2026-09-29, the user's follow-up: "Besides packaging a composite can have another subassembly such as topping, filling… we can reuse the Role settings"). The user chose reusing `SubAssemblyRole.IsProductOwned` (over a second per-role checkbox, and over any role), and refusing to untick it while a composite uses the role (over letting existing sections stay).
      - `CompositeProductRules.EnsureSectionRoles`, run by `SaveAsync` through `ProductComponentRepository.EnsureSectionRolesAsync`, refuses a section, new or linked, in any other role, naming it.
      - `SubAssemblyRoleRepository.UpdateAsync` refuses unticking Product-owned while a composite has a section in the role, and `SubAssemblyRepository`'s standalone save refuses moving a sub-assembly a composite links into a role that isn't product-owned. Both name the composites (`CompositesUsingRoleAsync`).
      - A composite is one unit, so a section's piece size is per whole composite, and its cost per piece joins the products' lines in the recipe cost. A composite held in another brings that whole recipe cost, sections included.
      - Clone and Create Variant copy or link its sections as any product's (`DerivedRecipe`). Raw Materials' Used By needed nothing new: `SectionUsers` already takes any product's sections, and `RecipeUsers` climbs from there.
    - It's always **one sellable unit**, `QuantityRequired` 1. `CompositeProductRules.EnsureOneUnit` (in the static Add and Update), `ProductValidator` and `CK_Products_CompositeIsOneUnit` all enforce it.
  - **Each product brings its recipe cost, as a Box of 8 does** (over bringing its recipe and its packaging, and over its full base cost with only the composite's markup and VAT on top).
    - A line brings the product's Sub-assemblies (ingredients) figure for one sellable unit, × the line's quantity (`CompositeProductRules.CalculateComponentCost`).
    - Its packaging stays out, so the composite's packaging covers the box and anything wrapping the pieces. This is the same choice as the per-piece wrapper written into a Box of 8's set (2026-09-20).
    - The composite's own labor, overhead, admin, markup and VAT apply to that recipe cost as for any product. So `ProductCostSummary` takes the lines, then its sections, as its `ProductCostInputs.Parts` at a quantity of 1, and every formula is unchanged; `IsComposite` and `HasOwnSections` only rename the rows (`PartsTitle` "Products (ingredients)", or "Products and sub-assemblies (ingredients)" with sections; `PartsBase` "products", or "products and sub-assemblies").
    - Pricing stays linear: products at the same percentages and a composite with no packaging price at exactly the sum of their prices. `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests` pins it: 2.50 → 5.096 and 5.00 → 10.192 make 12.50 → 25.48.
  - **Include each product's packaging** (`Product.IncludesComponentPackaging`, 2026-09-30, at the user's request: "a checkbox option … to include individual product packaging when the Product is composite"). Off by default, so the rule above holds unless it's ticked, for pieces that go into the box already wrapped.
    - **It joins the composite's packaging, not its recipe cost** (the user's choice, over folding it into each line's Recipe Cost): labor, overhead and administrative, percentages of the recipe alone, don't go on it, as for any packaging. So products at the same percentages still price at exactly the sum of their prices with their packaging in, which `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests` pins.
    - A line brings its product's Packaging figure for one sellable unit × its quantity (`CompositeProductRules.CalculateComponentPackagingCost`). A composite held in another brings all of its Packaging figure, its own products' included when it has the box ticked, so it nests the way the recipe cost does.
    - `SavedProductReader.ReadComponentCostsAsync` (was `ReadRecipeCostsAsync`) returns `ComponentCosts` (was `RecipeCosts`): recipe and packaging, buffered and unbuffered, from the one read. `ProductCostSummary` takes it as `ProductCostInputs.ComponentPackaging` (null when not ticked), keeps `OwnPackaging` apart for the Packaging tab's caption, and names the row `PackagingTitle`, "Packaging (own and products')" when ticked, on screen and in print.
    - Only a composite can have it: `CompositeProductRules.EnsureComponentPackagingOnComposite` in the static Add and Update, and `CK_Products_ComponentPackagingOnComposite`. Clone and Create Variant copy it. Migration `CompositeComponentPackaging` is additive (false for every product), applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup to the instance's Backup folder (`TinapayanRMS_before_CompositeComponentPackaging_20260930.bak`).
    - Raw Materials' Used By climbs for it: `PackagingCosts` in `UsedBySql` goes from every product whose packaging cost the material is in to each composite with the box ticked holding it, and on up. It's a CTE of its own because a packaging link takes only the linked product's lines, so `PackagingUsers` mustn't climb through composites.
  - **Composites nest, with a loop check** (over single products only).
    - `CompositeProductRules.EnsureNoCycle`/`Contains` check every other composite's lines, in `SetComponentsAsync`. The message names both: "Party Package can't go into Pasalubong Box: it already contains Pasalubong Box, so …".
    - The picker leaves out the composite and every composite holding it.
    - `SavedProductReader` guards against a loop that data changed outside the app could still hold.
  - **At least two different products** (`CompositeProductRules.MinimumProducts`; over no minimum): six of one product is a Box of 6. A new composite saved with a null recipe (the form's unchanged-recipe shortcut sends none) counts as holding none, so the rule can't be skipped. The public `AddAsync` refuses a composite: only `SaveAsync` writes one with its products.
  - **Quantity**: a whole number, 1 or more, counting the component's sellable units. 1 of "Ensaymada Box of 8" is the whole box and brings 8 pieces' recipe. Every variant is a product of its own, so each is a line of its own.
  - **Storage**: `ProductComponent`.
    - `ProductId` cascades. `ComponentProductId` is Restrict: a product in a composite can't be deleted, and `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` names the composites before the key would refuse: "Pasalubong Box holds this product. Take it out there first."
    - `Quantity` is an int above 0, and `SortOrder` records the dragged order. It's unique per composite and product, with `CK_ProductComponents_Quantity` and `CK_ProductComponents_NotItself`.
    - Migration `CompositeProducts` is additive (`IsComposite` defaults to false). It was applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup to the instance's own Backup folder (`C:\Program Files\Microsoft SQL Server\MSSQL17.SQLEXPRESS\MSSQL\Backup\TinapayanRMS_before_CompositeProducts_20260929.bak`), since the SQL Express service account can't write to the user's profile, where the LocalDB backups went.
  - **Written as the recipe**: `RecipeDraft.Components` is an init property, the `LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns` precedent, so `IProductRepository.SaveAsync`'s call sites didn't change.
    - `ProductComponentRepository` is static, like `UsageAddInLineRepository`. Its reads are `IProductRepository.GetComponentsAsync` and `GetAllComponentsAsync`, so no ViewModel constructor changed.
    - `SavedProductReader` gained `IProductRepository`, which `ProductsViewModel` passes from its own.
  - **Worked out on read, nothing stored**: `SavedProductReader.ReadDetailAsync` reads a composite's lines and prices each product through itself, recursively (`ProductDetail.Components`, `SavedComponent`). So a composite's line always matches its product's own Costing tab, in Reports and Print as on the screen. `ReadRecipeCostsAsync(ids)` serves the screen.
  - **Clone and Create Variant** give the copy the same products at the same quantities (`SetComponentsAsync` in place of `SetRecipeAsync`). Create Variant still links the packaging.
  - **Raw Materials' Used By** climbs `ProductComponents` for an ingredient, however deep. For packaging it climbs only into composites that include their products' packaging (2026-09-30, above).
  - Tests:
    - ApplicationCore: `CompositeProductRulesTests` and `ProductValidatorTests`.
    - Infrastructure: `CompositeProductPersistenceTests` and one case in `RawMaterialUsedByPersistenceTests`.
    - UI: `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests`, `ProductsViewModelCompositeTests`, one case in `PrintTabViewModelTests` (a saved composite reads as the screen shows it), two in `ProductPrintLayoutTests`, and the tree-menu tests.
- **Line order: every line table stores each line's position** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly Lines, Product Recipe Lines, Packaging Lines, and BOM should allow reordering of rows through dragging"). `SortOrder` (an `int`, from 0) is on `SubAssemblyLine`, `SubAssemblyPackagingLine`, `IngredientBomLine`, `PackagingLine` and `UsageAddInLine`, the five tables whose lines the user types in and now drags into order (see Application Architecture: rows are dragged into order).
  - **Written from the list's order, read in it.** Every `SetLinesAsync` (and `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync`) already deletes a set's lines and inserts them again, so each line's `SortOrder` is simply its index in the list it's handed; no signature changed. Every read that returns lines in order (`SubAssemblyLineRepository.GetForSubAssembliesAsync`, `IngredientBomLineRepository.GetForIngredientAsync`, `RawMaterialReads.GetPackagingLinesAsync`/`GetSetLinesAsync`, `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetAddInsForProductAsync`, `ProductCloner`'s add-ins) orders by `SortOrder, Id`.
  - **Why a column, when the lines were already read `ORDER BY l.Id` and inserted in list order:** EF Core inserts several rows into an identity table with one `MERGE … OUTPUT`, and SQL Server doesn't promise to hand out the identities in the `VALUES` order, so Id order was only the insert order by habit. A dragged order is the user's, and has to survive a save for certain.
  - **Migration `LineSortOrder`** adds the five columns at 0 with no data statement: `ORDER BY SortOrder, Id` reads a set that's all 0s in Id order, which is the order its lines were shown in before, and the next save of that set numbers them. Pinned by `LineOrderPersistenceTests` (a reorder round-trips on each table; the stored order beats the Id order; all-0 rows keep their Id order).
- **CostingParameters**: labor, overhead, administrative (office, permits, bookkeeping; added 2026-09-14, at least 0, default 0), margin %, VAT. **Since 2026-09-15 labor, overhead and administrative are percentages** (`LaborPercent`, `OverheadPercent`, `AdministrativePercent`, at the user's request), not pesos per piece. **Since 2026-09-20 the base they're percentages of is the sub-assembly (ingredient) cost per piece alone, packaging left out** (`CostingRules.CalculateCostFromSubAssemblyPercent`, at the user's request: "Labor Cost is Sub-assemblies (ingredients) * Labor Cost %"): 25% labor on ₱20 of sub-assemblies is ₱5, and packaging still goes into the base cost, just not into these three — what they pay for is making the recipe, not wrapping it. They were percentages of the whole material cost (sub-assemblies **plus** packaging, `CalculateCostFromMaterialPercent`) from 2026-09-15 to then, and `LaborCost`, `OverheadCost` and `AdministrativeCost` in pesos before that. **The stored percentages are untouched by the 2026-09-20 change — no migration, no schema change** — but every product with packaging reprices: its labor, overhead and administrative costs, and so its base cost and selling price, all go down. **Always independent per product**, even for derived/linked products.
  - **The VAT is the shop's** (2026-11-05, the user's choice after an independent code review): `CostingDefaults.VatPercent`, read live with every product as the wastage is (`ProductDetail.VatPercent`), where until then each product kept its own copy in `CostingParameters` (all 69 at 12% in the dev data; the column went in migration `ShopVat`, and `CK_CostingDefaults_VatPercent` took over its check). The Costing tab shows it greyed; Settings changes it, repricing every product. Version snapshots keep the shop's VAT they were priced with (schema 3; older ones kept the product's in their costing, `ProductVersionContent.VatThen`), and the History tab lists a change in it, or in the wastage, after the product's own changes (`ProductVersionRules.ShopChanges`), never counted as one. **Apply Costing Defaults** on the Products list's menu, for the products picked (2026-11-05, the same review), sets their labor, overhead, administrative and markup percentages to the defaults once it's confirmed, in one unit of work with a version of each one it changes, noted "Costing defaults applied" (`VersionedSaves.ApplyCostingAsync`); the product on the form has its unsaved changes saved or thrown away first, and shows its new costing after.
  - **Costing defaults** (`CostingDefaults`, 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "set the default value of Labor %, Overhead %, Administrative %, Markup %, and VAT % … at the Settings"): the five percentages a **new product's** costing starts with (four since VAT became the shop's, 2026-11-05), edited on Settings' **Costing Defaults** tab (`CostingDefaultsView`/`CostingDefaultsViewModel`, checked by `CostingParametersValidator`, and `CostingRules.EnsureValidParameters` in `CostingDefaultsRepository.SetAsync`). A table of one row (`Id` 1, never an identity, `CK_CostingDefaults_SingleRow`; migration `CostingDefaults`), which reads as all zero until first saved. `ProductsViewModel` reads them with every `LoadCommand` (Settings closing reloads the screen on show) and `StartNewProduct` puts them in the Costing fields while what Save compares against stays at none, so the product's first Save writes them as its own `CostingParameters` (all-zero defaults write nothing, as before). A new product still showing the defaults it started with takes up changed ones on the next load; one whose costing was typed into keeps it. **They never reach a saved product**, not even one without a `CostingParameters` row (it still shows zeros), nor a clone or a variant, which copy their source's.
    - **And the shop-wide wastage** (2026-10-23, at the user's request: "remove [the shrinkage] and add a single wastage cost percentage to be added per piece", with a note recommending one company-wide percentage applied to material cost once, never again at each level of a composite). `CostingDefaults.WastagePercent`, a **Wastage %** box under VAT % on the same tab, in place of Shrinkage %, from 0 to 100 (`CostingRules.EnsureValidWastage` in `SetAsync`, `CK_CostingDefaults_WastagePercent`); with no row it reads as `CostingDefaults.DefaultWastagePercent`, 3%. The user's choices: **one figure for the whole shop**, not a per-product percentage started from it; a **cost allowance** added on top (₱100 × 3% = ₱3), not a physical loss (₱100 ÷ 0.97); a percentage of the **sub-assembly (ingredient) cost per piece alone**, as labor, overhead and administrative are, packaging left out; and **labor, overhead and administrative stay on the ingredients before it**. So `BaseCost = SubAssemblies + Wastage + Packaging + Labor + Overhead + Administrative` (`CostingRules.CalculateSellingPrice`'s `wastagePercent`, last and optional; `ProductCostSummary.Wastage`/`WastagePercent`/`WastageShare`).
      - **Unlike the other defaults it isn't a starting value: every product is priced with it, saved ones included**, so changing it reprices them all. `SavedProductReader` takes `ICostingDefaultsRepository` and stamps `ProductDetail.WastagePercent` on every detail `ReadDetailsAsync` reads (one read a call), which `Build` passes on, so Reports, Print, the Price Simulator, the Production Plan and composites all have it; the Products screen keeps it from each `LoadCommand` (`_wastagePercent`, `RefreshCostingDefaults`, which reprices the open product when it changed) for the live Costing tab. One migration (`ReplaceShrinkageWithWastage`), with `HasDefaultValue(3m)` and `HasSentinel(-1m)` as `CostBufferPercent` has: the row already saved got 3%, and 0 is written as typed.
      - **Added once, never at each level of a composite**: a composite's products bring their recipe cost before their wastage (`ComponentCosts` holds `SubAssemblies`), and the composite adds it on all of it, so a composite of products at their own percentages is still priced at exactly what they are together.
      - **Shown** as a **Wastage (3% of sub-assemblies)** row under the sub-assemblies on the Costing tab's breakdown (read-only: Settings owns it), the same line on a printout's costing and the Price Simulator report's, and a **Wastage Cost** column on Reports. The Price Simulator's Raw Material Cost (`ProductCostSummary.Material`) leaves it out, as it leaves labor out.
      - **A version keeps the wastage it was priced with** (`ProductVersionContent.WastagePercent`, null for a version saved before, which prices with none), and its today figures take today's (`ProductVersionHistory.WastageNow`, from the product as it's saved now). It's the shop's, like a material's price, so it isn't part of a version's recipe (`IsSameRecipe`).
      - Tests: `CostingRulesTests`, `CostingDefaultsPersistenceTests`, `CostingDefaultsViewModelTests`, `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.The_pieces_share_out_the_whole_batch_and_the_wastage_goes_on_the_costing` and `A_wastage_changed_in_Settings_reprices_the_open_product_on_the_next_load`, `ReportsViewModelTests.The_product_cost_summary_breaks_the_base_cost_into_its_parts`, `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests.A_composite_at_its_products_percentages_prices_at_exactly_what_they_do`, `ProductsViewModelVersionTests.A_version_keeps_the_wastage_it_was_priced_with_and_today_takes_the_wastage_now`. Tests whose figures are about something else call `PersistenceTestBase.WithoutWastageAsync()` (or `SetWastageAsync`) first.
    - **Until 2026-10-23, the shrinkage a new section started with** (2026-10-06, at the user's request: "Add shrinkage % to settings, set the default value to 3%"), replaced by the wastage above. `CostingDefaults.ShrinkagePercent`, a **Shrinkage %** box under VAT % on the same tab, held to a section's own range (`ProductRules.IsValidShrinkage`, `EnsureValidDefaultShrinkage` in `SetAsync`, `CK_CostingDefaults_ShrinkagePercent`). With no row it reads as `CostingDefaults.DefaultShrinkagePercent`, 3%, the property's initializer. `ProductsViewModel` keeps it from each load and `StartUsageFormMode` starts the add form's Shrinkage % at it for New, Link and Copy alike, since the shrinkage is the product's. The form is still untouched at that value (`_usageFormStartShrinkagePercent`), so it isn't applied on Save or on leaving the tab. Saved sections keep their own.
      - **Two migrations, so the existing row got 3% and no store default stays.** `CostingDefaultsShrinkagePercent` added the column with `HasDefaultValue(3m)`, which filled the row already saved; `CostingDefaultsShrinkageNoStoreDefault` dropped the default once `HasDefaultValue` was removed. A lasting store default would make EF leave out a `ShrinkagePercent` of 0 (the CLR default) on the first insert, and the row would read 3%.
    - **And the stage New files a product in** (2026-10-07, at the user's request: "add a settings for where the new products will be placed (Production or Research and Development) set the default to Production"). `CostingDefaults.NewProductStage`, a **New Products In** picker (`ComboBoxAdv` over `CostingDefaultsViewModel.Stages`, each stage with its folder title, `SelectedValuePath="Key"`) first on the same tab, saved by the same Save. With no row it reads as `CostingDefaults.DefaultNewProductStage`, Production. Stored by name as `Product.Stage` is, with one migration (`CostingDefaultsNewProductStage`): the column's database default, Production, filled the row already saved, and `HasSentinel((ProductStage)(-1))` keeps it, since EF then always writes the stage picked, Research and Development (the CLR default) included. It's only New's (and New Composite Product's, from the empty space) stage: New Product Here keeps its folder's, and a clone or a variant still starts in Research and Development. Tests: `CostingDefaultsPersistenceTests`, `CostingDefaultsViewModelTests`, `ProductsViewModelTreeTests.New_files_a_product_in_the_stage_Settings_gives_a_new_one_and_follows_it_when_it_changes`.
    - **And the cost buffer a new raw material starts with** (2026-10-07, at the user's request: "Add settings for Raw Materials Cost Buffer %, default it to 10%"). `CostingDefaults.CostBufferPercent`, a **Cost Buffer %** box last on the same tab, held to a material's own range, 0 to 100 (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureValidCostBuffer` in `SetAsync`, `CK_CostingDefaults_CostBufferPercent`). With no row it reads as `CostingDefaults.DefaultCostBufferPercent`, 10%. **One migration** (`CostingDefaultsCostBufferPercent`), not the shrinkage's two: the store default of 10 filled the row already saved, and `HasSentinel(-1m)` keeps it harmless, since EF then always writes the buffer, 0 included. `RawMaterialsViewModel` takes `ICostingDefaultsRepository`, reads the default with every `LoadCommand`, and `ClearForm` (New, New … Here, after Delete) starts `EditingCostBufferPercent` at it; the field's initializer is the constant, for the form before the first load. A new material still showing the default it started with takes a changed one on the next load, with `History.Rebase` so it stays clean (`RefreshDefaultCostBuffer`); one whose buffer was typed keeps it. Only a purchased material keeps it: a linked one inherits and a made one stores 0 (`StoredCostBufferPercent`), as before. Saved materials are untouched. Tests: `CostingDefaultsPersistenceTests`, `CostingDefaultsViewModelTests`, `RawMaterialsViewModelRefreshTests.A_new_material_starts_at_Settings_cost_buffer_and_an_untouched_one_follows_it_when_it_changes`.
