- **ProductVersion: a product's version history** (2026-10-16, at the user's request: "is it possible to implement version control on products?", then "let's go with the snapshots"). The user's choices: a version on **every Save that changes the recipe or price** (not only on demand), each version's cost shown **as it was then and at today's prices**, and **view and compare, Restore and Notes**.
  - **Table `ProductVersions`**: `Id`, `ProductId` (Cascade: a product deleted for good takes its history; the Deleted folder keeps it), `Number` (1, 2, 3 … per product, unique with `ProductId`, `CK_ProductVersions_Number` ≥ 1), `SavedAt`, `Note` (optional, at most `ProductVersionRules.MaxNoteLength`, trimmed, blank stored as null), and `Content`, the snapshot as JSON (`nvarchar(max)`).
  - **A snapshot is self-contained** (`ApplicationCore/Interfaces/ProductVersionContent.cs`, serialized by `ProductVersionContent.ToJson`/`FromJson`, with a `Schema` number for later changes of shape): the product's quantity required, current price, whether a composite includes its products' packaging, its five costing percentages, each section (its sub-assembly's id and name, whether it's the product's own, its role, piece size, lines and add-ins; a `ShrinkagePercent` too until 2026-10-23), the shop's wastage then (`WastagePercent`), its packaging link and the lines it showed (resolved through the link, so a link changed later doesn't change the version), a composite's products with what each brought then, and **every material it uses with its costs then** (both bases, base unit and alternative units). It points at nothing, so a deleted material, sub-assembly or role leaves old versions readable. The name, description, category and stage are kept for display but aren't part of a version's recipe.
  - **Taken after Save, from what was saved**: the Products screen's Save reloads the product's `ProductDetail` (`SavedProductReader`), and `ProductVersionSnapshot.From` turns the product and that detail into the content, which `IProductVersionRepository.AddAsync` writes. Not in Save's transaction, since a product is only priced in the UI layer (`SavedProductReader.Build`); a failed write shows its message, and the next Save records the version anyway, since its recipe still differs from the latest.
  - **Only a change of recipe or price makes a version** (`ProductVersionRules.IsSameRecipe`, checked by `AddAsync` against the latest, inside its own transaction): its sections, lines, add-ins, piece sizes (and shrinkage, until 2026-10-23), packaging, products, costing percentages, quantity required and current price. Prices of materials don't count (that's what the today column is for), nor do the name, description or category, so a rename records nothing. A linked sub-assembly changed on the Sub-Assemblies screen shows up at the product's next Save, as a change of its recipe.
  - **The first version is the product as it was before**: a Save of a saved product with no versions yet first records it as it stood (`Note` "Before version history began", `SavedAt` its `UpdatedAt`), priced at the costs of that moment, so there's something to go back to.
  - **Figures, then and today, are worked out on read, by the code the screen prices with**: `ProductVersionSnapshot.ToDetail` rebuilds a `ProductDetail` from the content, and `SavedProductReader.Build` prices it. Then: its materials at their costs then, and its products at what they brought then. Today: the materials as they are now in their place (`Build`'s `materialsById`, the Price Simulator's way in), and each product at its recipe cost now (`ReadComponentCostsAsync`); a material deleted since keeps its cost then. Both at the screen's cost basis (buffered, or unbuffered when switched).
  - **The History tab** (the Products screen's details tab after Clone, `ProductsViewModel.HistoryTab`; a saved product only): a table of versions, newest first (Version, Saved, Note, Base Cost and Suggested Price then and today, Current Price), the note typed into the table and saved as it's left (`IProductVersionRepository.SetNoteAsync`); under it the picked version's **changes**, against the version before it or against the product as it's saved now (`ProductVersionRules.Changes`, lines such as "Dough: Bread Flour 500 g → 450 g", "Packaging: Paper Bag added, 1 pc", "Margin %: 30 → 35"); and **Restore**. Read when the tab is shown and after every Save, not with each product picked.
  - **Restore loads a version into the form as unsaved edits**, in one undo step (`RestoreSnapshot`), so Save keeps it and Undo takes it back. It puts back the sections, packaging, products, costing percentages, quantity required, current price and whether a composite includes its products' packaging; the name, description, category and stage stay as they are. Section by section:
    - **The product's own** keeps its sub-assembly's id when the product still owns it (rewritten in place by Save); otherwise it comes back as a new section of the product's own.
    - **A linked one is linked again as it is now**: Save never writes a version's lines into a sub-assembly other products may share. When its lines have changed since, the message says so.
    - **A sub-assembly deleted since** comes back as a new section with the version's lines, which Save treats as any new section in its role.
    - **Packaging**: the link, when what it linked still exists; otherwise the lines it showed, as the product's own. A line in an alternative unit deleted since is put in base units.
    - **Refused, naming them**, while a material, role or product it needs no longer exists: nothing is changed.
    - **Print Version, and Print while the tab shows, prints the version picked** (2026-10-17, at the user's request: "is there a way to preview or show the recipe at the history i currently selected?", option 1 of two: the other was a read-only recipe panel in the tab). As Print on the Scaling tab prints the scaled recipe, `PreparePrintout` returns `VersionPrintout`: the version priced at its costs then and the screen's cost basis (`SavedProductReader.Build` … `ToPrintout`), titled "Almond Cheese Bread (Version 2)", dated with its save, its footer note `ProductPrintout.VersionNote`, its category and stage the product's now. The form isn't applied first, and isn't changed. With no version picked, Print says so and the Print tab doesn't open. Checked in the app: version 2's 16 g of yeast on the page where the product now has 15 g.
    - **The section tab showing opens its form afterwards**, as showing it would, and what Save measures against moves onto that form (`RebaseSettledForm`): only the recipe, packaging, costing and price put back count as changes.
  - **Checked in the app on the secondary monitor (1366 × 768 at 125%) on 2026-10-17**, which found three things the tests hadn't: the table beside the changes left its figure columns scrolled out of sight (so the changes went under the table, and the headings wrap onto two lines); a restore closed the section form and left its tab showing an empty one (no lines, a 0 g batch), and the closed form alone lit Save for a version the same as the saved product; and a Save made from another tab left History without the new version, since only a showing tab was read again (now the versions it holds are cleared, so showing it reads them).
  - **A product picked while History shows reads its versions** (2026-10-17, reported by the user: going to another product and back left the tab empty). Every product picked on the tab showed none: `LoadProductDetailAsync` set `AreDetailsReady`, which the tab's read watches, before `_detailProductId`, so `RequestVersions` still saw the previous product's details and read nothing, and nothing asked again. The id is now set first. Test: `ProductsViewModelVersionTests.Picking_another_product_while_History_shows_reads_its_versions_and_the_first_ones_again_on_the_way_back`. Checked in the app: Almond Cheese Bread (4 versions), Monggo Bread (2), and back to Almond Cheese Bread (4), the tab showing throughout.
  - **Four improvements** (2026-10-17, at the user's request: "can our products version control be improve?", then "I like all four of what you have suggested"):
    - **A save elsewhere records the products it changes** (`UI/ViewModels/ProductVersionRecorder.cs`). Before, only a product's own Save recorded its version, so a shared sub-assembly changed on Sub-Assemblies reached its products' histories only at their next Save, under that Save's date and mixed into its changes, or never. Now Sub-Assemblies' Save, and a Products Save (shared lines edited, packaging others link), snapshot every product reading through what's saved (`CaptureAsync`, over `IProductRepository.GetProductsReadingAsync`: usages, packaging links to a set, and packaging links to a product followed recursively in one CTE) and, after the save, record a version of each one whose recipe changed (`RecordChangesAsync`), with a baseline first for one with no history. Notes: "{sub-assembly} changed on Sub-Assemblies", "Changed when {product} was saved". The success notice names them (`RecordedMessage`, three named, the rest counted). In the UI layer for the reason the snapshot is: products are priced only there. A failure to read or record shows after the save and never stops it. `ProductsViewModel.LoadCommand` re-reads a showing History tab (and clears a hidden one), so a version recorded from Sub-Assemblies shows when Products is shown again. Not recorded, as before: a role change or rename (not part of a recipe), a material's cost (the Today columns), and a composite's held products' recipes (only their quantities are its recipe).
    - **Compare With lists every version** besides the version before and the product as saved now (`VersionComparison` records, equal by value so a re-read keeps the pick; the picker binds `SelectedItem`). Two versions always read older to newer. Compare With moved to its own line under the Changes heading, so it takes its half's width.
    - **Cost Over Time**: an `SfChart` beside the changes (the bottom row split in two), three `LineSeries` (Base Cost, Suggested Price, Current Price, then) over `ProductVersionHistory.Trend`, oldest first, from the second version on. The first chart since Reports' went on 2026-09-27; this one has a real trend to show. Colours from the dataviz skill's palette (slots 1–3), run through its validator against Latte Base: all pass, with a contrast warning met by the legend, tooltips and the table. No current price is `NaN`, a gap.
    - **A clone or a variant starts its history** with its first version, noted "Cloned from X, version N" / "Variant of X, version N" (`RecordMadeFromAsync`); the number only when the source as saved is its latest version. Its first Save then records no baseline.
    - Tests: `ProductVersionPersistenceTests.The_products_reading_a_sub_assembly_are_those_linking_it_and_those_whose_packaging_reads_theirs`, `SubAssembliesViewModelVersionTests`, `ProductVersionRecorderTests`, and `ProductsViewModelVersionTests` (shared lines, a version from another screen, comparing any two, the chart's points, clones and variants). Test fixture `VersionRecorders` builds the recorder for `SubAssembliesViewModel`'s tests.
    - **Performance (2026-10-17)**, measured on one dough 40 products link, and one product with 102 versions. Opening the History tab took 0.3 s in 10 commands, repricing for the cost basis 33 ms, and picking a row under 1 ms, so they were left alone. Two save paths were fixed:
      - `RecordChangesAsync` called `HasAnyAsync` and `AddAsync` for each product, each on its own context and serializable transaction: 209 commands and 0.8 s for the 40. `IProductVersionRepository.AddRangeAsync` now writes them in one transaction. A `ProductVersionDraft` carries its baseline, and every product's latest comes from one query (an OUTER APPLY): 12 commands and 0.4 s. A product deleted in between is skipped.
      - Every Products Save of a saved product captured the products sharing its sections, and read them again after, even when it changed only its own price: about 1 s and 51 commands with 39 sharing. It now captures only when it writes linked lines (`AreLinkedLinesEdited`) or packaging (`ChangedLines`, `ChangedPackagingLink`). Nothing else it writes is read by another product, since owned sub-assemblies are used by their product alone. That Save now takes 0.4 s and 32 commands.
      - Tests: `ProductVersionPersistenceTests` (`AddRangeAsync`'s baselines, skips, and a long note refusing them all), `SubAssembliesViewModelVersionTests.Recording_the_products_a_sub_assembly_changes_takes_as_many_commands_for_six_as_for_two`, and `ProductsViewModelVersionTests.A_Save_of_the_products_own_figures_reads_none_of_the_products_sharing_its_dough` (it fails, 64 commands against 46, with the old capture). Since 2026-10-30 that test counts once the Save's background work is done (`SqlCommandCounter.SettledAsync`). The list's below-markup flags (2026-10-29) are read in the background after every load, Save's reload included, so a count taken as the Save returned caught more or less of them: 53 to 56 against 53 in a run of the test on its own, which failed it, though both Saves send the same 62. Settled, the count stays exact: a Save made to treat the sharing products as changed fails it, 85 against 63.
- **PriceScenario: the Price Simulator's saved scenarios** (2026-10-22, at the user's request, from the suggestions "Price to Keep Markup" and "Saved scenarios"). The user's choices: kept **in the database** (shared, and in every backup) rather than per Windows user in the view-state file as the Production Plan is; **a line keeps what was typed**, so a percentage goes on the price as it is when the scenario is opened and a typed new price stays as saved; and **save, open and delete only**, one scenario on screen at a time (side-by-side comparison was offered and not taken).
  - **Tables `price_scenarios`** (`Id`, `Name` unique ignoring case, at most `PriceScenarioRules.NameMaxLength` 100, `SavedAt`) **and `price_scenario_lines`** (`PriceScenarioId` Cascade, `RawMaterialId` **Cascade**, `ChangePercent?`, `NewPrice?`, `SortOrder`; unique per scenario and material). Check constraints back up `PriceScenarioRules`: exactly one of `change_percent` and `new_price` (`CK_PriceScenarioLines_OneTyped`), a change above -100%, a price above 0. A material's lines go when it's deleted, rather than blocking the delete, since a scenario is only a trial; one no longer bought (its source changed) is left out when the scenario opens, and the screen says which.
  - **`IPriceScenarioRepository`**: `GetAllAsync` (newest first, then by name, each with its lines, two queries), `SaveAsync(name, lines, savedAt)` (an upsert by name, ignoring case: an existing scenario keeps its name and has its lines replaced, in one transaction; `PriceScenarioRules.EnsureValid` first), `DeleteAsync`.
  - **The screen** (`PriceSimulatorViewModel`): a **Scenarios list at the left of the Price Changes table** (2026-10-22, at the user's request, replacing the first cut's `ComboBoxAdv` picker: "displayed on the left inside a two column list, one column for the date, the second for the name"), an `SfDataGrid` of Saved (`MMM d, yyyy  h:mm tt`) and Name, newest first, whose `SelectedItem` is `SelectedScenario`, under a Scenarios heading with Save Scenario and Delete Scenario. The two sides share the card's rows, so headings, captions and the two 200-high tables line up. `SimulatedPriceChange.ToScenarioLine` gives the typed one of Change % and New Price, and `Open(line)` types it back. The table is compared with what was last opened or saved (`HasUnsavedChanges`, by value); picking a scenario over unsaved changes asks first, and keeping them picks the open scenario again in the list (`OpenScenarioAsync` goes on after a `Task.Yield`, so the list's own pick is over before it's undone). Save asks for a name (the open scenario's to start with, `TextPromptWindow`) and asks again before replacing another scenario of that name. Delete leaves the changes in the table. Clear All closes the scenario too. The list letting go of its pick, as the grid can when its rows are replaced, closes nothing.
  - **Price to Keep Markup**: `PriceChangeRules.PriceToKeepMarkup(currentPrice, baseCost, newBaseCost)` = the current price × the base cost's own factor, since the markup is on the base cost and VAT goes on top, so VAT cancels out; null without a current price or a base cost. The Products table shows **Current Price** before Markup %, and **Price to Keep Markup** after Simulated Markup % (`PriceSimulatorProductRow`); the report prints it under a moved product's Current Price ("To keep markup 61.28", `ProductPrintLayout.KeepMarkupNote`). Not rounded: there is no price-rounding rule.
  - **Checked in the app on the secondary monitor (now 1920 × 1080) on 2026-10-22.** The list was then checked the same way: clicking a saved scenario opened it into the table and repriced the products. With 14 columns the Products table's two-line headings lost words ("Simulated Raw Material Cost", "Price to Keep Markup"), so they take three lines (`GridHeaderHeight.Lines="3"`) and Product is 220 wide rather than 280.
  - Tests: `PriceScenarioRulesTests`, `PriceChangeRulesTests` (the price to keep a markup), `PriceScenarioPersistenceTests`, `PriceSimulatorViewModelTests` (save and reopen after a price moves, the question over unsaved changes, replacing another scenario, delete) and `ProductPrintLayoutTests`.
  - **Unbuffered** (2026-10-24, at the user's request: "Implement buffered and unbuffered price in the Price Simulator"; the user chose a switch like the Products screen's over unbuffered columns beside the buffered ones or typing a new buffered price apart from the new price). An **Unbuffered** button on the Products card, before Print Report (`ToggleCostBasisCommand`, `CostBasisButtonLabel`/`Tooltip`, named for the costs it switches to), switches every figure, now and simulated, between the materials' buffered and true costs, with an **Unbuffered costs** badge beside the Products heading (`IsUnbuffered`). Session-only like Products' (`PriceSimulatorViewModel.CostBasis`, buffered at every launch), not part of a scenario and not an unsaved change. A change still moves both costs by the same factor (`PriceChangeRules.CostsAfter`), so its percent is the same at either basis. `ReadAsync` prices every product as saved at both bases (`Data.NowBuffered`/`NowUnbuffered`, `NowAt`), so a switch only reruns the recalculation at the other basis; the recalculation keeps the now figures it used (`_now`) beside the simulated ones, so the report matches the table. Print Report's pages say "Unbuffered costs" through `ProductPrintout.CostBasis`, as Products' do; Export to Excel suggests **Price Simulator (Unbuffered)** (`ExportTitleNow`) with a **Products (Unbuffered)** sheet. Tests: `PriceSimulatorViewModelTests` (the figures and the report at true costs, and back; the export's name).
- **SavedPlan: the Production Plan's saved plans** (retired on 2026-10-31 with the Production Plan, which moved to TinapayanBMS; its tables dropped by `RetireProductionPlan`, empty; kept here as history) (2026-10-24, at the user's request, from the suggestions "Saved plans", "Show each mix's ingredients", "Spare pieces", "Sales value and margin", "Cost of what's bought", "Group the requisition by category" and "Half batches"; "Subtract stock on hand" was left for later and dropped on 2026-10-28 at the user's choice, and the grouping by category taken out again). The user's choices: kept **in the database**, as the Price Simulator's scenarios are, rather than per Windows user; **profit at the Current Price over the full base cost** (ingredients, packaging, wastage, labor, overhead and administrative), VAT out; **half batches as one tick on the plan**, not per sub-assembly or in Settings; and **each mix's ingredients in rows that open** in the Mixing Sheet, not a panel for the picked mix.
  - **Tables `saved_plans`** (`Id`, `Name` unique ignoring case, at most `SavedPlanRules.NameMaxLength` 100, `SavedAt`, `HalfBatches`) **and `saved_plan_lines`** (`SavedPlanId` Cascade, `ProductId` **Cascade**, `Units` above 0, `CK_SavedPlanLines_Units`, `SortOrder`). No unique index on a plan's product: the table lets a product be listed twice, its units added together. A product deleted for good takes its lines with it; one in the Deleted folder keeps them, and is left out when the plan opens, which says how many were.
  - **`ISavedPlanRepository`**: `GetAllAsync` (newest first, then by name, with its lines, two queries), `SaveAsync(name, lines, halfBatches, savedAt)` (an upsert by name, ignoring case, replacing the lines in one transaction; `SavedPlanRules.EnsureValid` first), `DeleteAsync`.
  - **The screen** (`ProductionPlanViewModel`, `ProductionPlanView`) follows the Price Simulator's scenarios: a **Saved Plans** list (Saved, Name) over the Plan table, with Save Plan and Delete Plan; picking one over a plan not saved asks first; Clear Plan closes the open one. The open plan's id is kept in the view-state file (`production-plan-open`), so the next run knows the table is that plan and whether it's changed since, and Half batches under `production-plan-half-batches`. Units are kept without trailing zeros (`0.##########`), since a saved plan's come back with four places.
  - **Spare** (`ProductionPlanRules.SparePieces`): each product's share of the rounding up, `(Batches − BatchesNeeded) × its pieces ÷ its batches needed`, rounded off at 4 places and down to whole pieces. A shared mix lists each product's figure joined by "or", since the spare dough goes to one or the other.
  - **Use the spare** (2026-10-29, at the user's request): a row of buttons under the Mixing Sheet, one per product with units to take (`ProductionPlanResult.SpareUses`, `SpareUse`), adds that many units to the product's first line (`UseSpareCommand`). The figure is `ProductionPlanRules.UnitsFromSpare`: the most whole sellable units of the product that leave every mix's batches as they are, found by halving between 0 and its smallest spare across its mixes ÷ Quantity Required (fewer units never need more batches), each candidate checked by planning it. So a spare dough is offered only as far as the product's filling and topping have room too; a topping at a yield of 1 never has any. One figure per product, under the table rather than in a column: a column of buttons narrowed For until `GridAutoRowHeight` measured a borderline text as one line where it drew two (tried and dropped the same day).
  - **Sales and profit** (`ProductionPlanRules.SalesOf`): units × (Current Price, or the worked-out selling price without one) is the sales with VAT; the profit is that less VAT, less units × `BaseCost`. The plan's Profit Margin is the profit over the sales before VAT (`CostingRules.PercentOf`), as `CalculateProfitMarginPercent` has it for one product. `PlanLineRow` takes a `ProductCostSummary` lookup beside its product lookup for them.
  - **Buy Cost**: the whole purchase units × the dearest brand's price (`PurchaseUnit.Price`), beside Cost, which is what's needed at buffered costs.
  - **Superseded: the requisition by category** (built the same day, removed at the user's request: "stop grouping the requisition by category"). It was an `SfDataGrid` group on a hidden `Group` column of a `RequisitionGroup` (a category, ingredients' before packaging's), with a heading row per category in the printout. The requisition is back to ingredients first, then packaging, by name, with its Type column. Worth keeping if it comes back: a caption summary row of each group's costs shows in place of the group's caption, so the category's name went (seen in the app).
  - **The Mixing Sheet's ingredients** are a `DetailsViewDefinition` on `MixRow.Ingredients` (`MixIngredientRow`: per batch and in all the batches; a made ingredient's BOM scaled to the grams to prepare, from `ProductionPlan.PreparedLines`). Two things it took:
    - **Row heights.** With a details view, `RowGenerator.QueryRowHeight` returns before raising `QueryRowHeight` (read from the decompiled 35.1.41), so `GridAutoRowHeight` never heard and the For column's second line was cut off again. It now gives a grid with details its lines' heights in `VisualContainer.RowHeights` directly, passing over the details rows (`IsInDetailsViewIndex`), once a line's cell is drawn (it waits for `LayoutUpdated` until then) and again on a change, a resize or a sort. A details row's height is the nested grid's, which the grid keeps itself. `GridAutoRowHeightTests` covers it, and fails without it.
    - **Rows staying open.** A merge replaces a mix whose ingredients changed, and the grid shows the new row closed. `ProductionPlanView` remembers the mixes opened, by name (`DetailsViewExpanded`/`DetailsViewCollapsed`), and opens them again after the change (`ExpandDetailsViewAt`). It subscribes to `Mixes` before `DataContext` is set, so before the grid, and a row closed while the change is under way isn't taken as the user closing it.
  - **Excel**: three sheets (Plan, Mixing Sheet, Purchase Requisition). The grid converter writes a row's details under it with their own heading row, outlined, and applies each numeric column's format itself, without a thousands separator, so `ExcelExport` now writes every figure in `CellsExportingEventHandler` (`Handled`), formatted by the column of the grid the cell came from (`GridViewDefinition.DataGrid` for a details cell, until 2026-11-04, when that and the bold details headings went, from a code review, as no table had details any more), and bolds every heading row. The old way, a format per sheet column after the export, would have given a details row the parent's formats.
  - **Checked in the app on the secondary monitor (1920 × 1080) on 2026-10-24**, against the dev database, nothing saved (the kept plan backed up and restored, `saved_plans` still empty). The kept plan, Pandesal × 1,150, showed Sales 5,750.00, Profit 1,382.22 and a 26.9% margin; Pandesal Dough 25 batches, 28 pieces spare, opening to its ingredients per batch and in all; Buy Cost 25,605.00 beside a Total Cost of 2,159.25 (whole cases and pails). Half batches made it 24.5 batches and 4 spare, and the open row stayed open. The tick was back on the next run. The print preview had all of it. Found there: the requisition's caption summary hid the category names (above).
  - Tests: `ProductionPlanRulesTests` (lines, half batches, spare, the units the spare has room for, prepared lines, sales), `RecipeScalingRulesTests.HalfBatches_rounds_up_to_the_next_half_batch`, `SavedPlanRulesTests`, `SavedPlanPersistenceTests`, `ProductionPlanViewModelTests` (spare and ingredients, sales at the current or worked-out price, Buy Cost, half batches kept, save, open, open over an unsaved plan, a product deleted since, delete, an empty plan refused), `ProductPrintLayoutTests`, `ExcelExportTests.A_rows_details_go_under_it_with_their_own_headings_and_their_own_columns_decimals` and `GridAutoRowHeightTests`.

### Derived products (Phase 10; reworked 2026-09-14)

Some products derive from others (e.g. Special Ensaymada Small from Special Ensaymada Big, differing only in weight; or products sharing a dough sub-assembly but differing in toppings). Since 2026-09-14, when sub-assemblies became owned by products, deriving works like this:

- **Since 2026-09-23 every sub-assembly is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen** (see SubAssembly above): a new section and a copy are saved there as sub-assemblies named after the product, and linked; Make a Copy gives a new section with the linked one's lines and add-ins and piece size, which Save keeps as "Cheese Roll Dough 2" and links instead. Copy an existing one takes the source's role and lines, not its name. The picker names each "Cheese Roll Dough — Sub-Assemblies (Dough)", by name (`GetLinkableAsync`). The bullets below are as they were written, with "another product's" meaning one a product owned.
- **Link a sub-assembly**: a product uses another product's sub-assembly, or a standalone one, as it is, through a `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` of it. The lines are the owner's (or the Sub-Assemblies screen's) and follow its every change (read-only in the linking product until 2026-09-27; since then its lines can be changed there too, and Save writes them to the sub-assembly: see Edit a linked one's lines in the product, under SubAssembly); the **piece size is the linking product's own**, so Small and Big can share a dough at 30 g and 60 g a piece. Links don't chain (only an owner's own or a standalone sub-assembly can be linked), so there's nothing to cycle. A linked section can be turned into the product's own with **Make a Copy**.
- **Copy a sub-assembly**: a new sub-assembly of the product's own, starting from another product's or a standalone one's lines and role (a name, made unique within the product, until 2026-09-17). If the recipe already has that role, the form starts on the first role it doesn't. Nothing connects it to its source afterward.
- The three ways in read **New sub-assembly**, **Link an existing one** and **Copy an existing one** (since 2026-09-15; "…another product's" before). They are the items on Add Sub-Assembly's drop-down since 2026-09-20 — the form's own mode picker before that — and a link's or copy's form is titled with the one picked ("Link an existing sub-assembly"). A new one's form has no title since 2026-09-25: in its place the **Moves to Sub-Assemblies on Save** badge shows while its role is neither product-owned nor packaging (`IsFormNewMovingToSubAssemblies`), and nothing does in a product-owned role, whose new sub-assembly stays the product's own. Its picker, **Existing Sub-Assembly**, lists standalone ones first (`GetLinkableAsync`), as "Butter Glaze — Sub-Assemblies (Finishing)". A linked standalone section's badge reads "From Sub-Assemblies" (`ProductUsageEdit.SourceNameOf`, the one place that names where a linked sub-assembly comes from). A new, unsaved product is offered them too: `RefreshLinkableOptions` matches `OwnerProductId is null` first, since a null owner compared with a new product's null id would otherwise count as its own.
- **Link the packaging** (`PackagingLink`, table `PackagingLinks`, one per product): a product's packaging reads through to another product's instead of owning lines. It resolves every line the linked product has. From 2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20 it resolved the per-piece lines only (`PackId is null`), since a pack's lines pointed at rows in the *linked* product's `ProductPacks` and following them would have shown the linking product lines for packs it didn't sell and couldn't edit; with packs gone there is only one set of lines to resolve. It's saved at once, can be repointed or removed at any time, and **unlinking never loses data**: it first materializes the lines it shows (resolved through the link) as the product's own, then removes the link. Chains are allowed (C's packaging linked to B's linked to A's) and resolved recursively at read time; a cycle is rejected when a link is made (`PackagingLinkRules.EnsureNoCycle`). A product whose packaging another links can't be deleted until that link goes.
  - **Or a packaging set** (2026-09-18): `PackagingLink.LinkedProductId` became nullable beside a new `LinkedSubAssemblyId` (Restrict to `SubAssemblies`), and `CK_PackagingLinks_OneTarget` requires exactly one. `IPackagingLinkRepository.SetSubAssemblyLinkAsync` links a standalone set only; `SetLinkAsync` and it each clear the other target on a relink.
    - **One set per sellable unit**: `PackagingLineRepository.ResolveEffectiveAsync` returns the set's lines as unsaved `PackagingLine`s of the product being resolved (`UnitId` and `QtyPerUnit` the set line's own, `AuthoredField = QtyPerUnit`, the material with its units). So the Products screen, the cost summary, Reports and unlinking's copy take them unchanged, and a chain through products ends at a set (it links nothing, so there's nothing to cycle).
    - **A clone keeps a set its source links directly**, like a standalone recipe (the 2026-09-15 rule); a set reached through another product's link is copied like the rest of that product's packaging.
    - The banner reads "Linked to Gift Box Set (Sub-Assemblies)". The Products `LoadCommand` refetches linked packaging's lines while the form still shows the link as saved, so a set edited on the Sub-Assemblies screen shows on return; own lines and an unsaved link choice aren't touched there, since they may hold unsaved edits.
  - **Made in the Recipe Structure, saved with Save** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "make the Recipe structure empty for new Products, then we can reuse the Add-Subassembly … If the Role is packaging the packaging datagrid will be use"). The **Links & Clone card is gone**, and the tab is just **Clone**; the user's choices were to save the link with Save (like a linked recipe section, so an unsaved product can link too), to drop the card, and to offer both sets and other products' packaging. **Since 2026-09-25 the Link picker offers only what the Sub-Assemblies screen keeps** (the user's rule: "Link only from the standalone SubAssemblies never from the product"): packaging sets and standalone recipes, never another product's packaging, which Copy still offers (`RefreshLinkableOptions`). A packaging link to a product still comes from Create Variant, and existing ones stay as they are.
    - **Storage didn't change**: the product's packaging is still its per-piece `PackagingLines` or its one `PackagingLink`, never a `SubAssembly` row, so `EnsureRecipeRole` still keeps the Packaging role off a product's own sub-assemblies, and costing, Reports, packs and the cloner are untouched.
    - **`IProductRepository.SaveAsync` takes a `PackagingLinkDraft?`** (a closed record since 2026-10-30: `Set(SubAssemblyId)`, `OtherProduct(ProductId)`, `None` or `NewSet`; null leaves the link alone), written in the same transaction just before the lines (`PackagingLinkRepository.SetAsync`, sharing the product and set checks with `SetLinkAsync`/`SetSubAssemblyLinkAsync`). `None` only drops the link; unlike `RemoveLinkAsync` it copies nothing, since the form sends the lines it holds.
    - **Unlinking is Make a Copy on the Packaging tab** (`CopyPackagingCommand`): the lines showing become the product's own, and Save writes both the unlink and the lines (`ChangedLines` writes them whenever the product was linked when loaded, even if they match).
    - **Move to Sub-Assemblies on the Packaging tab, Make a Copy's reverse** (2026-09-30, at the user's request: "Make the packaging sub-assembly in a product also have a command for moving to Sub-Assemblies and the product link to it automatically similar to how other roles of subassembly having this command"). While the packaging is the product's own lines (`CanMovePackaging`), the tab's top row has **Move to Sub-Assemblies** at the far right, after the cost caption (the user's choice, over the bottom row beside Remove Line: the same spot as a recipe section's form). `MovePackagingToSubAssembliesCommand` checks the lines (`CurrentLines`: a half-filled line, or none, is refused with the message) and sets `IsPackagingMoving`, in one undo step (`ProductFormSnapshot.IsPackagingMoving`); a "Moves to Sub-Assemblies on Save" badge takes the linked badge's place, and the lines are still typed into until Save. `ShowPackagingLink`, which every load, Delete Sub-Assembly, Make a Copy and add go through, ends it; `RestoreSnapshot` puts it back after. Save sends `PackagingLinkDraft.NewSet` with the lines (`ChangedLines` always sends them then), and `ProductRepository.SaveAsync` runs `PackagingLinkRepository.LinkNewSetAsync` in place of the link-then-lines pair, after the recipe: the lines become a standalone set in the Packaging role (found by its flag), named `StandaloneNameFor(product, role, null)` made unique ("Ensaymada Packaging", "… 2"), each line's quantity per sellable unit its quantity per set in the same unit and order, through `SubAssemblyRepository.SavePackagingSetAsync` (`SaveStandalonePackagingAsync`'s body on the caller's context, so every set check applies); the product's lines are deleted and its packaging links the set. Costs don't change. After the reload the tab shows the linked set as any other. The top row keeps the button's height and margin (`MinHeight="34"`) with a badge in it too: the badge is 5 px shorter, and the table jumped up as the button gave way to it (seen in the app). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with a throwaway product (Move, Undo, Move, Save, the badge opening the set), deleted afterwards. Covered by three cases each in `PackagingSetPersistenceTests` and `ProductsViewModelPackagingTests`. No migration.
- **Clone** (`IProductCloner.CloneAsync`, one transaction): a one-time copy of a product as saved. **Every sub-assembly it uses, its own and another product's, becomes the clone's own copy** (role, lines and piece size; a source with two in one role, saved before that was refused, can't be cloned until it's put right), plus its packaging as it shows (resolved through a packaging link — a clone never inherits a packaging link, only the data), **its packs with their own packaging lines, as new rows of the clone's own** (2026-09-18), and its `CostingParameters`. Until 2026-09-14 a clone pointed at the same sub-assemblies as its source, so editing one changed both.
  - **A standalone sub-assembly stays linked** (2026-09-15, the user's choice), at the same piece size: it's kept apart to be shared, so the clone follows it like its source does.
  - **Since 2026-09-23 a clone links every sub-assembly its source does** (the user's choice, over copying them as new ones named after the clone), at the same piece sizes, with the source's add-ins as its own: `ProductCloner`'s `CloneRecipe` went, and Clone and Create Variant share `LinkedRecipe`. They differ only in the packaging: Clone copies it, Create Variant links it. Make a Copy splits a section off afterward. **Later the same day** a product's own sub-assemblies (product-owned roles) came back, and a clone or a variant gets its own copy of each of those; see SubAssembly's "Product-owned sub-assemblies".
- **Create Variant** (`IProductCloner.CreateVariantAsync`, one transaction; 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "create a product variant by not only cloning the recipes but also create a linked to them automatically"): a clone whose recipe and packaging are **linked instead of copied**, for Ensaymada Small from Ensaymada Big. It's the Clone tab's second button, beside Clone, sharing its New Product Name.
  - **Reworked 2026-10-26, at the user's request** ("if the product's subassemblies are created using variant, all its subassemblies besides packaging are linked"; then, asked to choose: "Link all sub-assemblies, if Product already contains link subassembly - it must be skip. Packaging should never be copied or linked. In essence, only the ingredients subassemblies are being linked", and "only enable that linking when cloning a product using variant command"). It supersedes the bullets below on the recipe and the packaging. **A variant links every sub-assembly the source uses**, at the source's piece sizes, with the source's add-ins on each as its own: **the source's own** (product-owned roles, or unlinked into its own), and **the ones it links** from the Sub-Assemblies screen (a dough, sponge or batter), the user correcting the first cut, which left those out ("linkable subassemblies such as dough, sponge, or batter should also be copied to the new product created using variant command", then choosing the same link over a copy of its own). **It has no packaging**, neither linked nor copied. `ProductCloner.VariantRecipe` builds it; Clone is unchanged (`DerivedRecipe`, packaging copied).
    - **Only Create Variant links another product's own.** `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetRecipeAsync` gained `linksOthersOwn`, which the cloner alone passes. Every other save still runs `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureLinkable`, except on a sub-assembly the product already links, so a variant keeps its links through its own saves, and one it drops can't be linked again. The Link picker still leaves products' own out.
    - **The source can't pull one out from under a variant.** `SetRecipeAsync` refuses to drop one of the product's own that another product links (`EnsureNoneLinkedElsewhereAsync`, the Sub-Assemblies screen's message), before anything is written. `ProductRepository.EnsureNothingLeansOnAsync` refuses deleting the product, to the Deleted folder or for good, while one of its own is linked elsewhere. Moving one to the Sub-Assemblies screen, or a role losing product-owned, releases it, and the variant's link follows.
    - **The Products screen names where it's kept.** `SubAssemblyReads` now brings every sub-assembly's owner (`LEFT JOIN products o`, its id and name), usages included, and `ProductUsageEdit.LinkedOwner` carries it (`LinkedOwnerOf`, kept through `With`, `Released`, `WithLinked`, `WithLinkedForm` and a version restore). The linked badge reads "Ensaymada Big Filling, from Ensaymada Big", with its own tooltip (`LinkedFromToolTip`), and opens the source (`OpenLinkedSourceCommand`, `OpenProduct`) where a standalone one opens the Sub-Assemblies screen. A variant can change the linked lines as for any link, and Save writes them to the source's own.
    - No migration. Covered by `ProductVariantPersistenceTests` (rewritten), the composite and Quantity Required persistence tests, and `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.Create_Variant_makes_a_product_that_links_the_sources_own_with_no_packaging_and_shows_it`.
  - **Every recipe section is linked, at the source's piece sizes**, which are the variant's own to change: the source's own sub-assemblies are linked from the source, and one the source links (another product's or a standalone one) is linked where it's kept, since links don't chain, with the source's add-ins as the variant's own. The variant owns no sub-assembly; Make a Copy unlinks a section as usual. Where a role repeats, one of the source's own without a name goes by the source's name there (`SectionLabel`), so the sections stay apart; a source saved with two unnamed in one role is refused, as for Clone.
  - **Its packaging links the source's** (the user's choice, over copying it), a link to the source product even when the source itself links a set or another product (a chain), and only when the source has packaging (its own lines or a link). Make a Copy on the Packaging tab unlinks it. For a variant that sells differently — a Box of 8 — the link is repointed at a **packaging set** on the Recipe Structure's Packaging tab, which is what makes a variant the way to add a pack.
  - **Its `QuantityRequired` is copied**, and so are the description, category, current price and costing parameters, as for Clone. (Its *packs* were copied until 2026-09-20, when packs became variants of this kind themselves.) `ProductCloner` shares one core between the two (`CreateFromAsync`), which differ only in the recipe (`CloneRecipe`/`VariantRecipe`) and the packaging.
  - The usual guards follow from the links: the source then can't drop a sub-assembly the variant links, or be deleted, until the variant copies or removes it, and the source's sections name the variant among the products linking them.
- **`CostingParameters`** (labor/overhead/administrative/margin/VAT) always stay independent per product: nothing links them.
- **Superseded (2026-09-14): role-tab Live Template links** (`ProductTabLink` with `TabKind = SubAssemblyRole`), which linked all of a product's sub-assemblies in one role to the same role on another product, weights and all. Sub-assembly links replaced them (the user's choice): they cover sharing a recipe, and each product keeps its own weight. What nothing does any more is follow the source's weights, or pick up sub-assemblies added to the source's role later. Migration `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` turned every role-tab link into links to the sub-assemblies its role showed, at the same weights, and renamed what was left of `ProductTabLinks` (the Packaging-tab links) to `PackagingLinks`.

## Syncfusion UI Framework

The app is styled with **Syncfusion Essential Studio for WPF**, every package at `SyncfusionVersion` in `Directory.Packages.props`, licensed with a key the build compiles in from the `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` environment variable (see Tech Stack), and Syncfusion's **Windows11Light** theme (the `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` package). What each control took, its quirks and the workarounds the app carries (the theme and its accent, the grids, the tree, per-monitor DPI awareness, the window chrome, printing) are in **[docs/plan/syncfusion.md](docs/plan/syncfusion.md)**. Read it before changing how a Syncfusion control is used.

## Application Architecture

- **Repository interfaces live in `ApplicationCore`, implementations in `Infrastructure`** (`IRawMaterialRepository`/`RawMaterialRepository`, and so on), consumed by UI ViewModels through DI. Every lookup table has a repository of its own, since each is edited in the Settings dialog: `IUnitOfMeasureRepository`, `IRawMaterialCategoryRepository`, `IProductCategoryRepository` and `ISubAssemblyRoleRepository` with full CRUD, and `ICostingDefaultsRepository` for its one row. **Superseded (2026-09-12): a generic, read-only `ILookupRepository<T>`/`LookupRepository<T>`** served `UnitOfMeasure`, `IngredientCategory` and `PackagingCategory`, while `SubAssemblyRole`, editable from Phase 7, had a dedicated repository. It was deleted once the categories, and then units of measure, became editable and left it with no consumers (see the `UnitOfMeasure` notes under the embedded management panel below). Don't bring a generic repository back just because a table is small.
- **`TinapayanRMSDbContext` is registered with `AddDbContextFactory`, not `AddDbContext`.** ViewModels are DI singletons (`MainWindowViewModel`, `RawMaterialsViewModel`, …) so they outlive any single screen, and WPF has no ASP.NET-style per-request scope to hand a plain `AddDbContext`-registered instance to — resolving one into two singleton ViewModels at startup handed them the *same* `DbContext` instance, and two screens' tabs loading concurrently on app startup then threw `InvalidOperationException: A second operation was started on this context instance before a previous operation completed` the first time the app was actually run (not caught by the build or by any test — only by launching it). Every repository instead takes `IDbContextFactory<TinapayanRMSDbContext>` and creates-and-disposes a short-lived context per call (`await using var dbContext = await dbContextFactory.CreateDbContextAsync(cancellationToken);`), which is also naturally safe for concurrent calls from different screens. Follow this pattern for every future repository — never inject `TinapayanRMSDbContext` directly into a repository or ViewModel.
  - **The factory is also the unit of work** (2026-10-28, at the user's request: a Save and the versions it records were to be written together). `SharedTransactionDbContextFactory` (`Infrastructure/Services`) replaced `AddDbContextFactory`'s and is registered as both `IDbContextFactory<TinapayanRMSDbContext>` and `IUnitOfWork`. `RunAsync(work)` opens one connection with a Serializable transaction (`ProductVersionRepository` numbers versions under Serializable) and puts it in a static `AsyncLocal`. Every context made on that flow, by any factory instance for the same connection string, is built on that connection with `UseTransaction`, so tests' many factories join too. A repository's own transaction is begun with `BeginOrJoinTransactionAsync` (`Services/Transactions.cs`), which inside a run hands back a part whose commit and dispose do nothing. No repository rolls back by hand or catches a database error and carries on, which a shared PostgreSQL transaction couldn't survive (a failed statement aborts it).
    - **Reads inside a run go one at a time**: they share one connection. `SavedProductReader.ReadDetailsAsync`/`ReadComponentCostsAsync` and `ProductVersionRecorder`'s snapshots read in sequence since. Measured on the dev database (68 products), every product's detail read went from 22 ms side by side to 31 ms in sequence, one product's from 12 to 13 ms; the 2026-10-03 batching, which keeps the number of queries fixed, is what mattered.
    - **`VersionedSaves`** (`UI/ViewModels`, since products are priced only in the UI) holds the saves that record versions, each in one run: the Products Save (baseline, `ProductRepository.SaveAsync`, the product read back and its version, then the sharing products' versions), Clone and Create Variant with their first version, and the Sub-Assemblies Save with its products' versions. Until then a version that couldn't be recorded was shown after a Save that stood; now it takes the Save back. Pinned by `SharedTransactionDbContextFactoryTests` and `VersionedSavesTests`.
- **Startup time (2026-10-22, at the user's request: "I notice that the app has a slow startup", after turning on `PublishReadyToRun`).** Measured on the published app: the window appeared in about 0.85 s, but the UI thread stayed busy until about 4.7 s (8.2 s without ReadyToRun, so ReadyToRun was helping). A trace of the UI thread put the time in building all seven screens in `MainWindow`'s constructor, theming and laying them out, and EF Core building its model on the first query (about 0.4 s, on the UI thread, before the Raw Materials load's first `await`). Three changes, all kept:
  - **`PublishReadyToRun`** in the UI project (it already had the `win-x64` runtime identifier ReadyToRun needs). Only `dotnet publish` uses it.
  - **A compiled EF Core model** in `Infrastructure/CompiledModels/`, made by `dotnet ef dbcontext optimize --project src/TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure --output-dir CompiledModels --namespace TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure.CompiledModels` and regenerated by the same command after every model change (the user's choice, over the `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Tasks` build step that would regenerate it on every build). EF Core finds it through the generated `[assembly: DbContextModel]` attribute, so `Configure` has no `UseModel`, and the design-time commands still build the model from `OnModelCreating`. A stale one compiles and runs (it finds properties by name), so `CompiledModelTests` compare it with the configured model. They don't use the migrations differ, which needs design-time data the compiled model leaves out, or column collations, which it doesn't keep: they compare the model's `ShortDefault` debug view (entities, properties, keys, relationships, indexes) and a listing of its tables, columns, store types, keys, foreign keys and indexes. Changing a max length or dropping a unique index without regenerating failed both.
  - **Screens are made on first show** (see "Tab content stays loaded" below).
  - Afterwards, traced the same way: the model build went from 389 ms to 18 ms, the first screen's load from 492 ms to 115 ms, and `MainWindow`'s constructor from 916 ms to 636 ms. CPU time to idle went from about 5.1 s to about 3.3 s. The first start after a publish is still slow (about 5 s to the window), since Windows reads and scans each new file once.
- **CRUD screen pattern** (established by `IngredientsView`/`IngredientsViewModel` and `PackagingMaterialsView`/`PackagingMaterialsViewModel`, Phase 4, merged into `RawMaterialsView`/`RawMaterialsViewModel` on 2026-09-13; card layout and right-rail arrangement reworked Phase 16): a read-only `SfDataGrid` list on the left, bound to a `DynamicData SourceCache<T,int>` via `.Connect().SortAndBind(out collection, comparer).Subscribe()` (not the obsolete `.Sort().Bind()` pair); a master-detail edit form on the right, populated from the grid's `SelectedItem` via a `WhenAnyValue` subscription; `New`/`Save`/`Delete` as `ButtonAdv`s bound to `ReactiveCommand`s. Follow this pattern for future CRUD screens rather than in-grid cell editing, which needs SfDataGrid event wiring that's much harder to get right without a live designer.
  - **Since Phase 16, the shape is a list card plus a right rail, not fixed Grid columns of bare panels.** The root `Grid` carries `PageBackgroundBrush`, `Margin="16"`, and an `ErrorToast` laid over the whole Grid (it takes no row of its own). The Grid is two columns: a `*`-width list card (`CardStyle` `Border` + `SectionHeaderStyle` header + the `SfDataGrid`, `ColumnSizer="AutoWithLastColumnFill"` so every column — including money columns — fits without a horizontal scrollbar; `RawMaterialsView` gives every column a fixed width instead and sizes its list card to fit them (see the Raw Materials note below). A column's `Width` accepts pixels only, as in `Width="120"`. A value of `"*"` or `"2*"` builds cleanly but crashes the app at startup with a `XamlParseException` ("* is not a valid value for Double"), so star-size through `ColumnSizer="Star"` on the grid or on a column), and a fixed ~360px right rail in a `ScrollViewer` holding, top to bottom: a Details card (the edit form), the screen's existing conditional detail card(s) (Price History, BOM, Lines) shown under their existing `Show*` flags with an `EmptyStateStyle` hint (bound through `EmptyStateVisibilityConverter`) filling the same slot when nothing is selected, and a collapsed `Expander` for any embedded lookup panel (see the note below). `UnitsOfMeasureView` and `CategoriesView`, the Settings dialog's tabs, are the simplest case — list card plus a single form card, no expander, no conditional detail card. Every number column in a grid, money included, uses `controls:NumericColumn` (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/NumericColumn.cs`). Never use `syncfusion:GridNumericColumn` or `syncfusion:GridCurrencyColumn` directly. Money shows as a bare number (`930.00`, not `₱930.00`), so `GridCurrencyColumn` would add nothing useful: its currency editor never opens in these read-only grids, its parentheses for negatives never apply because costs are never negative, and it doesn't group thousands by default. Money columns use 2 decimals (`NumberDecimalDigits`) for totals and prices, and 4 for per-unit costs (`CostPerBaseUnit`, `CostPerPiece`). A packaging line's own total (`PackagingLineRow.CostPerUnit`, headed Cost per Set, Cost per Unit or Cost per Piece) counts as a total: 2 decimals since 2026-09-28, at the user's request; its Unit Cost stays at 4. `NumericColumn` is a `GridNumericColumn` whose constructor sets the app's defaults: `NumberGroupSeparator=","`, `NumberGroupSizes="3"` and `HorizontalHeaderContentAlignment="Right"`. Out of the box, `GridNumericColumn` doesn't group thousands (`NumberGroupSizes` is `[0]`) and centres its header. A grid column is a `Freezable`, not a `FrameworkElement`, so an implicit `Style` can't carry these defaults. This was confirmed by reflecting over `Syncfusion.SfGrid.WPF` 34.2.7. Set only what differs per column (`NumberDecimalDigits`); a value set in XAML still overrides a default.
  - **`ProductsView` (Phase 8/9) no longer uses a horizontal `ScrollViewer` of fixed-width `StackPanel`s — Phase 16 replaced that with sub-tabs plus a pinned summary**, once the six-panel version pushed the Selling Price summary off-screen. Now three columns: a 300px-wide left column (Products list card, then a Product form card below it), a centre `TabControlExt` (`CloseButtonType="Hide"`, `ShowTabItemContextMenu="False"`, `ShowTabListContextMenu="False"`, every `TabItemExt` `CanClose="False"` — these are fixed sub-tabs, not user-closable top-level tabs) holding **Usages**/**Packaging**/**Costing**/**Links & Clone**, each carrying its existing panel's content verbatim, and a pinned 260px right column with the Price Summary card (Material Cost, Base Cost, Pre-VAT Price, then Selling Price large and `AccentBrush`-colored) that stays visible across every sub-tab. Both the tab strip and the summary card are visible only when `ShowProductDetail` is true; an `EmptyStateStyle` hint fills the centre otherwise. The linked-tab notices (`IsSelectedRoleLinked`/`IsPackagingLinked`) moved from inline amber `TextBlock`s into `WarningBannerStyle` banners.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-14): the sub-tabs, pinned summary and per-part Saves are gone.** The screen was rebuilt from the user's Cream Loaf / Chocolate Cake mockups, in the Raw Materials shape: see "One Products screen" below.
- **Grids show lines both ways, and a grid with height to spare is padded with empty rows down to its bottom edge** (2026-09-15, at the user's request, following the technique in their `SfDataGrid_Demo_fixed` sample).
  - **`GridLinesVisibility="Both"` is set on every `SfDataGrid` in `Views/`.** The property's own default is Both, but the Windows11Light style sets Horizontal, so it's set locally on each grid (the app doesn't style Syncfusion controls itself: a `Style` would replace the theme's). The two recipe section tables draw their Total row under the grid, so each total cell but the last has a right divider (`SectionTotalDividerStyle`, `CardBorderBrush`) carrying the grid's vertical lines through it. The ingredient pickers' pop-up grids keep their look.
  - **The tables lines are typed into show a row header (`ShowRowHeader="True"`)** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "add row selector to datagrids", the typed-into grids when asked which): Packaging, the Products sub-assembly form's lines and add-ins, Sub-Assemblies' lines, the Bill of Materials and Alternative Units. It's the grid's own column: an arrow on the current row, a pencil while a row is edited and + on the add row; filler rows' header cells stay blank. The read-only lists (Raw Materials, Products, Sub-Assemblies, Price History, Settings) don't have it. The app's grid behaviours need no change for it: they go by row index, and `GridFillerRows.IsRecordCell` uses `GetFirstColumnIndex`, which counts the header. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on all five screens' grids.
  - **Rows are dragged into order** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly Lines, Product Recipe Lines, Packaging Lines, and BOM should allow reordering of rows through dragging"). `controls:GridRowReordering.IsEnabled="True"` (`Controls/GridRowReordering.cs`) is on the seven grids lines are typed into and saved from: Sub-Assemblies' lines and a packaging set's lines, the Products sub-assembly form's lines, add-ins and packaging, the Packaging tab, and the Bill of Materials. Not on Alternative Units or Purchase Cost Setup, whose order means nothing. The order is the ViewModel collection's, which Save writes (see Domain Model: Line order), and it needs nothing else to count as a change: every form snapshot compares its lines with `SequenceEqual`, so a move is an undo step, marks the form dirty, and an undo puts the order back.
    - **It turns on the grid's own row drag** (`AllowDraggingRows`, `AllowDrop`, `RowDropIndicatorMode="Line"`), which starts from the row header or any cell not being typed into, once the mouse has moved the system drag distance: `GridRowHeaderCell` is a `GridCell`, and `GridCell.OnPreviewMouseDown` arms a drag only when the cell isn't editing, so selecting text in an open cell never drags the row. A plain click still opens the cell (`EditTrigger="OnTap"`).
    - **Each press decides whether the grid can drag** (`ArmOnLinePress`, on the grid's `PreviewMouseDown`, which tunnels before the cell's): only a press on a line of a grid whose lines can be changed sets `AllowDraggingRows`. Once armed, `GridCell` calls `ProcessOnDragStarting` on every mouse move until the button comes up, and that logs Syncfusion telemetry each time (`TelemetryService.Log` → `Telemetry.TrackFeature`, a new client and an HTTP post per call, no de-duplication; decompiled from 34.2.7). A read-only grid's drag is refused and the add row has no line to drag, so either would have retried, and posted, on every move of the gesture. Presses on filler rows never reach the cell (`GridFillerRows` handles them).
    - **Every drop is handled by the behaviour, because the grid's own doesn't reorder anything that's saved.** Read from the decompiled `GridRowDragDropController` (Syncfusion 34.2.7): within one grid, `DroppingItemsInDataGrid` removes and re-inserts the `RecordEntry` in `View.Records` only, so the bound `ObservableCollection` never moves. And across grids it moves a row into any other grid whose items are the same type, which the recipe lines and add-ins are (`IngredientLineRow`). So `Drop` is always `Handled`, and the line is moved in the collection with `ObservableCollection<T>.Move`, bracketed by the grid's `GridUndoSteps` recorder. It's a single Move notification, not a remove and an insert, so the row stays selected and a formula-base line never briefly leaves the batch, which would set off the baker's-percentage recomputation twice. `Move` is found by reflection, since the behaviour only knows the collection as an `IList`.
    - **The drop's `TargetRecord` is a record index in the view, not the item** (`ResolveToRecordIndex` of the row dropped on), and the line grids allow sorting, so it's resolved through `View.Records[i].Data` and then found in the collection. A sorted view is unsorted on the drop (`SortColumnDescriptions.Clear()`), so the grid shows the order that was just changed rather than a sort that hides it.
    - **Refused:** a drag from a read-only grid (`AllowEditing` false: a linked section's lines, a linked packaging), a drag in from another grid (`IsFromOutSideSource`, `DragOver` handled so no drop line shows), and a drop on the add row or on the empty rows under it, where `ResolveToRecordIndex` finds no record (-1), so the grid's `GetDropPosition` returns `None` for them itself (the empty rows are only drawn since 2026-10-15, so there's no row there at all). Selection is `Single`, so one line moves at a time.
    - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, with real mouse drags and nothing saved: Sub-Assemblies' lines (up from the row header, down from a cell, and in a view sorted by Grams, which the drop unsorted with the line placed by the lines' own order), the Products Packaging tab, and a made ingredient's Bill of Materials each moved the line, kept it selected, and offered Save and Undo; Undo put the order back and Save went off again. A linked section's lines didn't move, and an add-in dragged onto the recipe lines above it showed no drop line and stayed where it was. Not driven by hand: a packaging set's lines and the Products form's own lines and packaging (the same behaviour on the same kind of grid). That a dragged order survives a save is `LineOrderPersistenceTests`' to show.
  - **A line can be put in above the current one** (2026-10-15, at the user's request: "In the ingredient's line of Sub-Assemblies and Products Sub-Assembly. I want you to implement row inserting if possible"). `controls:GridRowInserting.IsEnabled="True"` (`Controls/GridRowInserting.cs`) is on three grids: Sub-Assemblies' lines, and the Products section form's lines and add-ins. It isn't on the packaging tables or the Bill of Materials, which weren't asked for, though it would work on any grid with a `GridAddNewRow.ItemFactory`.
    - **One routed command, `GridRowInserting.InsertLine`, with a command binding on each grid.** The Insert key is the command's own gesture. The Insert Line or Insert Add-In button under each table reaches it through `CommandTarget="{Binding ElementName=…Grid}"` (`ButtonAdv` is a `ButtonBase`, so it honours that), and Vim's `O` (`VimAction.OpenAbove`) calls `InsertAbove` itself. The command is refused while a cell is being typed into, where Insert is the text box's own key. A button click has already ended that edit, because focus leaving the grid commits it (`GridEditCommit`).
    - **The new line is the grid's `ItemFactory` item**, as the add row's is, so it prices itself and reads its percentage against the current base. It goes in above the line the current cell is on. If the current cell is on the add row, or there isn't one, the line goes in at the bottom. The open edit is committed first with `GridEditCommit.Commit`, so a half-typed add row is added and an untouched one is dropped. The current row is read before that commit, because committing the add row moves the current cell. A sorted view is cleared, as a drag does, because the line goes in at its place in the lines' own order. The insert is bracketed by the grid's `GridUndoSteps` recorder, so it's one undo step. Then the first column (the ingredient picker) is opened at once: the grid is laid out (`UpdateLayout`) so the new line has its row, the current cell is moved onto it and scrolled to, and the cell's edit begins, all before the command returns.
    - **Nothing is left for later, after a stability check on 2026-10-15.** The first version opened the cell at `Background` priority. Three things went wrong in that gap, each caught by a test in `GridRowInsertingTests` that queues input before the dispatcher drains. A key pressed in between, from a fast typist or a held Insert, landed on the line the new one went in above. A burst of Inserts had all its deferred opens work against each other, so none stayed open. And a grid hidden in between, by another recipe tab picked, still had its cell opened. Moving the cell synchronously also fixed a scroll that was worked out before the change was laid out. Now that the new cell is open as the command returns, it takes every key after the Insert, so a held Insert puts in one line and `InsertLine`'s `CanExecute` refuses the repeats.
    - **Measured on 2026-10-15.** On a bare offscreen grid, an insert, including layout and opening the cell, has a median of about 4 ms with 20 lines and with 500; the grid only lays out the rows in view. On Products, against the dev database, a 70-second `dotnet-trace` (`dotnet-sampled-thread-time`) of 30 undos and 20 insert-and-undo cycles kept the UI thread busy for about 0.75 s in all, layout and painting included, and no `GridRowInserting` frame reached the top 400. A heap dump after about 50 inserts, all undone, held 14 live `IngredientLineRow`s: the form's lines and nothing inserted, so nothing leaks. The log had no errors.
    - **A line left blank stays a blank line.** It isn't dropped when it's left, as an untouched add row is. A drop would either be a second undo step or would need the insert's step to stay open across the cell edits that follow, and those open steps of their own. A blank line costs nothing: `IngredientLineRow.CompleteLines` leaves it out at Save, and Undo or Remove Line takes it away. It does mark the form as changed.
    - Covered by `GridRowInsertingTests` (11, in an `OffscreenGrid`: the insert itself, from the add row, the Insert key, a locked grid, undo, a key typed straight after, a held Insert, a half-typed add row, lines replaced under the open cell, a sorted view, and a table scrolled down) and `Capital_o_types_a_new_line_put_in_above_the_current_one` in `VimKeysGridTests`.
  - **Every grid's header row fits its text** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "Increase the height of all the datagrid header to fit the font height"). The Windows11Light grid style fixes `HeaderRowHeight` at 24, sized for its 12px body text; since the fonts went up 2 (App's `BodyFontSize = 14`), a header's text needs 19px plus the header cell's 3px padding above and below and 1px bottom border, so descenders were cut ("Ingredient" read "Inqredient"). `Controls/GridHeaderHeight`, registered once from `App`'s static constructor (class handlers on `SfDataGrid` for `Loaded` and `SizeChanged`), sets each grid's `HeaderRowHeight` (a local value, unless its XAML sets one) from a header cell's own font, padding and border: 26 today. **The header cell's font isn't the grid's**: the theme gives `GridHeaderCellControl` its `BodyTextStyle` (14) while the grid's own `FontSize` stays 12, so it's measured from a realized header cell. A grid hidden when it loads has none, so it's fitted the first time it's sized, once per grid (an attached `IsFitted` flag), which covers the multi-column drop-downs' lists too. Checked by rendering a themed grid off-screen at the app's font sizes (before: the g's descender clipped; after: whole), and covered by `GridHeaderHeightTests`. **A grid whose headings wrap sets `GridHeaderHeight.Lines`** (2026-10-02, for Reports' Product Cost Summary), and its header row fits that many lines (45 for two). Rendering a themed control in the test host needs `PackUriHelper.UriSchemePack` touched and an `Application` created first, or the theme's `pack://` resources throw "The URI prefix is not recognized".
  - **Keyboard focus in a grid sits on a cell, never on the whole grid** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "Keyboard focus must be on the grid instead of the outline. It's absurd and nonstandard"). `Controls/GridKeyboardFocus`, registered once from `App`'s static constructor (class handlers on `SfDataGrid`, so every grid, including future ones, gets it without XAML): as a grid loads its `FocusVisualStyle` is set to null (a local value, unless its XAML sets one), which removes the dotted rectangle WPF drew round the whole grid whenever the grid itself took focus by keyboard (tabbed into, or handed focus back as a cell's edit ended); and when the grid itself takes keyboard focus by keyboard (`InputManager.MostRecentInputDevice`, so a click, which places the current cell where it lands, is left alone), `PlaceCurrentCell` gives a grid without a current cell one in the first column: on the selected row, else the first line, else an empty table's add row (without starting a line). A row-selecting list (`NavigationMode="Row"`: the Sub-Assemblies list, the Settings lists, Price History) only gets one on its selected row, since selecting a row there opens it in the form, which tabbing past mustn't do. Covered by `GridKeyboardFocusTests`; the Tab path itself needs a focused window, so it wasn't verified in the app (only one monitor was connected that day).
  - **`CurrentCellBorderBrush="{StaticResource AccentBrush}"` is set on every `SfDataGrid` in `Views/`, the same way and for the same reason** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: the cell selector outline was black). `SfGridBase` registers the property with a hardcoded `SolidColorBrush(Colors.Black)` default (confirmed by decompiling `Syncfusion.SfGrid.WPF` 34.2.7 with `ilspycmd`) that the Windows11Light accent re-color (`PrimaryBackground` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnStartup`) doesn't reach, so it stays black regardless of theme unless set explicitly. `CurrentCellBorderThickness` (default `2`) is set to `1` too, at the user's request the same day. `1.5` looked identical to the default when checked pixel-by-pixel in a screenshot (both rendered a 2px border at the secondary monitor's 100% scale — `UseLayoutRounding` snaps a fractional thickness up to the next whole device pixel), so only a whole-number step down actually reads as thinner.
  - **`controls:GridFillerRows.IsEnabled="True"`** (`Controls/GridFillerRows.cs`) is on the grids with a height to fill: the Raw Materials, Products and Sub-Assemblies lists, Price History, Settings' Units, Categories and Roles, and the tables lines are typed into (Sub-Assemblies' lines, the Products sub-assembly form and Packaging tab, the Bill of Materials). Not on the recipe section tables, which are sized to their rows, or Alternative Units, whose `MaxHeight` would make it always 240 px tall.
  - **The empty rows are drawn, not added to the grid** (2026-10-15, at the user's request: "check if we can improve blank row padding in datagrid"). `Controls/GridFillerLayer`, a `FrameworkElement` that's never hit-tested, is put in the grid's template: in the root `Grid` that holds the `ScrollViewer` and its `VisualContainer`, across all its rows, so it's drawn and clipped with the grid and under anything over it on the screen (an error toast, the Vim hints). Until then the rows were `GridUnBoundRow`s (below), live rows the grid selected, edited, moved its current cell onto and froze as footers. Most of `GridFillerRows`' 450 lines were guards against that, and the rows still crashed the app twice and drew a second current-cell outline. Drawn, the grid never knows they're there. There are no selection, edit, current-cell or overflow guards, no 150 ms settle delay, and nothing for the Excel export, Vim's `G` or row drag-and-drop to leave out: a click or drop below the rows lands on the grid's own empty space. `GridFillerRows` is the attached property and `FillerRowCount`, and the 23 XAML uses didn't change.
    - **Where:** the rows' space is measured on the row scroll axis as before (`ScrollRows` `RenderSize` against `TotalExtent`), and the columns come from the column scroll axis's visible lines (`ScrollColumns.GetVisibleLines()`: `Origin`/`Corner` where a column's cells are, `ClippedOrigin`/`ClippedCorner` what's shown), so the lines follow column widths, the row header and horizontal scrolling.
    - **Down to the grid's bottom edge, the last row cut off by it** (2026-10-16, at the user's request: "is there room for improvement?"). `FillerRowCount` rounds up, less a hair for fractional layout, so a part of a row left at the bottom gets a row the layer's viewport clip cuts off, as the grid cuts its own last row. Until then it rounded down to whole rows, a rule kept from the unbound rows, where a part of one would have made the grid scroll; that left a strip up to a row high where the column lines stopped short of the edge.
    - **When:** on the grid's `LayoutUpdated` while it's loaded and shown (`IsVisible`), `GridFillerLayer` works out a `FillerLayout` and, only when it differs from the last, replaces its `DrawingGroup`'s content. That redraws without `InvalidateVisual`, so no layout pass follows, and the padding changes in the same layout pass as the grid: records added or removed, a group expanded (`ExpandAllGroup` raises nothing either), the window resized. A new template (a theme change) leaves the layer in the old one, so it's checked and re-inserted each time. `LayoutUpdated` is raised after every layout pass in the app, not only the grid's, so a grid that's loaded but not shown (in a collapsed panel) skips the work (2026-10-16); showing it again is a layout pass, which brings it up to date.
    - **What it draws, read from the decompiled theme (Syncfusion 35.1.40) and checked pixel by pixel.** A theme cell is a `Border` with `BorderThickness` 0,0,1,1 in `BorderAlt`, which is also the grid's own `BorderBrush`, and `SnapsToDevicePixels`, which gives the cell `UIElement` snapping guidelines at its own bounds only. So each empty cell is drawn as that border's shape: a strip down its right side and one along its bottom that stops short of it, under a `GuidelineSet` of that cell's own edges. The strips' inner edges fall unsnapped, 1 px inside, so at 125% a line is a full pixel and a faint quarter, as the real cells' are. Three first attempts each showed in the comparison:
      - one guideline set for the whole drawing snapped both edges of some lines (2 px wide at 125%) and none of others;
      - whole-row and whole-column rectangles overlapped where they cross, and the theme's brush is see-through, so the crossings came out darker;
      - a clip per column at a fractional edge shaved the line it cut.
      Now a column is clipped only on a side scrolled under the row header, never on the grid's own edge, which the viewport's clip already takes.
    - **Tests.** `GridFillerRowsDrawingTests` renders a themed grid padded with drawn rows beside the same grid holding that many blank records, at 96 and 120 dpi, with and without a row header, scrolled sideways under it, and under an add row (2026-10-16), and compares them pixel by pixel. A grid can't hold records below its add row, so for that case the blank grid has no add row and one more blank record in its place, and only the pixel rows wholly under the add row are compared. Both grids run past the bottom edge, the blank one's vertical scroll bar hidden so it isn't narrowed, and the test checks the last drawn row is cut off. It allows 2 levels in 255, for the one pixel column where the last column's line meets the grid's right edge at 125%, and 4 in the one pixel row where the bottom edge cuts the last row partway across: the grid cuts its row with a `Clip` on the row element, the layer with one in its drawing, and they cover that pixel 3 or 4 levels apart where a line crosses it at 125%. (Setting the layer's own `Clip` instead, to match, was tried: it moved the right-edge line by 7 levels at 96 dpi.) `GridFillerRowsLayoutTests` checks that the grid has no unbound rows and that the padding follows lines added and removed, and a resize, in the same layout pass, and that a hidden grid's padding is left alone and brought up to date when it's shown. `GridFillerRowsGroupedTests` and `GridFillerRowsCurrentCellTests` are kept and still pass. The latter's two tests of the add row's current cell when its lines go were moved to `GridAddNewRowCurrentCellTests` (2026-10-16), without the padding, since that's `GridAddNewRow`'s fix (below), not the padding's.
    - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved. Raw Materials' Purchase Cost Setup and Price History, Sub-Assemblies' lines and the Products section's lines were padded, and zoomed in, the real add row's line and the drawn ones below it couldn't be told apart. A click on the padding with a Purchase Price edit open left the edit and its current cell as they were, and Esc left nothing to save. Shrinking and growing the window, captured 100 ms later, showed the padding already fitted.
  - **A current cell on the add row stays on it when the lines above it go** (`UntouchedAddRowSelectionController.PutCurrentCellBackOnAddRow` in `Controls/GridAddNewRow.cs`, 2026-10-15). When the last lines go while the add row's cell is current (Ctrl+Z back to no lines, another material with none), SfDataGrid moves the add row up but leaves `CurrentRowColumnIndex` where the add row was. The add row keeps its outline, but the next key moves from the stale index: Left, Right and Tab each left no cell current. `ProcessSourceCollectionChanged` notes whether the current cell was on the add row and, one `Background` dispatcher pass later, moves it back if it's now past the add row (with the add row at the bottom, the last row it can be on). Until the add row is laid out, the grid can't find its cell and notes only the index. `GridFillerRows.MoveCurrentCellOffFillerRow` used to do this for padded grids, since a filler row sat at that index, and dropping it with the unbound rows broke the keys, which the new `A_key_after_every_line_is_removed_moves_along_the_add_row` caught (the same keys without the clear pass). It and `Removing_every_line_while_the_add_row_is_current_leaves_one_current_cell_on_the_add_row` are in `GridAddNewRowCurrentCellTests`. Every grid with an add row sets `GridAddNewRow.DropWhenUntouched`, so every one has the fix.
  - **Superseded (2026-10-15): the unbound rows, 2026-09-15 to 2026-10-15.** Until the rows were drawn (above), like the sample they were `GridUnBoundRow`s, recalculated when the grid loads, resizes, its view's records change, or a group expands or collapses. **What differs was read from the decompiled Syncfusion 34.2.7 (ilspycmd):**
