    - **`ShowBelowSummary = true`.** `GridIndexResolver.ResolveUnboundRowToRowIndex` and `GridAddNewRowController.GetAddNewRowIndex` put bottom unbound rows *without* it before the add-new row, which would have sunk the add row below the filler rows. With it they're the grid's last rows.
    - **Those rows are frozen footers** (`UpdateFreezePaneRows` counts them in `FooterRows`). With a short body they follow it (`ScrollAxisBase.GetVisibleLines` starts footers at `Max(HeaderExtent, ViewSize − FooterExtent)`, and `PixelScrollAxis.ViewSize` is `Min(RenderSize, TotalDistance)`), but rows that no longer fit would be pinned over the grid's last rows. So rows that don't fit are removed at once, synchronously, when records are added, a group expands or the grid shrinks, **and after any layout pass that finds them overflowing** (see the crash notes below); adding rows waits for layout (one `Background`-priority recalculation however many triggers ask) and, when the grid grows, for 150 ms of calm.
    - **The space is measured on the row scroll axis:** `GetVisualContainer().ScrollRows` (`PixelScrollAxis`) `RenderSize` against `TotalExtent`, which counts the header, group captions and add-new row as drawn, in O(1). `GridFillerRows.FillerRowCount` (unit-tested) floored to whole rows, so filler never added a scrollbar (it rounds up now the rows are drawn, above).
    - **Only the difference is added or removed, never `Clear()`**: `UnBoundRows`' Reset handling (and `ReCalculateUnboundRowsCount`) counts rows with `Select(cond).Count()`, which counts every row.
    - **The rows are inert.** Unbound rows are selectable, and in a single-selection list a click on one deselects the record, which clears the form beside it; in a table with `EditTrigger="OnTap"` a click anywhere begins editing the current cell (`GridCurrentCellManager.ProcessOnTapped`). A press on a filler row is handled in `PreviewMouseDown`, and `SelectionChanging`/`CurrentCellActivating` are cancelled for one (keyboard). Filler rows are recognised by position (the last rows of `VisualContainer.RowCount`), not with `SfDataGrid.GetUnBoundRow`: an unbound row's `RowIndex` isn't kept up to date as records arrive, and a first version using it swallowed clicks on the Ingredients group caption.
      - **Only a press in the grid's own window counts** (2026-09-17, reported by the user: a packaging material picked with the mouse never showed in its cell). A drop-down cell's list is a `Popup`, a window of its own but inside the grid's tree, so its presses tunnel through the grid's `PreviewMouseDown` too, and the filler-row check was by position alone. Where the list opens below its cell it lies over the filler rows, so a click on an item was taken for a click on a filler row and stopped. The list's own `GridCell` then never recorded the press (`GridSelectionController.ProcessPointerPressed`), the release selected nothing, and `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl.OnInternalGridTapped` committed no item. Opened above (the sub-assembly form, lower on the screen) it worked, which is what gave it away. Now the press must come from the grid's own `PresentationSource`. It hit every filler-row grid with a drop-down; found with a temporary trace of the press's `Handled` flag down the route. Verified in the app: a material picked with the mouse from a list opened below the add row, and a click on a filler row still selecting nothing.
    - **Changing unbound rows reaches the selection** (`GridSelectionController.ProcessUnBoundRowChanged`): it ends an edit of an existing row without committing it, and adding one while the current cell is on the row above the add-new row moves the current cell onto that row. Nothing changes while `View.IsEditingItem` (tried again 150 ms later), and a current cell moved by an addition is put back (`MoveCurrentCell`) — **only from the add-new row, and only onto a record's cell that still exists** (see the crash notes below).
    - **A current cell left on a filler row is put back on the add-new row** (2026-09-25, reported by the user: "sometimes two cell selection is being shown simultaneously" on Raw Materials' Purchase Cost Setup). When the last lines go while the current cell is on the add-new row, SfDataGrid moves the add-new row up but leaves `CurrentRowColumnIndex` at the old row index. Without filler rows nothing is there; with them a filler row is, and the grid outlines a cell on it as well as keeping the add-new row cell's outline, and every later click moves only the filler row's outline. Ctrl+Z back to no brands did it (`UndoShortcuts` commits the grid's edit, then `RestoreSnapshot` clears `BrandPriceRows`), and so would any clear of a typed-into table with the add-new row current. `FillerState.MoveCurrentCellOffFillerRow` moves it onto the add-new row one dispatcher pass after `Apply`: straight after rows are added the grid can't find the add-new row's cell yet, notes only the index, and the next move then leaves that cell outlined. Test: `Removing_every_line_while_the_add_row_is_current_leaves_one_current_cell_on_the_add_row` (fails on the old code). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real clicks and keystrokes, nothing saved: a new material's first brand typed in, Ctrl+Z from the add-new row's Purchase Price, then clicks on Buffer % and Brand each left one outline.
  - **Two crashes it caused, fixed 2026-09-16** (reported as "sometimes the program crashes"; neither could be reproduced by clicking around). The evidence was the Windows Application event log (`.NET Runtime`, event 1026, which keeps the full stack of an unhandled exception) and the app's own Serilog file (`logs/tinapayanrms-<date>.log` beside the exe), whose warnings showed what the user did just before. Both were then reproduced by **fuzzing a real `SfDataGrid`** off-screen: random sequences of what the screens do to a grid, with the app's own behaviours attached, until one threw. The fuzz harnesses were throwaway; the sequences they found are the tests.
    - **"InValid RowColumnIndex" from `FillerState.Apply`** (twice on the Products screen, each shortly after an add-in was refused). The grid keeps the current cell of a row that's gone: select a line (the grid's `SelectedItem`, which `SelectedFormLine` binds), then replace the lines (another sub-assembly's form), and the current cell still points at the old row index. Filler rows added to the emptier table make the grid drop it (`ProcessUnBoundRowChanged`), and `Apply` moved it back — to a row past `GetLastNavigatingRowIndex`, which `CanMoveCurrentCell` rejects by throwing, from a `Background` dispatcher callback, so nothing caught it. **The move back now only undoes the one move it exists for**: the current cell pushed from a record onto the add-new row, and only onto a record's cell that still exists (`ResolveToRecordIndex`, the visible column range). `GridFillerRowsCurrentCellTests` fails on the old code with the same exception.
    - **`ArgumentOutOfRangeException` from `GroupDisplayElements.GetItemAt` during layout** (the grouped Raw Materials list). Fuzzing the list as `MaterialListCard` sets it up — grouped, name-sorted, `AllowDataShaping`, fed by a DynamicData `SortAndBind` with the ViewModel's merge-then-reselect refresh — crashed within 15 steps with filler rows on, and never in the identical sequence with them off. The row counts always added up (header + group display elements + filler rows); what preceded every crash was **filler rows left overflowing**: `ExpandAllGroup` (and `CollapseAllGroup`) raise no `GroupExpanded`/`GroupCollapsed` and change no records, so nothing told `GridFillerRows` the rows had grown, and frozen footer rows were left over the body until a later refresh broke row generation. Deferring the handlers (so they ran after the notification) changed nothing. **A `LayoutUpdated` check now takes away rows that no longer fit after any layout** — two numbers compared, so it's cheap on every grid. With it, 30 seeds × 70 steps of the list and 12 × 60 of an editable lines table ran with no crash and no filler row left overflowing after layout. `GridFillerRowsGroupedTests` pins the overflow (it fails on the old code); the crash itself only ever followed it.
    - **Grid tests run in a real window**: `Fixtures/OffscreenGrid` shows an `SfDataGrid` off-screen on an STA thread of its own and pumps the dispatcher, so an exception the grid throws from a dispatcher callback surfaces in the test as it would have closed the app. It needs `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` (an unlicensed control shows a dialog) and skips without it. **These classes share one xUnit collection, `OffscreenGridCollection`, so they run one at a time** (2026-09-28). While each class was its own collection, their windows were up at once on separate STA threads, and a different current-cell or focus test failed on almost every full run, though each passed on its own. **The themed grid tests joined it on 2026-10-25** (`CheckboxFilterFillTests`, `GridFillerRowsDrawingTests`, `GridHeaderAlignmentTests`, `TreeGridThemeScopeTests`, all on `StaThread.RunThemed`): Syncfusion keeps whether the current cell has been validated in one static flag for the whole process (`ValidationHelper.IsCurrentCellValidated`). `GridEditCommit.Commit` ends an edit through `GridCurrentCellManager.EndEdit`, which validates the cell only while that flag is false, and the validation is the one place an Explicit binding (`AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer`'s) writes its value. A themed grid beside an edit could set the flag, so the commit skipped it and the pick never reached the row: `GridAddNewRowTabTests`' retyped product stayed 1, about one full run in three. Traced by logging the picker's `SelectedItem`, `SelectedValue` and `Text` with their callers: the pick was in place, and the editor was unloaded at once rather than after a validation. The app has one UI thread and one edit at a time, so it never met this. Every test class that builds a real `SfDataGrid` or `SfTreeGrid` goes in this collection.
  - **Found on the way, not caused by it:** Raw Materials' groups start collapsed although the grid has `AutoExpandGroups`. Syncfusion.Data expands a new top-level group only while grouping is being applied (`CollectionView.IsInGroup`), and the list loads empty, its records arriving later through `LoadCommand`.
  - **Verified in the app** (the unbound rows, 2026-09-16) on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved:
    - lists filled to the bottom with vertical lines and no scrollbar; a search down to one match filled more rows; expanding Ingredients removed them all (the list scrolled) and collapsing brought them back, with the form (New, or Cake Flour) untouched;
    - clicking a filler row changed nothing;
    - in Sub-Assemblies' lines table, a committed line kept the add row right under it, Down from the add row stayed put, a click below started no edit, and deleting the line brought a filler row back;
    - the Products list and form, Price History, and the Settings dialog's lists (opened twice, padded once) were padded; the recipe Total row's dividers lined up;
    - shrinking the window showed no filler rows over the data even 60 ms later, and maximizing refilled.
- **Dropped: the Price Simulator's Set Current Prices, and its whole-category and costing changes** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: "drop this feature, it's confusing"). Both were added the same day (commits 154c667 and 17e2aa1) and were reverted whole: the Price to Charge column and Set Current Prices (`IProductRepository.SetCurrentPricesAsync`, `VersionedSaves.SetCurrentPricesAsync`), and Whole Category and the Costing row (`SimulatedCosting`, `PriceChangeRules`' VAT overload). The simulator is back to raw material price changes and scenarios. A product's Current Price is set on the Products screen only. No migration was involved.
- **A performance pass over the code** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: "check the codebase for code that can be refactored to improve performance, run the analyzer"). A rebuild with every performance rule on (`-p:AnalysisLevelPerformance=latest-all`) found only CA1849, none worth changing but `PrintTabViewModel.PrepareAsync`'s `Cancel()`, now `await CancelAsync()`. The dotnet-diag performance skill's scan found no critical pattern (no culture-sensitive string search, sync-over-async, `new Regex` or uncached JSON options), and these were applied: every class in `src` that isn't a base class sealed (132 of them; `AppWindow` is the base of the windows); `TryGetValue` in place of `ContainsKey` and the indexer in `CostDriverAnalysis`, `MaterialCostShares`, `ProductsViewModel.ReadScalingProductsAsync` and `RecipeScaler`; `FrozenDictionary` for `BackupRules.Suffixes` and `StandardUnitRules.ByName`, the latter looked up by span (`GetAlternateLookup`) so a unit's trimmed name isn't allocated; `AsSpan` for `BackupRules`' archive date; and `ProductVersionContent`'s JSON source-generated (`ProductVersionJsonContext`, same settings), checked against all 311 versions in the dev database, which read and wrote identically before and after. LINQ (849 calls) was left alone: none is on a path where it measurably costs anything.
- **A saved plan has a day, and records what was made** (retired with the Production Plan on 2026-10-31; TinapayanBMS does both now) (2026-10-29, at the user's request: plans by date, record actual results). `SavedPlan.MakeOn` (`DateOnly?`) and `SavedPlanLine.MadeUnits`/`LeftoverUnits` (`decimal?`), one migration, `SavedPlanDayAndResults`, with `CK_SavedPlanLines_MadeUnits` (0 or more) and `CK_SavedPlanLines_LeftoverUnits` (0 to made, and only with it), the same in `SavedPlanRules.EnsureValid`. `ISavedPlanRepository.SaveAsync` takes `makeOn`, and `GetAllAsync` orders by `coalesce(make_on, saved_at::date)` newest first. On the Production Plan: **Make On** (an `SfDatePicker` with `AllowNull`, and **No Day**, `ClearMakeOnCommand`) over the Plan table, which gains **Made** and **Left Over** columns (`PlanLineRow.MadeUnits`/`LeftoverUnits`); all three are kept in the view state (`production-plan-make-on`, and `"id:units:made:left"` in the plan), saved with a plan and part of `PlanContents`, so a result typed after saving is an unsaved change. A result typed only keeps the plan; it changes nothing to make or buy. The left card is 720 wide (was 600) for the two columns, and the Saved Plans list shows **Make On** first. **The plan for today opens by itself** on the first load when the table is empty and no plan is open (`_lookForTodaysPlan`): not when the table holds something, which it does whenever the app ran with a plan in it. A day of the week ("every Saturday") wasn't built: a plan without a day serves that. **Reports → Production Results** (`ProductionResultsAnalysis`, `ProductionResultRow`) adds the lines with Made typed up by product category in pieces (`RecipeScalingRules.PiecesFor`, so boxes and pieces add up alike): plans, planned, made, Short % of planned, left over and Left Over % of made, beside the category's Wastage % (the products' `ProductCostSummary.WastagePercent`), and a Note when Short % is over it by a tenth of a point or more. Category rows only, not product rows; a deleted product's lines are left out. Tests: `SavedPlanPersistenceTests` (day and results, refused results), `ProductionPlanViewModelTests.A_plans_day_and_results_are_saved_with_it_kept_for_next_time_and_opened_again` and `The_plan_saved_for_today_opens_by_itself_when_the_table_is_empty_and_only_then`, `ProductionResultsAnalysisTests`.
- **A purchased material can be costed at a brand picked instead of the dearest** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: cost by preferred brand). `RawMaterial.PreferredBrand` (nullable, 100 characters; migration `RawMaterialPreferredBrand`), kept trimmed for a purchased material and null for any other (`RawMaterialRepository.StoredPreferredBrand`), is **Cost From** on the Purchase Cost Setup card (`EditingPreferredBrand`, `CostFrom`/`CostFromOptions`, `ViewModels/CostFromOption`; in the form snapshot, so Undo takes it back). `RawMaterialRules.FindCostBrandPrice` picks the price the cost comes from: the dearest per base unit of that brand's prices (matched as brands are), else the dearest of all, which is also the fallback when the brand picked has no price left. It's the one rule `ApplyBrandPrices` (so the Save, `RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.SetAsync` and a unit's new multiplier), the price history entry `RepriceAsync` logs, `PurchaseUnit.Of` (the Price Simulator's current price and the Production Plan's Buy unit) and the form's live figures (`CostBrandIndex`) all use, so nothing downstream of `CostPerBaseUnit` changed: the cascade starts from it as before. The Raw Materials Save always sends a purchased material's brand prices, so a change of Cost From alone reprices it and cascades. **Average was not built** (the user had asked for "a chosen brand or the average"): an average comes from no one brand price, and price history, the simulator's current price and the Buy unit each need the one price the cost comes from. Wording that said "dearest brand" now says the brand it's costed at, and Reports' Cost Buffers column is **Brand Cost**. Found on the way: an Undo left the Cost Buffer % box showing the percent from before it (`TypedCostBufferPercent` is only raised outside a restore); `RestoreSnapshot` now raises it. Tests: `RawMaterialRulesTests.A_preferred_brand_is_the_cost_its_dearest_unit_when_priced_in_more_than_one` and `ApplyBrandPrices_costs_a_material_at_its_preferred_brand`, `RawMaterialSavePersistenceTests.A_preferred_brand_costs_the_material_and_what_its_made_into_and_dropping_it_goes_back_to_the_dearest`, `RawMaterialsViewModelUndoTests.Cost_From_costs_the_material_at_the_brand_picked_and_undoing_it_puts_the_buffer_box_back`.
- **A product category can set its own Wastage %** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: wastage per category). `ProductCategory.WastagePercent`, nullable (`CK_ProductCategories_WastagePercent`, 0 to 100, `CostingRules.EnsureValidWastage` in the repository; migration `ProductCategoryWastagePercent`), a **Wastage %** box and column on Settings → Product Categories (blank for the shop-wide one). A product is priced with its category's figure, else `CostingDefaults.WastagePercent`: `SavedProductReader` reads them with `IProductRepository.GetCategoryWastageForProductsAsync` (one query a read) and stamps `ProductDetail.WastagePercent`, so Reports, Print, the Price Simulator, the Production Plan and versions follow; the Products form prices with `EditingCategory?.WastagePercent ?? _wastagePercent` and recomputes when the category picked changes. A composite uses its own category's figure on everything it holds, never its products' (they bring their cost before wastage, as before). A version keeps the figure it was priced with, as before. Tests: `ProductCategoryPersistenceTests.A_categorys_own_wastage_is_kept_read_for_its_products_and_held_to_0_to_100`, `SavedProductReaderBulkTests.A_product_whose_category_sets_its_own_wastage_is_priced_with_it_and_the_rest_with_the_shop_wides`, `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.A_category_with_its_own_wastage_prices_the_product_put_in_it_at_once`.
- **Find, Ctrl+K, looks up any record by name from anywhere** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: "search everything"). The title bar's magnifier (before Back) or Ctrl+K opens `Views/QuickFindWindow`, a modal dialog over `ViewModels/QuickFindViewModel`: the names of every raw material, standalone sub-assembly and undeleted product, read afresh each time it opens, matched word by word ignoring case and accents, best first (`QuickFindViewModel.Match`), at most 50, with each record's kind and category or role. Enter or a double-click opens the pick on its own screen through `MainWindow.OpenOnAsync`, as the links between screens do, so the screen left asks about its unsaved changes first. Names only, not categories or descriptions; product-owned sub-assemblies aren't listed (they open with their product). No Vim binding of its own: the Vim keys see a text box, so the dialog types in Insert, and Esc closes it from Normal. Tests: `QuickFindViewModelTests`.
- **Automatic backups are checked every hour and can be copied to a second folder** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: safer backups). `ViewModels/AutomaticBackups` takes the day's backup if it's due (`IDatabaseBackups.BackUpDailyIfDueAsync`) when the window shows and on an hourly `DispatcherTimer` in `App` (`CheckEvery`), so a copy left open over midnight takes the next day's. When the user has chosen a second folder (Settings → Database → Also Copy To, `OpenFolderDialog`; kept per Windows user under `backup-copy-folder` in the view-state file), each new automatic backup is copied there (`CopyToAsync`) and that folder's automatic backups beyond the newest 14 are deleted (`BackupRules.AutomaticToDelete` over the files `BackupRules.TryParse` names; nothing else there is touched). A folder that isn't there, or a copy that fails, is logged and shown in amber on the Database tab (`LastCopyProblem`), and the backup itself still stands. Choosing the folder copies the newest automatic backup at once. `DatabaseBackupsViewModel` takes the service as an optional last parameter. Tests: `AutomaticBackupsTests`, with a stand-in `IDatabaseBackups` writing files (taking a real backup is `DatabaseBackupsTests`').
- **A backup can't hang, pass for a whole one, or fail unnoticed** (2026-11-03, at the user's request, from a code review). `PostgresTools.RunAsync` gives every run `--no-password` and a timeout (`DefaultTimeout`, half an hour, then `TimeoutException`). With no password available, `pg_dump` waited at a prompt on a console nobody could see, for ever, holding `DatabaseBackups._turn`, so Back Up Now and Restore waited behind it (reproduced with `PGPASSFILE` pointing nowhere). `--no-password` goes first, since `pg_restore`'s file comes last and older tools stop reading options there; only `pg_dump --version` minds, which nothing but a test ran. `pg_dump` makes its file before it connects, so a run cut short left a file under the backup's own name, which counted as the day's backup and took one of the 14 places kept. It now writes `<name>.backup.partial` (`BackupRules.PartialSuffix`), named by `File.Move` once it has finished. `List` only matches `*.backup`, so a partial file is never listed or counted; `FreePath` won't reuse its name; and `DeleteAbandonedPartials` removes one over a day old (`PartialAbandonedAfter`, longer than a tool may run, so it's never one another copy of the app is writing). `CopyAsync` does the same, flushed to disk first, so a USB stick pulled out part-way leaves the file it would have replaced as it was. `AutomaticBackups` now keeps what went wrong in the view-state file (`backup-problem`, `backup-copy-problem`), so a restart can't make backups look fine, and the Database tab shows it in amber (`BackupProblemText`, `CopyProblemText`). `OverdueWarningAsync` also says so in a message, once a run, but only when the last automatic backup failed and the newest backup of this database is three days old or more (`BackupRules.Newest`, which leaves out one copied in since its name dates when it arrived, `IsOverdue`, `OverdueAfter`): a single failure may well put itself right at the next hourly check. Tests: `DatabaseBackupsTests` (a real `pg_dump` with no password, and one blocked by a table lock until its timeout), `PostgresToolsTests`, `AutomaticBackupsTests`, `BackupRulesTests`.
- **A form with unsaved changes is never left without asking** (2026-10-29, at the user's request: "when there is a modification on both new and old products, subassemblies or raw materials, don't leave that tab until its been save"; the user chose a Save / Don't Save / Cancel question over a hard block or saving by itself, and kept Print exempt). Until then a change of tab kept the edits, but picking another record, New, a link, Clone and closing the app threw them away unasked.
  - **The question.** `ViewModelBase.ConfirmUnsavedChanges` (`Interaction<string, UnsavedChangesAnswer>`), answered by `RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler` with a Yes / No / Cancel `MessageBox` ("Save the changes to Pandesal?"); `AskAboutUnsavedChangesAsync` asks only while `History.IsDirty`, turns away a second question while one is up, and reads a Save as done when `History.IsDirty` is false after `SaveCommand`, so a refused Save keeps the user on the form with its error. Whatever fails on the way shows on the form, which isn't left (2026-11-04): Don't Save reading the record back with the database down threw out to a move nothing awaited, so the click did nothing, with no word of why (reproduced on all three screens, `LoadFailureTests`).
  - **Each screen asks itself** (`IUnsavedChangesScreen`, on `ProductsViewModel`, `RawMaterialsViewModel` and `SubAssembliesViewModel`) before its form shows another record: a row picked in the list (`TreeSelectionChanged` hands it to `PickAfterAskingAsync`, which yields so the tree finishes its change, puts the list back on the record showing, then asks), New and the list's New … Here, `:e`, the `Open*Async` methods the links use, and on Products Clone, Create Variant, Restore from the Deleted folder and Sub-Assemblies' New Product (`StartNewProductFromAsync`). Don't Save there needs no undoing: the next record replaces the form. Sub-Assemblies' Copy doesn't ask, since the copy holds the edits.
  - **The main window asks before a screen is left, through `ScreenNavigator`** (`ViewModels/`, moved out of `MainWindow` on 2026-11-04, at the user's request, from a code review, since `MainWindow` can't be built in a test without a live `App`). It knows screens, and `MainWindow` tabs: it's handed each `NavigationScreen`, which screen is showing, and how to show one. Print's `NavigationScreen` has Products' form, so moving between those two asks nothing (Print shows the unsaved edits by design). `TabControlExt` has no selection change to cancel, so `TurnBackToUnsaved` selects the tab left again inside `SelectionChanged` (under `_isTurningBack`, recording and loading nothing), asks on the dispatcher, and shows the tab picked once the changes are saved or thrown away; Don't Save then reverts the screen (`ResolveUnsavedChangesAsync(revert: true)`: Products' Revert without its confirmation, Raw Materials re-reading the material, whose alternative units write through to the list's copy, Sub-Assemblies re-reading its lines), so the edits aren't there to come back to. That covers the tab strip, the menu, `gt`/`gT`, the screen commands, and the tab showing closed with its ✕ (it's hidden before the selection moves, so it's shown again, and hidden after). Links ask first in `OpenOnAsync`; Back and Forward peek at their target (`NavigationHistory.BackTarget`/`ForwardTarget`) and ask before stepping, so a Cancel leaves the history as it was. `OnClosing` cancels a close while any screen is dirty and asks about each, the one showing first, each shown as it's asked about, except when the app is closing to start again after a restore (`App.IsRestarting`). A form answered for while closing isn't asked about again until that close ends: Don't Save leaves it unsaved for the app to close over, and showing the next screen read that as leaving it (found 2026-11-04, in the real app: No to the first of two forms asked about the second with the first still showing, then about the first again).
  - **Nothing awaits a move, since it starts from a click, so nothing that fails in one may be left in its task** (2026-11-04, at the user's request, from a code review). A failure there only reached `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException`, a warning in the log when the task was collected. `ScreenNavigator`'s moves never fault: they hand what failed to `showFailure`, and `MainWindow` puts it back on the dispatcher, so App's handler says what went wrong, as it does for a tab picked directly. The screens' own (`_ = RunSafelyAsync(...)` for a list pick and `:e`, and `CommitCategoryTextAsync`) show it in their error notices. `FetchCostsAsync` and `ReadScalingProductsAsync` already catch their reads.
  - Tests: `ScreenNavigatorTests` (every move and failure above, against forms that answer as the test says), `*ViewModelTreeTests` on each screen (a pick answered Cancel, Save and Don't Save; New; Products' Clone; Don't Save on leaving reverts; what's typed after Don't Save on a new record counts, which on Products it didn't, since `RevertToSavedAsync` cleared what the blank form is measured against, found 2026-11-04) and `NavigationHistoryTests.BackTarget_and_ForwardTarget_say_where_a_step_would_go_without_taking_it`. `MainWindow`'s own wiring still has no test, as it can't be built without a live `App`, whose styles would reach every other test in the process: it was walked in the real app on 2026-11-04 through a `DOTNET_STARTUP_HOOKS` assembly, answering only No and Cancel, nothing saved: a tab picked, Back and Forward, each kind of link, Print, closing with one form and with two, and a move that fails. A tab's ✕ wasn't driven.
- **A tab keeps its view state while the app runs, and still picks up changes made on other tabs.** Nothing is saved to disk; the state lasts for the session only.
  - **Tab content stays loaded.** `MainTabControl` has `IsDisableUnloadTabItemExtContent="True"`. By default `TabControlExt` unloads a tab's content on every switch; measured off-screen, content was unloaded twice and re-loaded three times over four switches, and never unloaded with the property set. So scroll position, sorting, sub-tabs, expanders, focus and typed text survive a switch without any code.
  - **A tab's screen is made the first time it's shown** (2026-10-22, for startup time; see "Startup time" above). `MainWindow` holds a factory per tab (`_screenFactories`) and `CreateScreen` runs it from `MainTabControl.SelectionChanged`. That's in time: `TabControlExt.OnSelectionChanged` raises the event (`base.OnSelectionChanged`) before its `UpdateSelectedItem` makes the content presenter for the selected tab, which it finds by the tab's `Content`. A presenter made while the content was still null would stay in the panel, empty, beside the one made later. The control also does that from `LayoutUpdated` and its generator's status change, before any `SelectionChanged`, so the first open tab's screen is made in the constructor. Nothing reaches a view before its tab shows: each view's interaction handlers and command subscriptions serve only its own screen, `ProductPrintView` reads `PrintSet` through `WhenAnyValue`, which hands it the current set on subscribing, and a link from another screen calls the target ViewModel's `Open*` and then shows the tab. Checked in the app on the secondary monitor: each tab, the menu's Reports, Production Plan and Price Simulator, and a Reports double-click opening its product on a Products tab not yet made.
  - **One load trigger: tab activation.** Views don't load data on `Loaded`. `MainWindow.xaml.cs` maps each `TabItemExt` to its screen's `LoadCommand` and runs it for the tab shown at startup and on each `MainTabControl.SelectionChanged`. The Print tab's is a command of the window's own, which lays out the product Products has open (see the Products screen's Print). It ignores bubbled events whose `OriginalSource` isn't `MainTabControl`, and skips a tab whose refresh is still running.
  - **Every `LoadCommand` is a fetch-then-merge, never a clear-and-reload.** Clearing a list is what used to lose the selected row, picker choices and Products' unsaved rows on every return.
    1. All `await`s finish first.
    2. The ids of the selected row, every picker and the lookup-panel selection are recorded. A ComboBoxAdv can clear its own `SelectedItem` when its item is replaced mid-merge, so the ids must be read before merging.
    3. Inside `ApplyRefresh` (`ViewModelBase`, which sets `IsRefreshing`), the lists are merged with `CollectionMerge.MergeFrom`, and each selection and picker is re-pointed by id.
  - **`CollectionMerge`** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ViewModels/CollectionMerge.cs`) keeps the existing object for every row whose values didn't change, which is what a grid's selection and scroll and a picker's `SelectedItem` are tied to. A `SourceCache` merge uses DynamicData's `EditDiff`; an `ObservableCollection` is synced in place, by a key of any type (since 2026-10-24, for the Production Plan's mixes, keyed by name), rows sharing a key matched in order. `HasSameValues` compares scalar properties plus those of referenced entities one level deep (so a renamed category counts as a change to an ingredient showing it). A collection the row owns (a raw material's `Units`) counts as part of the row: its elements are compared in order, the same way as the row, so an added unit — or a unit of measure renamed in Settings — replaces the row. A screen whose selected row is replaced that way rebuilds the unit rows and pickers derived from it, keeping picks by id.
  - **Selection-driven subscriptions skip while `IsRefreshing`**: form loads, "reset the detail fields" blocks, detail reloads, lookup-panel form loads, and Products' role and packaging-material pickers. Re-pointing a selection at its refreshed copy must not reload the form or throw away unsaved edits. `ReportsViewModel` isn't a `ViewModelBase` and has no selection to re-point: its Load replaces both summaries outright.
  - **Products** also rebuilds its unsaved usage and packaging rows with the refreshed sub-assemblies, materials and units (`RefreshUnsavedRows`), keeping each row's numbers, so costs changed on other tabs reach the recipe sections and the cost summary. Since 2026-09-14 it also refetches the lines of every sub-assembly the recipe uses (`GetForSubAssembliesAsync`, one query), and rebuilds only a row whose sub-assembly or lines changed (`CollectionMerge.HasSameValues`). It also refreshes the selected product's tab links.
    - **Since sub-assemblies became owned (2026-09-14)** it's `RefreshRecipeRows`: a section of the product's own keeps its unsaved lines and is only repriced with the refreshed ingredients; a linked section is refetched (`ISubAssemblyRepository.GetByIdsAsync` and its lines, one query each), since its owner may have changed its lines, name or role there; each keeps its own piece size, and the sections are re-sorted if a role or name moved one. Who links the product's own sub-assemblies ("Also used by …") and its packaging link are refreshed too.
  - **Save, Delete and the lookup panels call the same `LoadCommand`**, so an existing record stays selected after saving, and renaming a category or role updates the grid.
  - Covered by `tests/TinapayanRMS.UI.Tests/ViewModels/`: `CollectionMergeTests`, plus Ingredients and Products refresh tests that build the ViewModel on real repositories.
  - **The Production Plan merges too** (2026-10-24, at the user's request: "Improve the performance of the Production Plan feature"). Measured against the dev database (51 products): the load's reads took 40–60 ms warm, off the UI thread, and the plan's arithmetic about 3 ms for all 51 products, so neither was changed. The cost was on the UI thread. Every edit and every showing of the tab cleared and refilled the Mixing Sheet and the Purchase Requisition. In an offscreen grid of 90 rows that took a median of 29 ms per table, against 0.2 ms for a merge with nothing changed and 4 ms with three rows changed. A load also re-pointed each line by setting its `ProductId`, which wrote the view-state file and started a recalculation for every line. Four changes:
    - Both tables are merged with `CollectionMerge.MergeFrom`, a mix by its name and a material by its id.
    - A load puts the lines in or re-points them (`PlanLineRow.FindProduct`) with `_restoring` set, then keeps and works out the plan once.
    - The picker's `Products` list is replaced only when a product changed (`HasSameValues`).
    - `ProductionPlanSource` maps the products, ingredients and BOMs to `ProductionPlanRules`' inputs once per load rather than on every edit.
    - Covered by `ProductionPlanViewModelTests`: showing the tab again with nothing changed keeps every row object and writes nothing, and an edit replaces only the rows it changes.
  - **The plan follows the add row's line too** (the same day, reported by the user: "when I add a product to an empty list in the Plan card, the Mixing Sheet and Purchase Requisition is not updated immediately"). The add row's line isn't in `Lines` until the add row is committed, so the tables only took it in then. Now `NewLine` keeps the line it makes (`_newLine`), and the plan counts it until it's committed (then it's an ordinary line) or dropped. The drop comes from a new behaviour, `controls:GridAddNewRow.ItemDropped` (removed with its test on 2026-11-04, from a code review, as nothing had used it since the Production Plan moved to TinapayanBMS). The view raises `PropertyChanged("CurrentAddItem")`: it's set to the factory's item just after `AddNewRowInitiating`, and moves off it on commit (after the item is added to the source) or on cancel (never added). Read from the decompiled `GridAddNewRowController` and `CollectionViewAdv` (35.1.41). A cancelled row doesn't hear of it otherwise: `CancelNew` calls nothing on the item.
    - Checked in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved, the kept plan backed up and restored afterwards. In an empty plan, Hopia Ube x 5 at 100 filled both tables within 0.4 s of Enter (3 mixes, total 2,759.39). Hopia Monggo x 5 at 50, left with Tab, shared the Hopia Dough (6 batches for 750 pieces). A product picked with the mouse stayed picked.
    - Found while checking: retyping an existing line's product cleared the line's product as soon as the text matched none, and Esc didn't bring it back. Fixed below.
    - Covered by `The_add_row_line_is_planned_as_it_is_typed_and_counted_once_committed` and `ItemDropped_hears_of_an_add_row_thrown_away_but_not_of_one_committed`. `WaitUntilSettledAsync` treats a condition that meets a merge under way ("Collection was modified") as not yet held: with no dispatcher in a test, the merge runs on a pool thread.
  - **Retyping a line's product, and Tab to the next line** (the same day, at the user's request: "Fix retyping an existing line's product and additionally, i notice that i couldn't add a third product immediately").
    - **Retyping.** The drop-down cell's edit binding comes from the column's `UpdateTrigger`. The default left it to the control's `SelectedValue`, which pushes each change, so text matching no product (which clears the pick, `AllowNullInput`) emptied the line as it was typed and Esc had nothing to undo. The Plan's Product column has `UpdateTrigger="Explicit"`: Syncfusion's `CreateEditBindingBase` keeps it, and the grid pushes it only in the commit's validation (`renderer.UpdateSource`, when the value changed), never on Esc. That alone wasn't enough with Vim keys on, since Vim's Esc commits a cell (`VimKeys.Escape` → `GridEditCommit.Commit`), so the unmatched text was still written. `AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer.UpdateSource` now skips the push when the box holds text that names nothing, so the line keeps its product; emptied text still clears it. The Plan's column set the trigger in XAML at first; it moved into the renderer the same day (below).
    - **Tab.** On the add row's last cell Tab can reach (Units; Pieces has `AllowFocus="False"`), Syncfusion's `HandleKeyNavigation` ended the cell's edit and went no further, so the row stayed an add row with its values in. A memory dump (`dotnet-dump`) showed it: the view's `currentAddItem` still the typed line, the ViewModel's `_newLine` the same line, `Lines` holding two, and the grid's row count without a fresh add row. The tables showed the line only because the plan follows the add row. `UntouchedAddRowSelectionController.ProcessKeyDown` now commits a typed-into add row on that Tab (`GridEditCommit.Commit`), moves to the new add row's first cell and opens it. An untouched add row is left to the grid, so Tab still leaves the table. It's on every grid with `DropWhenUntouched`, the app's line tables.
    - Checked in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved, the kept plan backed up and restored. Hopia Monggo x 5 and then Hopia Baboy x 5 went in one after the other with Tab, each opening the next row. Retyping Hopia Ube x 5 as "zzz" left the tables as they were, and Esc twice (Vim on) kept the product. Retyping it as "Hopia M" and Tab changed it.
    - Covered by `GridAddNewRowTabTests` (5, in an offscreen grid set up as the Plan's): both checked to fail without their fix.
    - The driver script's clicks missed until it made its thread per-monitor DPI aware with `SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext`. The process call (`SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext`) fails once the host process (PowerShell) is already system-aware, as the earlier note on DPI-aware driving scripts warns. A driver must also refuse to send keys until it has found the app's window: once, launched too soon, the keys went to whatever window was in front (the app's Raw Materials search box and a blank New form that time, nothing saved).
  - **Every drop-down table writes on commit, and the Mixing Sheet's rows fit their text** (the same day, at the user's request: "check the two things you have noticed", the two left open above).
    - **All 12 typed-into drop-down columns** (9 tables: the Plan, the Price Simulator's changes, a product's components, packaging, section lines and add-ins and the section form's packaging, Sub-Assemblies' lines and packaging, the Bill of Materials, Alternative Units and Purchase Cost Setup) had the Plan's fault: the pick was written as it was typed. `AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer.OnInitializeEditElement` now makes the column's `UpdateTrigger` Explicit before the base builds the edit binding, so the rule lives with the commit rule (`UpdateSource`) and no column sets it. The only change seen: a row that prices itself as its ingredient or material is picked now does so as its cell's edit ends, not as the list's row is clicked. Nothing in the app listens to the picks themselves (`CurrentCellDropDownSelectionChanged`). Checked in the app on Sub-Assemblies: Puto Batter's First Class Flour retyped as "zzz" kept its cost meanwhile, and after Esc twice it read First Class Flour with Save and Undo off.
    - **Mixing Sheet rows** (`controls:GridAutoRowHeight.IsEnabled`, `Controls/GridAutoRowHeight.cs`, removed with its tests on 2026-11-04, from a code review, as nothing had used it since the Production Plan moved to TinapayanBMS): a mix shared by three products wrapped its For text onto a second line in a row one line tall. The behaviour answers `QueryRowHeight` with `GridColumnSizer.GetAutoRowHeight`, measuring only `TextWrapping="Wrap"` columns, from the Sample Browser's `DataGridRowHeightBehavior`. Two things the sample doesn't show: the sizer measures in its own font (12 px Segoe UI, a 5,1,5,1 margin) while the theme draws cells at 14, so rows came out a few pixels short and the second line was cut until the sizer was given a drawn cell's font, padding and border; and a row left unanswered keeps the height it had, so every line is answered, short ones at `RowHeight`, or a line shortened in place stayed tall. Heights are asked again (`InvalidateRowHeight` for every row, then `InvalidateMeasureInfo`) once per batch of changes to the lines or the grid's width.
    - **A blank line left behind**, found while checking: the Plan's `PlanLineRow` had no parameterless constructor, so `GridAddNewRow.IsUntouched` counted every add row as typed into, and one left empty was added as a blank line. Tab now opens the next add row straight away, so leaving it with Esc did that every time. It has one now, as the other line rows do.
    - Covered by `GridAutoRowHeightTests` (2) and `A_plan_add_row_left_untouched_is_dropped_not_added_as_a_blank_line` in `GridAddNewRowTabTests`, which fails without the constructor.
  - **Back and Forward between screens** (2026-09-23, at the user's request).
    - **What they do.** ← → buttons on the title bar's right, just before Minimize (`RightHeaderItemsSource`, since 2026-10-01; after the menu toggle, then at the tab strip's right end, before that), and Alt+Left / Alt+Right.
    - **What a step is.** A change of screen, however it happens: the menu, the tab strip, a tab closed, or a link from one screen to another (a Used By tile, a linked badge). Picking another record in a list isn't a step. The user chose this over "every record opened" and "screens only".
    - **Where it lives.** `NavigationHistory` (`ViewModels/NavigationHistory.cs`, on `MainWindowViewModel.Navigation`) holds the back and forward stacks of `ScreenVisit(AppScreen, RecordId)`, capped at 50. It knows nothing of tabs.
    - **Recording.** `MainWindow.MainTabControl_SelectionChanged` records each step, with the record the screen had open *as it was left*. Each tab maps to a `HistoryScreen`: the selected product, sub-assembly or raw material id; the screen's `Open*` method (`OpenProduct`, `OpenSubAssembly`, `OpenRawMaterial`); and its form's `History.IsDirty`. Reports has no record.
    - **Restoring.** Back reopens that record through the screen's `Open*` method before showing the tab, as the links do, **unless the screen's form holds unsaved changes**. Then the screen shows as it is, so Back never throws away typed work. `_isRestoringHistory` keeps the move from being recorded as a new step. Since 2026-10-29 the screen being left asks about its own unsaved changes before the step is taken (see "A form with unsaved changes is never left without asking").
    - **The keys.** They're on the window's `PreviewKeyDown`, so no grid or box takes them first. With Alt held, WPF reports the key as `Key.System`, so the handler checks `SystemKey`.
    - **The lists scroll to a record opened this way.** `ProductListCard`, `MaterialListCard` and (since 2026-10-07; `SubAssembliesView` watched its grid's `SelectedItem` before) `SubAssemblyListCard` watch their tree's, which also opens the folders above the record (`BringIntoView` with `canExpand`).
  - **Fixed along the way: Products listed a role's usages twice.** `LoadProductDetailAsync` set `EditingUsageRole`, whose subscription loads the usages, then also awaited its own load. Whenever the role actually changed (the first product picked each session, for example), both loads ran side by side and each added every row, which double-counted those costs in the summary. It now loads directly only when the role didn't change, and `LoadUsagesForRoleAsync` recomputes the summary itself. (The per-role loading itself went in the 2026-09-14 rebuild: every role's usages now load together, with the product.)
- **Embedded management panel for an editable child lookup** (`SubAssembliesView`, Phase 7), and the Sub-Assemblies screen around it: its design notes are in **[docs/plan/sub-assemblies-screen.md](docs/plan/sub-assemblies-screen.md)**.
- **A "replace-all" child collection editor** (`SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`, following `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` from Phase 6, and now also `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetUsagesForRoleAsync`/`PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` from Phase 8): the UI builds up an in-memory list of lines (here, `SectionLine` — an ingredient plus a quantity, not yet saved) via Add/Remove commands, and a single Save command sends the *whole* list to the repository, which deletes the existing rows for that parent and inserts the new set, then (where relevant) recomputes and stores the roll-up totals, and saves once. Simpler and less error-prone than diffing an edited collection against the database, and matches how a recipe is actually edited by a person (the whole line list at once, not one row at a time). **Delete the old rows with `ExecuteDeleteAsync`, not `ToListAsync` + `RemoveRange`** — every row is being unconditionally discarded, never mutated first, so loading them into memory first (found during an EF Core query review across all four of these repositories) buys nothing but an extra round trip and change-tracking overhead. `ExecuteDeleteAsync` executes immediately against the database, so call it before the `AddRange`/`SaveChangesAsync` that follows, same ordering as the old load-and-remove version.
  - **"Replace the whole set" had to become "replace one role's slice of the set" once tabs needed independent per-role linking** (Phase 10): `SetUsagesAsync(productId, usages)` replacing *every* usage on a product in one call was fine through Phase 9, but Phase 10's per-tab Live Template links mean one role's usages can be edited while another role is linked (and must be left alone) or vice versa. Renamed to `SetUsagesForRoleAsync(productId, subAssemblyRoleId, usages)`, scoped to just that role's rows (`Where(u => u.ProductId == productId && subAssemblyIdsInThisRole.Contains(u.SubAssemblyId))` before the delete, not a blind `Where(u => u.ProductId == productId)`). This also meant `ProductRules.EnsureValidUsages`'s "a product needs at least one usage" check had to be **dropped** — enforced per-role-scoped call, it would wrongly reject a perfectly valid product that just has zero usages in one particular role. The lesson generalizes: a "replace everything for this parent" method's scope has to match whatever the UI's unit of independent editing actually is, and that unit can get finer as later phases add more granular concepts (roles/tabs here) on top of an earlier, coarser one.
    - **And coarser again (2026-09-14):** with role-tab links gone, the unit of editing is the whole recipe. `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetRecipeAsync(dbContext, productId, RecipeDraft)` (called by `ProductRepository.SaveAsync` and `ProductCloner`, inside their transactions) replaces all of a product's usages, and adds, updates, relines or deletes its own sub-assemblies to match. A `RecipeDraft` is the product's own sub-assemblies (`OwnedSubAssemblyDraft`: id or 0, name, role, gram lines, piece size — an owned one always carries its owner's usage, so there's no separate id to match a new one by) and the ones it links (`LinkedUsageDraft`). Every check runs before anything is deleted: names, lines, piece sizes, roles, that listed ids are the product's own and linked ones another's, and that no sub-assembly being dropped is linked elsewhere. Then: the usages go (`ExecuteDeleteAsync`), then the dropped sub-assemblies (their lines cascade), then kept ones are updated before new ones are added (so a new one can take a name a kept one gave up), each relined through `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync(dbContext, subAssembly, lines)`, which recomputes its totals. The public single-purpose `SetLinesAsync`/`SetUsagesForRoleAsync` and the sub-assembly add/update/delete went with their screen.
- **A repository that only ever needs the single "latest" row from a growing history table should query for just that row, not load the whole table** (`IngredientPriceHistoryRepository`/`PackagingPriceHistoryRepository`'s `AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`/`DeleteAsync`, fixed during the same query review): `PriceHistoryRules.FindLatest` picks one row by `(Date desc, Id desc)`, but the original code loaded every price-history entry for the ingredient/material to run that pick in C# — a table that only grows over the life of the app, reloaded in full on every single price edit. Fixed by querying `OrderByDescending(Date).ThenByDescending(Id).FirstOrDefaultAsync()` for the current latest row (already well-served by the existing `HasIndex(IngredientId, Date)` composite index) and, where a new/updated in-memory candidate also needs to be weighed against it, running `FindLatest` over just that 1-2-item set — mathematically identical to running it over the full list, since the max of a set unioned with one more candidate equals the max of (the set's own max, that candidate). (Superseded 2026-09-18: price history no longer sets the cost, so none of these queries remain; the lesson stands.)
- **Derived values are computed on read, never stored, until a phase actually needs to persist them** (Phase 8): `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` stores only `WeightPerPiece` (since 2026-09-14, only the typed figure — weight per piece or yield — and the other is computed too); its `Yield` and cost-per-piece, and `PackagingLine`'s cost-per-piece, and `Product`'s total material cost, are all plain `ProductRules`/`CostingRules` functions called fresh every time a screen needs them (`ProductsViewModel.RecomputeSummary`), not columns kept in sync by a propagator. This is deliberately simpler than Phase 6/7's stored-and-propagated `CostPerGram`/`TotalCost`: nothing yet depends on *Product's* cost the way `SubAssembly` depends on `Ingredient.CostPerGram`, and wiring a real propagator here would be building Phase 11 early. Revisit when Phase 11 wires the full cascade — at that point a `RawMaterialPriceHistory` change needs to reach a product's displayed price without the user reopening the screen, which read-on-demand doesn't do on its own.
- **Cost-plus pricing, not gross-margin-on-price** (`CostingRules.CalculateSellingPrice`, Phase 9, an explicit user decision over the gross-margin alternative): `BaseCost = SubAssemblyCost + PackagingCost + Labor + Overhead + Administrative` (the administrative cost joined on 2026-09-14), where each of those three is its percentage of `SubAssemblyCost` — **the sub-assemblies alone, not the packaging, since 2026-09-20** (they were percentages of the whole material cost from 2026-09-15 to then). That's why `CalculateSellingPrice` takes the sub-assembly cost and the packaging cost as **two separate parameters** rather than one material cost: only the first is the base for the three percentages, and both go into the base cost. It takes the percentages and works the pesos out itself, so every caller prices the same way. `PreVatPrice = BaseCost × (1 + Margin%/100)`, `SellingPrice = PreVatPrice × (1 + VAT%/100)` — three separate steps, never folded into one formula, so a shop owner can audit each input by hand. An informational helper sits beside it: `PercentOf(part, whole)` (0 when the whole is 0), for every "% of the price" on the Products screen, which doesn't change the price. `CalculateProfitMarginPercent(baseCost, preVatPrice)`, the markup's share of the pre-VAT price (a 30% markup is a 23.08% margin), sat beside it until 2026-11-04, when the code review's cleanup removed it: nothing had called it since `PackCostSummary` went on 2026-09-20. If a future phase ever needs gross-margin pricing (margin as a % of the *final* price rather than a markup on cost), that's a different formula (`price = cost / (1 − margin%)`) and a different, explicitly-chosen mode — don't quietly swap the math in `CalculateSellingPrice`.
- **A full-precision in-memory reciprocal (e.g. `1m / 6m`) gets rounded to 4 places on its round trip through a `decimal(18,4)` column** (found by a failing Phase 8 test asserting the untruncated value): `PackagingLine.QtyPerPiece`/`PiecesPerPackagingUnit` are exact reciprocals in `ProductRules.ReconcileReciprocal`'s C# math, but SQL Server's `decimal(18,4)` (the app-wide convention from Phase 1) stores `0.1666666...` as `0.1667`. Expected and harmless for money-scale quantities, but any test asserting an exact reciprocal against data that's been through the database needs to assert the *rounded* value, not the raw division result.
- **A guarded field on an existing repository can be tightened once a later phase adds the FK that makes the guard meaningful, without that counting as "jumping ahead.**" `SubAssemblyRepository.UpdateAsync` left `RoleId` fully editable in Phase 7 because nothing referenced a sub-assembly yet; Phase 8 added `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` with a Restrict FK to `SubAssembly`, so `UpdateAsync` now blocks a role change (`InvalidOperationException`) whenever any `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` row references that sub-assembly, matching the Domain Model's "a sub-assembly's role can only be changed while no product uses it." Prefer this — tightening the one repository method whose precondition changed — over a broad revalidation pass across every earlier phase's code every time a new phase adds a relationship.
  - **The role lock itself went on 2026-09-14**: it existed only because a role change broke role-tab links, and those are gone. An owner can change its sub-assembly's role whenever it likes.
- **A "linked tab reads through to another product" feature resolves recursively at read time, with a visited-set guard, rather than eagerly propagating data on write** (`ProductTabLink`, Phase 10): `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetEffectiveForRoleAsync`/`PackagingLineRepository.GetEffectiveForProductAsync` follow a tab's `ProductTabLink` chain (if any) to whichever product's tab is actually unlinked, and return *that* tab's own rows — recursing through however many hops a chain of derived products has, not just one. `SetLinkAsync` separately rejects a cycle at write time (`ProductTabLinkRules.EnsureNoCycle`, loading only the other links for that exact tab identity into a dictionary first, mirroring `IngredientBomLineRepository`'s cycle-check shape from Phase 6), but the read path still carries its own `HashSet<int>` of visited product ids as defense in depth — cheap insurance against a cycle slipping in through a future write path that forgets the check, or a manual data edit. **Un-linking a tab materializes its current effective read into owned rows before removing the link row** (`ProductTabLinkRepository.RemoveLinkAsync`) — read the effective data while the link still resolves, delete the link and save, *then* write the materialized rows (which is only accepted once the tab no longer shows as linked) — so a product never momentarily shows less than it did the moment before.
  - **(SQL Server only, until 2026-10-21.) SQL Server needed `SET QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON` for any INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE against a table with a filtered index** (`ProductTabLinks`, which has two — see its `HasFilter` unique indexes below) — this is a session-level setting, and `sqlcmd`'s default session has it off, so a raw `sqlcmd -Q "INSERT INTO ProductTabLinks ..."` fails with `Msg 1934`. EF Core's own connections already set this correctly, so the app itself is never affected — only ad-hoc `sqlcmd` seeding/cleanup against this table needs `SET QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON;` prepended.
  - **A nullable column that should behave as "exactly one row per non-null group, plus at most one row where it's null" needs two filtered unique indexes, not one plain one** (`ProductTabLinkConfiguration`): SQL Server's default unique index treats every `NULL` as distinct from every other `NULL`, so a single `HasIndex(ProductId, SubAssemblyRoleId).IsUnique()` would let a product accumulate unlimited Packaging-tab links (all `SubAssemblyRoleId = NULL`, never colliding). Split into `HasIndex(ProductId, SubAssemblyRoleId).IsUnique().HasFilter("[SubAssemblyRoleId] IS NOT NULL")` for the many role-tab rows and `HasIndex(ProductId).IsUnique().HasFilter("[SubAssemblyRoleId] IS NULL")` for the at-most-one Packaging-tab row.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-14): only packaging links are left**, as `PackagingLink` (table `PackagingLinks`: `ProductId`, plainly unique, and `LinkedProductId`), with `IPackagingLinkRepository`/`PackagingLinkRepository` and `PackagingLinkRules` (renamed from `ProductTabLinkRules`). The read-through (`PackagingLineRepository.ResolveEffectiveAsync`), the visited-set guard, the write-time cycle check and the materialize-then-unlink order are the same; the role-tab resolution (`GetEffectiveForRoleAsync`/`GetEffectiveForProductAsync` on usages), `ProductTabKind` and the filtered indexes went.
  - **`ObservableAsPropertyHelper` (`ToProperty`) and manually calling `this.RaisePropertyChanged(nameof(...))` on that same property don't mix** — a real bug caught before it shipped in this phase: `IsSelectedRoleLinked`/`IsPackagingLinked` were first wired as `ToProperty` outputs of a `WhenAnyValue` pipeline, but their actual source of truth (whether `TabLinks`, a plain `ObservableCollection`, contains a matching link) only changes via imperative `Clear()`/`AddRange()` calls in `LoadProductDetailAsync`/`LoadUsagesForRoleAsync` — nothing `WhenAnyValue` can observe. Calling `RaisePropertyChanged` on an OAPH-backed property doesn't recompute it; the `Value` a bound control reads back is still whatever the last `WhenAnyValue` push produced (in this case, computed exactly once at construction and never again). Fixed by making both plain `RaiseAndSetIfChanged` properties recomputed by an explicit `RefreshLinkedFlags()` call at the same two sites. General rule: reach for `ToProperty` only when every input is itself a bindable/observable property; when the real driver is an imperatively-mutated collection, use a plain property and an explicit recompute call instead.
- **Every screen was verified by actually running the app**, not just building it or passing tests: launch `dotnet run --project src/TinapayanRMS.UI`, screenshot the running window, and where practical seed real rows via `sqlcmd` first so the screenshot proves the data-bound path (grid rows, selected-row-into-form binding, dropdown selection) and not just empty-state layout. Clean up any rows seeded this way afterward — the dev `TinapayanRMS` database should hold only what the user actually entered.
  - **Bringing the window to front needs `AttachThreadInput` around `SetForegroundWindow`, not just `SetWindowPos`-with-`HWND_TOPMOST`.** The plain topmost trick (recorded here through Phase 6) stopped reliably working in Phase 7: `SetWindowPos(hwnd, HWND_TOPMOST, ...)` would report success, but mouse clicks at the expected screen coordinates still landed on the foreground terminal window underneath, confirmed with `WindowFromPoint`. The fix that actually works: attach the calling thread's input queue to the current foreground window's thread (`AttachThreadInput(myThreadId, fgThreadId, true)`), then `ShowWindow`/`BringWindowToTop`/`SetForegroundWindow` on the target, all inside the *same* tool invocation as the click that follows (a separate tool call in between gives the terminal a chance to reclaim focus) — then detach. Verify with `WindowFromPoint` before clicking, not after. `PrintWindow` (not `CopyFromScreen`) remains the right way to capture the screenshot itself, since it captures a window's content directly regardless of what's on top of it on screen — it's specifically mouse input, not rendering, that needs true foreground focus.
  - **Even `AttachThreadInput` can't win against a real foreground app that keeps re-asserting focus** (seen in Phase 8: a game the user had open, not the terminal this time, stayed foreground through repeated retries, confirmed each time with `WindowFromPoint`). When that happens, stop retrying rather than keep fighting for focus — it means the user is actively doing something else on the machine. Fall back to whatever partial screenshot evidence was already captured (e.g. a new tab visible in the tab strip) plus the integration test suite and a manual line-by-line binding review (every `x:Path` in the view's XAML checked against the ViewModel's actual property names/types) as the verification of record for that screen.
  - **Synthetic click/type automation (`SetCursorPos` + `mouse_event` + `SendKeys`) against Syncfusion's custom input controls is unreliable** — coordinates that look right against a screenshot don't reliably focus/type into a `DoubleTextBox` (tried once, Phase 5, no app error, the value just never landed). Don't burn time chasing exact coordinates. The read/render/selection path (data flowing into the grid and the selected-row-into-form binding) is what screenshots verify well; for write paths (a button's command), rely on the repository/rules integration tests exercising the exact same call chain the command invokes, plus a code review of the binding — the same depth `New`/`Save`/`Delete` got in Phase 4.
    - **A working exception, found later (verifying the length-unit conversion-factor field): UI Automation's `ValuePattern.SetValue(...)` sets a `DoubleTextBox`'s displayed text but does not commit it to the bound property** — a save attempted right after `SetValue` failed FluentValidation as if the field were still empty, even though the screenshot showed the typed value. The fix: physically move the cursor into the field and click it first (real focus, not just an Automation reference to the control), send the digits through `keybd_event` as real keystrokes, then send a real Tab keystroke to force the focus-loss that commits the WPF binding — only then does `Value` update. (Since 2026-09-13 every editable `DoubleTextBox` binds with `UpdateSourceTrigger=PropertyChanged`, so real keystrokes commit as they're typed and the Tab is no longer needed; `SetValue` still isn't enough.) `ValuePattern.SetValue` alone is fine for a plain `SfTextBoxExt`/`Text` binding (used successfully throughout Phases 13-14's verification) but isn't sufficient for a `DoubleTextBox`'s `Value`; when a save silently fails validation right after an automation-driven numeric-field edit, suspect this before suspecting the validation rule itself.
    - **Picking a `ComboBoxAdv` item needs real clicks too** (verifying the one Save, 2026-09-13). Its items expose no SelectionItem pattern, and until the dropdown opens they carry no text (their name is the item's type name).
      - Open the dropdown with a real click near the picker's right edge.
      - Then find the `ComboBoxItemAdv` whose Text child matches, searching under the desktop root filtered by the app's process id (the dropdown is a separate window), and click it.
      - `ButtonAdv` has no Invoke pattern, so buttons need real clicks as well.
      - Look labels up inside the panel being filled, not the whole view: a grid column header ("Category", "Component") can share a form label's text.
      - Type into a `DoubleTextBox` without Tab when the next control is an editable picker. Tab opens the picker's dropdown, which swallows the next click.
      - When the item's position is known, the keyboard is enough (verifying the Products screen, 2026-09-14): a click on the picker didn't open its dropdown but gave it focus, and each Down press picked the next item.
  - **Make the script DPI-aware before placing, clicking or capturing** (2026-09-14). The primary monitor is at 125% and the secondary (1,366 × 768) at 100%. A DPI-unaware PowerShell gets scaled window rects and captures, and WPF resizes a window moved onto the other monitor: a 1,400 × 800 `SetWindowPos` landed at 1,120 × 640. Call `SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext(-4)` (per-monitor v2) first, and set the size again once the window is on the secondary monitor. 1,400 × 800 doesn't fit there, so check the default layout maximized (1,366 × 720).
  - **`SfDataGrid` sometimes renders only its first few columns when screenshotted via `PrintWindow` right after a tab switch or fresh data load** (seen in Phase 7's Sub-Assemblies grid, and on closer inspection, in the Phase 4 Ingredients grid too, so it predates Phase 7 and isn't a regression from it) — the grid's rendered width shrinks to fit only the columns it managed to realize rather than the full `*`-allocated Grid column width, with no visual trace (no header, no border) of the missing columns. This looks like a virtualization/layout-timing artifact of capturing a hardware-accelerated custom control via GDI shortly after it's populated, not a real data or binding problem — the same ViewModel data (e.g. a seeded sub-assembly's `Role`/`TotalWeight`/`TotalCost`) round-trips correctly through the integration tests. Don't chase this pixel-by-pixel; treat a screenshot showing at least the grid's leading column(s) with real seeded data, plus a passing integration-test suite covering every column's underlying property, as sufficient verification.
- **A value that can never be typed is shown in a `TextBlock`, not a read-only text box** (2026-09-19, found by a CPU trace of the Products screen). WPF connects every `TextBox` (and so every `SfTextBoxExt`, `DoubleTextBox`, …) to Windows' Text Services Framework after its first layout, whatever its `Focusable` or `IsReadOnly` (`TextEditor.InitTextStore`, confirmed by decompiling PresentationFramework 10.0.8), and there's no per-control opt-out. Each change of its text then calls `TextStore.RequestLock`, which waits on text services through COM: the Products form's Total Weight (`UsageFormTotalWeightText`, a read-only unfocusable `SfTextBoxExt`) cost about 115 ms of UI-thread waiting per product picked on the dev machine, 31% of the redraw, and the wait depends on the text services active (input methods, touch keyboard, dictation, assistive tools), which likely explains 1.76 s freezes seen on the user's own run. It's now a `Border` with `ValueBoxStyle` (`UsageFormPanel.xaml`: the theme's text box drawn by hand — 24 px, corners of 4, `#F1F1F1` with a `#8E8E8E` bottom pixel via the theme's flipped 2 px gradient, text `#1B1B1B` inset 6 px) holding a `TextBlock`. Since 2026-09-24 the two weights are plain right-aligned `TextBlock`s in the form's "Computed" rows, with no box drawn, and `ValueBoxStyle` is gone; still TextBlocks, for the same reason. This is for a value that is *never* typed; a box that is only sometimes read-only stays a text box, greyed as below. Linked Cost's always-read-only inherited Cost Buffer % (`LinkedCostCard`) is the one other such box, on Raw Materials, not changed.
- **Result of both 2026-09-19 fixes** (this and the recipe tabs' one shared form): the UI thread's busy time over the same ten product clicks went from 3,767 ms to 970 ms (about 377 → 97 ms a click), measured with `dotnet-trace` on the dev database.
- **Picking another product swaps its details in one pass, with no empty product between** (2026-09-19, at the user's request: "why does the UI flicker between clicks in the product list?"). `ResetDetails` used to clear the recipe, packaging and costing the moment a product was picked, and the screen drew that empty product (no recipe tabs, ₱0.0000, the overviews' empty states) until the new details arrived, with the recipe's frame collapsing and growing back. Now, for another product, it only sets `AreDetailsReady` to false and leaves the previous product's details showing; `LoadProductDetailAsync` clears them (`ClearDetailsForAnother`) and applies the new ones in the same dispatcher turn, so nothing is drawn in between, then sets it back. Picking none (New) still clears at once, and picking the same product again still keeps its details until the reload. If the reads fail, the previous product's details are cleared rather than left under the new name. While `AreDetailsReady` is false (a fraction of a second) the details aren't the form's any more: the details tabs ignore the mouse (`IsHitTestVisible`, no visual change), and Save returns without saving, since it would write the previous product's recipe under the picked one's id and name. Save has no `canExecute` for it on purpose: the button would grey for that moment on every pick, a flicker of its own. Measured by sampling the recipe tab strip's pixel every ~16 ms for 800 ms after each of six clicks: the previous commit lost it for 300–450 ms a click, this build never. Test: `Picking_another_product_keeps_the_previous_details_until_its_own_arrive_and_Save_waits_for_them`.
  - **Another product's sections take over the recipe's tabs in place** (2026-09-19, at the user's request after a UI performance review: a CPU trace of product picks put a fifth of the UI thread's time in the recipe's tab strip). `ClearDetailsForAnother` used to empty `UsageEdits`, which took every tab away, and `ReplaceUsages` then added a new `SubAssemblyRecipeTab` per section, so `TabControlExt` built a new `TabItemExt`, template and all, for every tab on every pick (`TabLayoutPanel.MeasureOverride` → `ApplyTemplate`), and dropped and re-added Packaging's as well. Now the previous recipe's tabs stay (`ClearDetailsForAnother(keepRecipeTabs: true)`), and `ReplaceUsages` replaces the sections index for index (`MirrorUsageTabs`' Replace sets the tab's `Usage`, and its header and glyph follow), adding or removing only the difference; Packaging's tab stays when both products have packaging. The form is closed first, so no tab carries the previous product's form over: the recipe still opens on its first tab, on the picked product's section. A read that fails still clears everything (`keepRecipeTabs: false`). Measured off-screen against the dev database, six interleaved runs of 24 Recipe-tab clicks each: UI-thread time per click 86.6 → 57.2 ms (median of the runs' medians), tab-strip changes per click 3.3 → 1.3. Test: `Picking_another_product_keeps_the_recipe_tabs_and_opens_the_form_on_its_first_section` (fails on the old code). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved: Almond Cheese Bread (linked Dough, Topping, Filling, Packaging), then Cheese Roll (its own Dough and Packaging: the first tab's glyph turned from link to list, and two tabs went), Pan Bread, All Purpose (Dough alone: Packaging went too), Almond Cheese Bread again (all four back), and Cheese Roll picked while Almond Cheese Bread's Filling showed (it opened on its Dough, with its own form).
- **Picking another raw material swaps its details in one pass too, and a quick second pick no longer mixes two materials' details** (2026-09-19, at the user's request, found by an off-screen probe during the same review). A material's price history, brand prices and BOM were three loads, each clearing its grid at once and adding what it read when the read came back, with no cancellation (`Observable.FromAsync(() => …)`), so a second pick before the first one's reads returned got both. On the dev database Butter Icing then Custard Filling showed 6 BOM lines instead of 2, and All Purpose Flour then Buttermilk Powder 5 brand prices instead of 2, even 2 ms apart. Save sends every brand row, so saving then would have written one material's base-unit prices onto the other; a Save in that moment sent none and deleted the material's own. Now it has the Products shape: one `LoadDetailsAsync` reads all three on the thread pool (`ReadDetailAsync`, in one `Task.Run`), a newer pick cancels it (`FromAsync(cancellationToken => …)` with `Switch`), and `ShowDetails` replaces the grids in one pass without clearing them first, so they don't draw empty in between. `AreDetailsReady` is false from picking another material until its details arrive: the details tabs ignore the mouse (`IsHitTestVisible`) and Save returns without saving. A new material's first Save isn't another material: `ReloadSavedRawMaterialAsync` sets `_detailMaterialId` before selecting it, as Products does. Delete Price still reloads the price history alone (`ReplacePriceHistory`). No cost to speak of: a click took 26.3 → 27.3 ms of UI-thread time (three interleaved runs); the mouse guard is about 4 ms of it, the same with a transparent overlay in its place, which was tried and dropped. Tests: `A_second_pick_before_the_first_ones_details_arrive_shows_only_its_own` (fails on the old code: Sugar showed Flour's brands too) and `Picking_another_material_keeps_the_previous_details_until_its_own_arrive_and_Save_waits_for_them`; the tests that pick a material and then edit or save it now wait for its details first (`SelectAsync`). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved: six Down presses in a row from All Purpose Flour left Buttermilk Powder's two brands alone, the Price History tab answered a click once they'd arrived, and Butter Icing then Custard Filling at once left Custard Filling's two lines.
- **A read-only number box greys its text** (2026-09-17, at the user's request): `controls:ReadOnlyText.IsGreyed`, bound to the same flag as the box's `IsReadOnly` (or `"True"` for one that always is), paints the text in `ReadOnlyForegroundBrush` (#A8A29E), so a value that can't be typed doesn't look typeable. Used by Linked Cost's inherited Cost Buffer %, and Purchase Cost Setup's Quantity per Purchase Unit (read-only for the base unit and a saved alternative unit, whose multiplier is edited in Alternative Units) and Cost Buffer ₱ (read-only until a price above 0 is entered). The user had it reverted once, then asked for it back once it was clear the grey says "edited elsewhere", not "broken".
  - A `DoubleTextBox` never draws its text with `Foreground`: a non-zero value uses `PositiveForeground`, and 0 uses `ZeroColor` only once `ApplyZeroColor` is on (the theme's template reads them; `EditorBase` decompiled, 34.2.7). So those three are set, and turning it off **clears** them rather than setting a colour back, which returns the theme's own text colour exactly — a converter binding would have had to know that colour. Verified in the app: Cost Buffer ₱ grey at 0.00 on a new material and black at ₱246.75 on a priced one, and a saved tray's 1,500.00 greyed.
- **One Raw Materials screen (`RawMaterialsView`/`RawMaterialsViewModel`)**, from the user's Ingredients mockup (2026-09-13): the list beside the selected material's cards. Its design notes are in **[docs/plan/raw-materials-screen.md](docs/plan/raw-materials-screen.md)**.
- **One Save writes the whole material** (2026-09-13, at the user's request: adding one took a Save for the details, then Save Unit per alternative unit, Save Price and Save BOM).
  - **The screen edits everything in memory.**
    - Alternative units are rows over the form's own copies of the units (Id 0 until saved). Save checks them first (`EnsureValidUnitRows`, what Apply used to): every row has a unit ("Pick a unit for every alternative unit, or remove the empty row.") and a positive multiplier (naming Quantity per Purchase Unit for a unit added there, else Alternative Units), then `RawMaterialUnitValidator`, not the base unit, not already listed. Remove only drops the row.
    - The purchase unit choices (`PurchaseUnitOptions`, `PurchaseUnitChoice` records keyed by unit of measure id, `0` for the base unit) are the base unit and every unit of measure, and `BaseUnitName`/`BaseUnitAbbreviation` follow the form's type and base unit, so a new packaging material shows its own unit before it's saved. The list is rebuilt only when it actually changes, so an open unit cell keeps its items.
    - **An alternative unit can also be added from the Purchase Cost Setup** (2026-09-13, at the user's request: a unit had to be added in Alternative Units before it could be priced in; carried into the grid 2026-09-18).
      - The unit cell lists every unit of measure, the ones the form doesn't have yet among them, by name. A packaging material's base unit and a unit named like an ingredient's gram are left out.
      - Picking one the form doesn't have adds it as a row straight away. The row's Qty per Purchase Unit is then its multiplier: typeable only for a unit not saved yet (`BrandPriceRow.CanTypeQuantity`, through `controls:GridCellEditing.AllowWhen`), read-only for the base unit and saved units, whose multiplier is corrected in the Alternative Units grid, since that reprices. A packaging material's unit picked this way and then made its base unit (2026-10-25, a bug the user found: Save refused "That's already the material's base unit.") moves its rows onto the base unit and takes the added row away again (`MoveBrandRowsOntoTheBaseUnit`); one typed into the Alternative Units grid is still refused.
